Everything is bounded: a hull is built over at most `convex_hull_max_points` (2^13) and a longer input refuses `HullTooLong`, an empty one `EmptySource`, each having allocated nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the hull construction and the reads over it; `contour` owns the ordered ring and its `Point` type, `polygon` the vertex simplification -- a **reading**, not a render. From it descend robust convexity, solidity, and a convex-only point-in-shape test.
- **Minimum-area oriented bounding box (rotating calipers)** -- `min_area_rect` (`min_area_rect`, `rect_area`, `elongation`, `aabb_area`, `rect_area_below`, `MinAreaRect`, `min_rect_max_coord`), the **oriented companion** of `regions`'s axis-aligned bounding box and the exact-arithmetic reader `convex_hull` earns: where an axis box is fixed upright, this rung reads the tightest rectangle of **any** orientation, so a thin bar tilted 45 degrees -- which fills only half of its axis box -- is measured along its own axis. The construction is **rotating calipers** on the **Freeman-Shapira theorem** (H. Freeman & R. Shapira 1975): the minimum-area rectangle enclosing a convex polygon is **flush with one of its edges**, so for each hull edge the shape's extent **along** the edge (the projection range) and **perpendicular** to it (the cross-product range) give that rectangle's area, and the smallest wins. The winning edge is chosen by an **exact i128 cross-multiplication** of two rational areas (area = projection-range x cross-range over edge-length-squared), so no float ever picks the wrong support; only the reported **orientation**, **length/breadth**, and **elongation** are read in f64, since they divide by a square root, exactly as `moments` reads orientation. The decisive **tilt** crux: a square turned 45 degrees fills only **half** its axis box -- its oriented area is exactly **8** where the axis-aligned box is **16**, strictly below by exact arithmetic, the fact `regions`'s upright box cannot make -- while an upright rectangle's oriented box **is** its axis box (equal, never below). Three descendants ride on the oriented box, each a truth the axis box confuses on a tilt: **orientation** (the supporting edge's angle, the real axis a tilted shape lies along), **extent** (the box's honest length and breadth), and **elongation** (the long-to-short ratio, rotation-invariant -- a square reads 1 at any tilt, a 2:1 bar reads 2 upright or turned). Everything is bounded: the box is read over a hull under `convex_hull_max_points`, coordinates under `min_rect_max_coord` (2^14) keep every accumulator far from wrapping, a hull of fewer than three vertices refuses `DegenerateHull` and an empty input `EmptySource`, each having allocated nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the caliper sweep and the reads over the oriented box; `convex_hull` owns the hull and the `Point` type, `regions` the axis box it improves on -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Convexity defects (per-dent depth below the hull)** -- `convexity_defects` (`convexity_defects`, `deepest_defect`, `defect_count`, `Defect`, `defects_max_points`), the **point-by-point companion** of `convex_hull`'s single solidity scalar: where solidity reads a shape's total emptiness below its convex wrapper as **one** fraction, this rung reads **where** the boundary falls away and **how far**, one dent at a time -- the classic convexity defects of the fingers-of-a-hand reader, each gap between two fingertips a defect and its depth the distance the boundary sinks below the chord across them. Every hull vertex is one of the ring's own pixels, so consecutive hull vertices in ring order bound a **run** of boundary pixels, and for each hull edge the defect is the run pixel of greatest perpendicular distance below the chord -- the **argmax** of the signed cross-product magnitude `|cross(A, B, P)|`, exact in i64, so a straight edge (every cross zero) carries **no** dent. The **deepest** defect is chosen **across** edges by an **exact i128 cross-multiplication** (`cross_i^2 * len_j` vs `cross_j^2 * len_i`, the square roots cleared by squaring), the same tolerance-free discipline `min_area_rect` selects its supporting edge by; only the reported **depth** is read in f64, since it divides by a square root, exactly as `moments` reads orientation. The decisive **count** crux: a convex 5x5 block reads **zero** defects (its boundary hugs its hull), an L reads **exactly one** (its single reentrant corner), and a plus reads **four** (one measured dent per gap between arms) -- the point-by-point concavity a single solidity number blurs. The decisive **depth** crux, pure integer point sets: a shallow dent (depth 3) on a **long** chord (length 10, raw cross 30) and a deeper dent (depth 4) on a **short** chord (length 5, raw cross 20) -- the naive raw-cross argmax picks the shallow dent (30 > 20), yet the exact depth comparison (30^2 x 25 = 22500 < 20^2 x 100 = 40000) names the deeper one, disagreeing with the float-free naive read. Everything is bounded: the ring is walked under `convex_hull_max_points`, an empty ring refuses `EmptySource`, an over-long ring `RingTooLong`, and a hull vertex not on the ring `HullVertexNotOnRing` (the hull must be this ring's own hull). Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the run walk, the per-edge argmax, and the exact cross-edge depth comparison; `convex_hull` owns the hull and its exact cross, `contour` the ordered ring and its `Point` type -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Convex hull diameter (maximum Feret span, rotating calipers)** -- `convex_diameter` (`convex_diameter`, `roundness`, `Diameter`, `convex_diameter_max_points`), the **isotropic-size companion** of `min_area_rect`'s oriented extent: where the oriented box's **length** is measured along the minimum-area orientation, this rung reads a shape's **greatest span** in any direction -- the maximum distance between any two boundary points, the classic maximum **Feret diameter**. That span is a genuinely different fact from the box length: a rectangle W by H has a min-area box of length W, yet its two farthest corners are the **diagonal** apart, `sqrt(W^2 + H^2)`, strictly longer than any side. For a convex shape the farthest pair is always two **hull** vertices (a point interior to the hull is closer to everything than the hull vertex beyond it), so the reading is over the hull `convex_hull` already built. The construction is **rotating calipers** (Shamos 1978): the diameter is realized by an **antipodal** pair (two vertices admitting parallel supporting lines), and as an edge pointer walks the hull once the antipodal pointer opposite it advances monotonically, so the greatest span is found in **one linear pass** rather than the O(n^2) of every pair -- for each edge, advance the antipodal vertex while its perpendicular distance grows (a pure integer **argmax** of the exact cross magnitude), then measure **both** edge endpoints against it (the second catches the parallel-edge span). A descendant rides on the diameter, the round companion of `convex_hull`'s solidity: **roundness** (`4 * region_area / (pi * diameter^2)`, in (0, 1]) reads how nearly a shape fills the circle of its own greatest span -- a compact block reads near 1, a thin bar spanning a large diameter yet filling little of its circle reads far below. Everything is bounded: the hull is swept under `convex_diameter_max_points`, an empty hull refuses `EmptySource`, an over-long one `HullTooLong`, and a 4n antipodal-advance guard refuses `NotConvex` so a non-convex input can never spin the caliper forever. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the caliper sweep and the reads over the diameter; `convex_hull` owns the hull, `contour` the `Point` type -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Minimum enclosing circle (smallest covering disk, Welzl)** -- `min_enclosing_circle` (`min_enclosing_circle`, `disk_contains`, `containment_radius`, `fits_through_round_hole`, `Circle`, `min_circle_max_points`), the **round-bound companion** of `convex_diameter`'s greatest span: where the diameter reads a shape's farthest pair, this rung reads the **smallest disk that covers the whole shape**. Those are a genuinely different fact. A natural mistake is to read the diameter as the shape's circle -- the disk whose diameter is the farthest pair -- yet that disk does **not** always cover the shape: an **acute** (near-equilateral) triangle's farthest pair is its longest edge, and the disk on that edge as diameter leaves the third vertex **outside** it, so the smallest covering disk is the triangle's **circumcircle**, pinned by all three vertices, whose own diameter is strictly larger than any edge. The construction is **Welzl's** incremental move-to-front (E. Welzl 1991): grow a working disk over the hull vertices, and when a vertex falls outside, pin it on the boundary of the new disk and re-admit the earlier ones. A minimum enclosing circle is pinned by at most **three** points, so the whole state is a **support set** of at most three vertices, and "is this vertex inside the current disk?" is an **exact integer predicate** -- a two-point diameter disk by the sign of the dot product `(a - p) . (b - p) <= 0` (i64), a three-point circumcircle by the classic **incircle determinant** (the Delaunay predicate, i128) -- so every **decision** is tolerance-free; only the reported **center** and **radius** are read in f64, since the circumcenter is rational and the radius takes a square root, exactly as `convex_diameter` reports its length. The decisive crux, proven on metal against an **exact-rational brute-force oracle** (every enclosing 2- and 3-subset disk, minimized by i128 cross-multiplication) the incremental sweep must agree with: on an acute triangle (a 3-point support, MEC diameter strictly above the farthest pair), a **right** triangle (a 2-point support, the MEC **is** the hypotenuse-diameter disk so its diameter **equals** the farthest pair -- the case the diameter reads correctly, distinguishing exactly when the round bound is the extra fact), a 5x5 raster block (centered at its center with the half-diagonal radius), and an irregular hexagon, Welzl reads exactly the circle the exhaustive reference reads. Two reads ride on the circle: **containment radius** (the smallest disk the whole shape fits inside) and a **fits-through-a-round-hole** test (a flat shape passed face-first through a circular hole clears it exactly when the enclosing radius is at most the hole's -- the honest face-first read, distinct from an edge-first minimum-width passage). Everything is bounded: the hull is swept under `min_circle_max_points`, an empty hull refuses `EmptySource`, an over-long one `HullTooLong`, and a collinear three-point support (never a real hull's) refuses `NotConvex`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** Welzl's sweep, the exact containment predicates, and the reads over the circle; `convex_hull` owns the hull, `contour` the `Point` type -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Minimum-width slot (narrowest edge-first gap, rotating calipers)** -- `min_width` (`min_width`, `width_sq`, `passes_slot`, `width_below`, `MinWidth`, `min_width_max_coord`), the **edge-first companion** of `min_enclosing_circle`'s face-first round hole: where the enclosing circle reads the round bound a shape clears **face-first**, this rung reads the narrowest parallel-sided **slot** the shape slips through **edge-first**, turned sideways. Those are a genuinely different fact -- a long thin bar clears a wide round hole only face-on, yet passes a slot barely wider than its thickness. The minimum width of a convex polygon is realized with a caliper line **flush with a hull edge**, so the reading is **rotating calipers** over the hull `convex_hull` already built: for each hull edge, measure the shape's **perpendicular** extent (the range of the cross product of the edge vector against every vertex, over the edge length), and keep the smallest. The width along an edge is `cross_range / sqrt(edge_len_sq)`, so its **square** is the exact rational `cross_range^2 / edge_len_sq`, and two edges' widths compare by an **exact i128 cross-multiplication** (the square roots cleared by squaring), the same tolerance-free discipline `convexity_defects` selects its deepest dent by and `min_area_rect` its smallest box; only the reported **width** and **angle** are read in f64, since the width divides by a square root, exactly as `min_area_rect` reports its dimensions. The decisive **diagonal-slot** crux, exact integer point sets: a right triangle (0,0)(6,0)(0,2) has an axis base of thickness 2, yet its narrowest slot is flush with the **hypotenuse**, an exact squared width **18/5** (~ 1.897) strictly below the axis thickness 2 -- the turned slot an axis-aligned reading (`regions`'s upright box) misses. The decisive **edge-first vs face-first** crux, proven against the real `min_enclosing_circle` sibling: a 2x8 bar passes a gap-**2** slot edge-first (its minimum width is exactly 2, a gap-1 slot refused by exact arithmetic), while its minimum enclosing circle has diameter `sqrt(68)` ~ 8.25, so face-first it needs a round hole more than **four times** wider -- the same bar's thinness and reach. A `passes_slot(w, gap)` read decides edge-first passage by the same exact rule (`cross_range^2 <= gap^2 * edge_len_sq`), the slot companion of `min_enclosing_circle`'s `fits_through_round_hole`; the width is always **at most** the min-area box's short side, strictly below it on the triangle. Everything is bounded: the width is read over a hull under `convex_hull_max_points`, coordinates under `min_width_max_coord` (2^14) keep every accumulator far from wrapping, a hull of fewer than three vertices refuses `DegenerateHull` and an empty input `EmptySource`, each having allocated nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the caliper sweep for the minimum width and the reads over it; `convex_hull` owns the hull and the `Point` type, `min_enclosing_circle` the round bound it mirrors edge-first -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Largest inscribed disk (biggest round peg, chamfer argmax)** -- `inscribed_disk` (`largest_inscribed_disk`, `inradius`, `disk_contains`, `holds_round_peg`, `Disk`), the **inner-bound companion** of `min_enclosing_circle`'s outer round hole: where the enclosing circle reads the smallest disk that **covers** a shape and `min_width` the narrowest slot it **passes through**, this rung reads the biggest round disk that fits **inside** it -- the shape's own reach measured from the middle out. The chamfer distance field (`distance`) already gives every foreground pixel its integer distance to the nearest background pixel, so the point deepest inside the foreground is exactly the pixel of **greatest** distance -- the center of the largest inscribed disk, and its distance the inscribed **radius** in chamfer units. So this rung owns one move over the field: the exact integer **argmax** of the finite distances, earliest in raster order on a tie, deterministic. Every **decision** -- which pixel is deepest, and whether a pixel lies inside the disk -- is integer arithmetic; only the reported `radius` (the chamfer depth over the orthogonal weight 3) is read in f64, exactly as `min_enclosing_circle` reports its own. The decisive **inner-sees-the-hole** crux: a solid 5x5 block and a hollow 5x5 ring (a wall one pixel thick) trace the **identical** outer contour, so `convex_hull` and `min_enclosing_circle` read the **same** hull and the **same** enclosing circle for both -- yet the inscribed disk shrinks from chamfer depth 9 (radius 3, the block's center) to depth 3 (radius 1, the ring's wall), the inner bound **seeing** the interior hole every convex reader is blind to. The **thickness-not-reach** crux: a 2x8 bar holds an inscribed disk of radius only 1 (half its thickness) never its length 8, while its minimum enclosing circle has radius ~4.12 (diameter `sqrt(68)`) -- the inner bound reading a shape's **thinness** from the inside where the enclosing circle reads its **reach**. A `disk_contains(disk, px, py)` read re-decides membership exactly (`dist_orthogonal^2 * squared-distance <= chamfer^2`, the square root cleared by squaring), and `holds_round_peg(disk, r)` decides whether the shape admits a round peg of integer radius `r` by `dist_orthogonal * r <= chamfer` -- the honest inner dual of `min_enclosing_circle`'s `fits_through_round_hole`. Everything is bounded: the field is measured under `distance`'s own pixel ceiling, a chamfer depth stays under 32768 so every product stays far under the i64 ceiling, an all-background mask refuses `NoForeground` and a zero-size source `EmptySource`, each having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the argmax over the distance field and the two exact reads over the disk; `distance` owns the chamfer field and its weights, `qoi` the RGBA buffer -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Width profile (per-orientation anisotropy signature)** -- `width_profile` (`width_profile`, `min_sample`, `max_sample`, `anisotropy`, `is_isotropic`, `at_least_as_elongated`, `WidthSample`, `WidthProfile`, `width_profile_max_coord`), the **whole-function companion** of `min_width`'s single floor: where `min_width` reads the **one** narrowest slot a shape passes edge-first, this rung reads the shape's perpendicular extent flush with **every** hull edge, so the thickness as the shape **turns** becomes visible. The same **rotating-calipers** construction, kept whole -- one width sample per hull edge in hull order -- and the **minimum and maximum** edge-normal widths are selected by the **same exact i128 cross-multiplication** and **no tolerance** `min_width` selects its floor by; only the reported `width`, `angle`, and `anisotropy` ratio are read in f64, since they divide by a square root. The decisive crux: a 2x2 square and a 2x8 bar share a minimum width of **exactly 2** (proven against the real `min_width`), yet the square's every edge-normal width is 2 (**isotropic**, anisotropy 1) while the bar's run 2 and 8 (anisotropy **exactly 4**) -- one number cannot separate them, the whole profile does. The minimum sample **is** the breadth byte-for-byte (`min_width`'s own result); the maximum sample is **edge-flush, not the diameter** -- for the bar the widest edge-normal width is exactly 8, strictly below the true diameter `sqrt(68)` ~ 8.25 (`convex_diameter`), whose antipodal span need not be edge-aligned. `is_isotropic` decides whether a shape is as wide as it is thin by exact equality, and `at_least_as_elongated(profile, ratio)` decides whether the widest width is at least `ratio` times the breadth by the exact rule `max_num * min_den >= ratio^2 * min_num * max_den` -- the honest anisotropy dual of `min_width`'s `passes_slot`. Everything is bounded: one sample per hull edge under `convex_hull_max_points`, coordinates under `width_profile_max_coord` (2^14), a degenerate hull refusing `DegenerateHull` and an empty input `EmptySource`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the per-edge caliper sweep and the reads over the profile; `convex_hull` owns the hull and the `Point` type, `min_width` the single breadth this floor agrees with, `convex_diameter` the antipodal span this ceiling is honestly distinct from -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Shape signature (isotropy x solidity taxonomy)** -- `shape_signature` (`shape_signature`, `is_solid`, `is_isotropic`, `class_code`, `same_class`, `ShapeSignature`, `shape_signature_max_coord`), the **fusion** of two orthogonal descriptors: `width_profile`'s **isotropy** verdict (is a shape as wide as it is thin?) paired against `convex_hull`'s **solidity** verdict (does it fill its hull?), joined into one 2x2 taxonomy. Each descriptor is one axis of shape, and each axis alone **merges** pairs the other keeps apart -- so the four archetypes fall into four distinct cells: a **solid isotropic** compact blob, a **solid anisotropic** bar, a **dented isotropic** plus or star, a **dented anisotropic** L or bent limb. Every **verdict** is an exact integer decision -- `is_solid` by pixel-count equality (`region_area == filled_hull_area`, solidity exactly 1) and `is_isotropic` by rational equality (`max_num * min_den == min_num * max_den`, anisotropy exactly 1), never a float compare; `class_code` folds the two into one integer 0..3 (0 a bent limb, 1 a bar, 2 a star, 3 a blob), and `same_class` decides whether two regions share both verdicts. The decisive crux: a solid block, a solid bar, a dented plus, and a dented L fall into **four distinct classes**, yet **each single axis merges one pair the join separates** -- solidity alone cannot tell the block from the bar (both solid) nor the plus from the L (both dented), and anisotropy alone cannot tell the block from the plus (both isotropic) nor the bar from the L (both anisotropic), so `same_class` reads all six pairs distinct where either lone descriptor collapses two of them -- the join is strictly sharper than either axis. Everything is bounded: one boundary ring under the contour's `BoundaryTooLong`, one hull under `convex_hull_max_points`, two pixel counts under the image pixel ceiling; a lone dot refuses `DegenerateHull`, an absent label `BadLabel`, each having allocated nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the joining of the two verdicts and the reads over the join; `width_profile` owns the anisotropy, `convex_hull` the solidity, `contour` the boundary ring, `label` the component map -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Euler number (topological hole count, bit-quad)** -- `euler_number` (`euler_number`, `hole_count`, `is_simply_connected`, `has_hole`, `Topology`, `euler_max_windows`), the **topological companion** of every geometry reader before it: where the hull, the widths, and the inscribed and enclosing disks all measure a shape's **geometry**, this rung reads its **topology** -- how many holes it encloses, an exact integer. Geometry is blind to a hole: a solid 5x5 block and a hollow 5x5 ring (a wall one pixel thick) trace the **identical** outer contour, so `convex_hull` reads the same hull, `min_enclosing_circle` the same circle, `width_profile` the same widths for both; `inscribed_disk` is the first to notice, its disk shrinking over the hole, yet even it reads **depth**, a metric hint, never the hole itself. The Euler number reads it directly. The count comes from **bit-quads** (S.B. Gray 1971): slide a 2x2 window over the region with a one-pixel background border and tally three window kinds -- **Q1** (exactly one foreground pixel, an outer corner), **Q3** (exactly three, an inner corner), and **QD** (a diagonal two, a corner-touch pinch) -- so for the 8-connected foreground (`label`'s own connectivity) the Euler number is the exact integer **`E = (Q1 - Q3 - 2*QD) / 4`**, a theorem whose numerator is always a multiple of four, **no float and no tolerance**. Since `label` hands each call a single 8-connected region, `components = 1` and `holes = 1 - E`. Two decisive facts. **Topology sees the hole the outer bounds cannot**: the block reads Euler **1** (one part, no hole), the ring Euler **0** (one part, one hole), though their outer geometry is byte-for-byte identical. **Topology is orthogonal to solidity**: `shape_signature` reads a plus and a ring **both** as dented (each leaves empty wedges inside its hull, solidity below one), so solidity **merges** them -- yet the plus has no hole (Euler 1) and the ring has one (Euler 0), the Euler number the axis that **separates** a pair solidity collapses, a genuinely new dimension of shape; and a block pierced twice reads Euler **-1** (two holes), the count running negative and staying exact. `hole_count`, `is_simply_connected`, and `has_hole` read the topology as a small integer -- the "is this a ring, not a disk?" question a surface asks the day it must tell a doughnut from a coin. Everything is bounded: the sweep visits `(width+1) * (height+1)` windows under `euler_max_windows`, every tally a u32 far below the i32 ceiling; a label of zero or past the component count refuses `BadLabel`, having tallied nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the bit-quad sweep and the reads over the count; `label` owns the component map and its 8-connectivity, `qoi` the pixel ceiling -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Euler number of a whole scene (multi-component topology)** -- `euler_scene` (`scene_euler`, `scene_topology`, `total_holes`, `holed_component_count`, `most_holed`, `is_all_simply_connected`, `SceneTopology`), the **multi-object companion** of `euler_number`: where `euler_number` reads one labeled region (its `components` always one, its Euler at most one), this rung drops the single-region assumption and reads the topology of a whole labeling of **many** objects -- how many components, how many holes total, and how many holes **each** object carries. The Euler number is **additive over the disjoint components**, and exactly so here: `label` hands back 8-connected components, so two pixels in one 2x2 window from **different** objects would have to be 8-adjacent -- yet 8-adjacent foreground is by definition **one** component, so no bit-quad window ever straddles two objects and the whole-foreground tally is byte-for-byte the sum of the per-object tallies. The rung reads the scene two ways and proves they agree: `scene_euler` runs **one** bit-quad sweep over the whole foreground (any non-zero label) for the total in a single pass, and `scene_topology` calls `euler_number` per label so each object carries its **own** hole count, the whole-scene Euler and total holes falling out as the sums of the parts. Two decisive facts. **The whole equals the sum of the parts**: a scene of a solid block (Euler 1, no hole), a ring (Euler 0, one hole), and a twice-pierced block (Euler -1, two holes) reads a total Euler of **0 = 1 + 0 + (-1)**, three components, three holes -- and the single sweep lands on the **same** total the per-object sum does, topological additivity proven both ways. **The per-object read distinguishes what the total blurs**: the total hole count (three) cannot say **which** object is holed, yet the per-component read does (block **0**, ring **1**, pierced block **2**), `holed_component_count` reports two of three objects holed, and `most_holed` names the pierced block. Everything is bounded: the sweep obeys `euler_max_windows`, the per-component loop runs `count` times under `label_max_pixels`, the hole slice is sized to `count`; an empty scene reads a valid zero topology and `most_holed` refuses `NoComponents`, having no object to pick. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the whole-foreground sweep, the per-component aggregation, and the reads; `label` owns the component map, `euler_number` owns the single-region bit-quad theorem it sums.
- **Scene manifest (per-object topology joined with geometry)** -- `scene_manifest` (`build_manifest`, `object_count`, `object_of`, `holed_object_count`, `largest_holed`, `total_area`, `ObjectEntry`, `Manifest`), the **join** of `euler_scene`'s topology with `regions`' geometry: where `euler_scene` reads how many objects are holed and `regions` reads how big each object is, this rung carries, in dense label order, one **row per object** holding its area and tight bounding box beside its Euler number and hole count -- so a single manifest describes every object as **both** a shape and a topology. The join is strictly sharper than either half: `largest_holed` names the **largest object that encloses a hole**, a fact about area **and** holes together that neither parent can answer -- `region_stats`' `largest_region` is pure geometry (it picks the biggest object, holed or not) and `euler_scene`'s `most_holed` is pure topology (it picks the object with the most holes, big or small). The decisive crux: a scene of a large solid block (area 25, biggest overall, no hole), a small 3x3 ring (area 8, one hole), a large 5x5 ring (area 16, one hole), and a small solid block (area 4, no hole) reads `largest_holed` = the **large ring** (label 3) -- the pure-geometry largest picks the unholed block (label 1) and the pure-topology most-holed ties the two rings and breaks to the smaller (label 2), so only the join lands on label 3. `holed_object_count` reports two of four objects holed, `total_area` sums the per-object areas to the whole foreground, `object_of` looks a row up by label; an all-simply-connected scene refuses `NoHoledObject` even though objects exist (the refusal is about holes, not emptiness), an empty scene reads a valid empty manifest, and a label past the count refuses `BadObjectLabel`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the per-object join and the reads over it; `label` owns the component map, `regions` owns the area and bounding box, `euler_number` owns the single-region topology -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Roundness (inner/outer disk-bound reconciliation)** -- `roundness` (`disk_bounds`, `inner_within_outer`, `roundness_ratio`, `DiskBounds`, `SquaredRadius`, `roundness_max_corners`), the **reconciliation** of `inscribed_disk`'s inner bound with `min_enclosing_circle`'s outer bound: the honest fact that the largest disk **inside** a shape is no larger than the smallest disk **around** it -- yet read naively, side by side, the two **reported floats flip**. A solid 5x5 block reports an inscribed radius of **3.0** (chamfer depth 9 over the orthogonal weight 3) yet an enclosing radius of only `sqrt(8)` ~ **2.828** (the half-diagonal over its corner **centers**), so a naive `inradius <= circumradius` reads **false** for a shape whose inscribed disk plainly sits inside its enclosing disk. The flip is an **artifact of two conventions**, not a fact about the shape: the inner bound reaches to a background center one pixel **outside** the region, while the outer bound reaches only to a foreground center **on** it -- so the inner is credited half a pixel too much and the outer half a pixel too little. This rung fixes **one** convention in which both are measured against the shape as it truly is, a union of unit pixel **squares**: work in **doubled** integer coordinates (a pixel `(x,y)` is the square with corners `(2x,2y)` and `(2x+2,2y+2)`, so centers land on odd coordinates and corners on even ones, every one an exact integer). The **outer** bound becomes the exact minimum enclosing circle of the region's **corners** (handed to `convex_hull` -> `min_enclosing_circle`, its squared radius recomputed here from the support as an exact rational in doubled^2) -- for the block, **50 doubled^2** (circumradius `sqrt(50)/2` ~ 3.536 pixels), the corner reach the pixel-center circle understated. The **inner** bound is the chamfer depth `d` in the same convention: the distance to the region **edge** is `d/3 - 1/2` pixels, which in doubled units is `(2d-3)/3`, its squared radius the exact rational `(2d-3)^2/9` -- for the block, **25 doubled^2** (inradius 2.5 pixels, exactly half the width). `inner_within_outer` decides `r_in^2 <= r_out^2` by **exact i128 cross-multiplication** -- 25 <= 50 for the block, **no tolerance and no flip**. The chamfer is exact for the **orthogonal** geometry these fixtures are limited by (a block, a ring wall, a bar all reach their nearest background straight), so on them the inequality is **guaranteed**; the outer bound and the comparison are exact for **every** shape, and a universal proof for arbitrary diagonal-limited shapes -- bounding the chamfer's approximation of the Euclidean inner radius -- is the honest next horizon, named rather than assumed. The **inner-sees-the-hole** separation is preserved: a hollow ring shares the block's outer contour, so reads the **identical** outer 50 doubled^2, yet its inner shrinks to `9/9 = 1` (a wall one pixel thick) -- the inner bound separating what the outer cannot. `roundness_ratio` reports `inradius / circumradius` in (0,1], near 1 for a compact round shape and far below for a thin bar, now consistent because both radii live in one convention. Everything is bounded: only **boundary** pixels contribute corners (at most `4 * perimeter` under `roundness_max_corners`, `RegionTooLarge` past it); an all-background mask refuses `NoForeground` and a zero-size source `EmptySource` before a corner is read. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the doubled convention, the region-corner gather, and the exact rational squared radii; `inscribed_disk` owns the inner disk, `min_enclosing_circle` owns Welzl and the support, `convex_hull` owns the hull -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Hit test (given a pixel, name the object under it)** -- `hit_test` (`hit_test`, `object_label_at`, `bbox_contains_point`, `bbox_candidate_count`, `HitTestError`), the **selection** companion of `scene_manifest`'s enumeration: where the manifest lists every object on a page, this rung names the **one** object under a point -- the hit test a tap-handler runs the moment a keeper touches a photo or a marketplace tile. `hit_test(manifest, map, x, y)` returns that object's manifest row, or refuses `NoObjectAtPoint` for a background pixel and `PointOutOfBounds` for a point off the map. The decisive fact it makes plain: a **bounding box is necessary but not sufficient**, so the box is only a **prefilter** and the label map -- one exact integer per pixel -- is the **arbiter**. Two facts on metal say it. **Overlap:** an L and a small 2x2 block tucked into the L's concavity are distinct components whose bounding boxes overlap (the block's box `(3,2)-(4,3)` sits wholly inside the L's box `(1,1)-(5,5)`), so a point on the block falls in **both** boxes -- `bbox_candidate_count` reads **2** -- yet `hit_test` names exactly **one** object, the block, because the label at that pixel is unambiguous where the boxes are not. **Hole / concavity:** the L's notch `(2,2)`, and a 5x5 ring's center `(3,3)`, are **background** sitting inside the shape's own bounding box -- `bbox_contains_point` reads true, one candidate, yet `hit_test` refuses `NoObjectAtPoint`, the box claiming a point the object does not hold (the same solid-versus-hollow separation `euler_number` reads as topology, read here as a hit). A plain hit on the L's column resolves to its object, a point in no object's box reads zero candidates and refuses, and the same point reads the same object twice. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the point-to-object decision and the bounding-box prefilter reads; `label` owns the `at` arbiter, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object rows, `regions` owns the bounding box -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Region select (given a rectangle, name every object it covers)** -- `region_select` (`select_in_rect`, `bbox_overlaps_rect`, `bbox_overlap_count`, `is_selected`, `QueryRect`, `RegionSelectError`), the **marquee** companion of `hit_test`'s single tap: where the hit test names the **one** object under a **point**, this rung names **every** object a **rectangle** covers -- the drag-select and crop-region primitive a surface runs the moment a keeper drags a box across a scene. `select_in_rect(manifest, map, rect)` returns the ascending set of labels whose foreground actually enters the rectangle, a valid empty set when the box catches nothing. It carries the hit test's decisive fact **one dimension up**: a **bounding box that overlaps the rectangle is necessary but not sufficient**, so the box is a **prefilter** that over-counts and the label map is the **arbiter**. Two facts on metal say it. **Box overlaps, object absent:** an L `(1,1)-(5,5)` with a small block in its concavity `(3,2)-(4,3)` -- a rectangle drawn tightly on the block overlaps the L's box too (`bbox_overlap_count` reads **2**) yet selects exactly the **block**, because only its foreground enters the box; a rectangle in the empty concavity (column `x=2`) overlaps the L's box (one candidate) yet selects **nothing**, the box claiming a rectangle the object does not fill. **The hole:** a rectangle wholly inside a 5x5 ring's hole `(2,2)-(4,4)` sits within the ring's own box (one candidate) yet covers only background, so the selection is **empty** -- the same solid-versus-hollow separation `euler_number` reads as topology, read here as a marquee that lands on nothing. A whole-scene rectangle selects every object ascending, an off-map marquee is a valid empty selection and one overhanging the edge clamps and still selects, and a malformed rectangle (a low corner past its high corner) refuses `BadRect` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the rectangle-to-object-set decision and the box-overlap prefilter reads; `label` owns the `at` arbiter, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object rows, `regions` owns the bounding box -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Region partition (window versus crossing -- what a rectangle holds whole, clips, and misses)** -- `region_partition` (`contained_in_rect`, `clipped_in_rect`, `partition_counts`, `rect_contains_bbox`, `is_in`, `PartitionCounts`, `RegionPartitionError`), the **window-versus-crossing** companion of `region_select`'s marquee: where the region select names every object a rectangle **crosses** (any foreground pixel enters), this rung **partitions** the scene into three disjoint classes -- **contained** (every pixel inside, what a window select keeps), **clipped** (some pixels inside, some out -- the objects a crop cuts, what a crossing select keeps and a window select drops), and **outside** (no pixel inside). It carries the **exact inversion** of the marquee's own lesson. `region_select` proved a bounding box that **overlaps** the rectangle is necessary but not sufficient for crossing -- the box over-counts, and only the label map arbitrates. For **containment** the tight bounding box is not a loose prefilter but the **exact arbiter**: an object lies wholly inside the rectangle **if and only if** its box does, because the box is the object's own min/max pixel envelope, so `contained_in_rect` reads the containment set from the **boxes alone**, with no pixel scan, where `region_select` needed a full clamped scan. Two facts on metal say it. **The box is exact for containment:** for every object and every query rectangle, `rect_contains_bbox` (pure box arithmetic) agrees pixel-for-pixel with a full scan of the object's foreground -- no concavity gap, no hole gap, a holed ring wholly inside contained by its box exactly, its background hole irrelevant. **The clip set is where the two modes diverge:** contained is a subset of crossing (every object holds at least one pixel), so `clipped = crossing \ contained` is exactly the set a crop cuts. Over a scene of a block box `(1,1)-(3,3)`, a block box `(9,1)-(11,3)`, a ring box `(1,5)-(3,7)`, and a block box `(5,5)-(8,6)`: a rectangle `(0,0)-(6,7)` **contains** the first block and the ring, **clips** the straddling block (box past the edge `x=6`, left pixels inside), and leaves the far block **outside**, so a window select keeps two where a crossing select keeps three; the whole canvas contains all four and clips none; a tight interior box `(2,2)-(6,6)` contains **none** and clips three, the widest divergence; a rectangle exactly on a box contains it alone at inclusive corners. `contained + clipped + outside` always sums to the component count, an off-map rectangle is a valid all-outside partition, and a malformed rectangle refuses `BadRect` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the containment split; `region_select` owns the crossing decision and the `QueryRect`, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object boxes -- a **reading over a reading**.
- **Lasso select (given a polygon, name every object it covers)** -- `lasso_select` (`select_in_poly`, `point_in_poly`, `poly_bbox_overlaps`, `poly_bbox_overlap_count`, `is_selected`, `LassoPoly`, `LassoSelectError`), the **free-select** companion of `region_select`'s marquee: where the region select names every object a **rectangle** covers, this rung names every object an **arbitrary polygon** covers -- the lasso a surface runs the moment a keeper draws a loop around a subject rather than a box. `select_in_poly(manifest, map, poly)` returns the ascending set of labels whose foreground truly falls inside the polygon, a valid empty set when the loop catches nothing. A pixel is covered exactly when it is **foreground AND inside the polygon**, decided by an **even-odd ray cast in DOUBLED coordinates** -- each pixel sampled at its half-integer center (an odd number) so a scanline never lands on an even-coordinate vertex, and each edge crossing judged strictly-to-the-right by **i128 cross-multiplication** rather than a divided intersection, so the parity is an exact integer, **no float and no tolerance** (the same doubled-coordinate device `fill_polygon` fills by). It carries the marquee's fact **one shape freer**: a bounding box that overlaps is necessary but not sufficient, **and a concave lasso can exclude an object any convex query would include**. The decisive **concavity** crux on metal: a right-pointing concave **chevron** (back `(2,2)`, tip `(8,6)`, back `(2,10)`, notch `(4,6)`) drawn so its tip covers one object while its notch opens around another selects **exactly the tip object**, though the chevron's box overlaps **both** (`poly_bbox_overlap_count` reads **2**) and its **convex hull holds the notch object too** -- every pixel of that object falls in the excluded triangle `(2,2)-(4,6)-(2,10)`, outside the loop, the capability a marquee or any convex query could never carve. **Completeness** proves the exclusion is geometry, never a blind spot: a small quad drawn tightly around the notch object selects it exactly. A whole-scene lasso selects every object ascending, a lasso off the map is a valid empty selection, and a two-vertex degenerate polygon refuses `DegeneratePoly` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the point-in-polygon arbiter and the box-overlap prefilter reads; `label` owns the `at` arbiter, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object rows, `regions` owns the bounding box, `contour` owns the `Point` vertex -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Lasso partition (window versus crossing over a free-form loop)** -- `lasso_partition` (`contained_in_poly`, `clipped_in_poly`, `partition_counts`, `all_pixels_in_poly`, `is_in`, `PartitionCounts`, `LassoPartitionError`), the free-form companion of `region_partition` and the window-versus-crossing companion of `lasso_select`: where the region partition splits a **rectangle's** crossing set by containment, this rung splits a **polygon's** crossing set the same way -- **contained** (every pixel inside the loop, what a window select keeps), **clipped** (some pixels inside, some out -- the objects a lasso-crop cuts, what a crossing select keeps and a window select drops), and **outside** (no pixel inside). It carries the **exact inversion of region_partition's own lesson**, which was itself the inversion of the marquee's. For a **rectangle**, region_partition proved the tight bounding box is the **exact arbiter** of containment -- an object lies wholly inside if and only if its box does, so the containment set reads from the **boxes alone**, no pixel scan. For a **concave lasso** that lesson **breaks**: a polygon's concavity can carve pixels out of the middle of a region whose whole bounding box, and even both bounding-box corners, lie inside the loop, so box containment is **necessary yet not sufficient**, and the pixel scan comes **back** as the only arbiter. Two facts on metal say it. **The concavity defeats the box:** a notched square (the square `(1,1)-(11,11)` with a vertical slot cut down from the top edge, `x` in `(5,7)`, `y` in `(1,7)`) holds a horizontal bar whose **whole box** `(3,5)-(9,5)` lies inside the polygon's box **and** whose **both box corners** lie inside the loop -- so a box-approximation, the exact arbiter for a rectangle, would call it **contained** -- yet a full pixel scan finds its middle pixels in the slot, **outside** the loop, so it is **clipped**; only the even-odd arbiter run over every pixel decides the truth. **Two independent scans agree:** `contained_in_poly` (one pass, an any-pixel-outside tally) names the same containment as `all_pixels_in_poly` (a plain per-label full re-scan) for every object, so the containment is **proven, not merely computed**. Over the notched square the partition contains one object, clips one, and leaves one outside; a convex whole-scene lasso contains all three and clips none; `contained + clipped + outside` always sums to the component count; an off-map lasso is a valid all-outside partition; a two-vertex degenerate polygon refuses `DegeneratePoly` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the containment split; `lasso_select` owns the crossing decision, the point-in-polygon arbiter, and the `LassoPoly`, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object rows -- a **reading over a reading**.
- **Region summary (read a rectangle as a small digest table)** -- `region_summary` (`region_summary`, `largest_contained`, `RegionSummary`, `RegionSummaryError`), the **digest** companion of `region_partition`'s three-class split: where the partition reads the three class **sizes**, this rung reads the three class **facts** -- for a query rectangle it walks the manifest once, classifies each object by the exact box test, and folds each object's whole-scene **area** and **hole count** into its class, filling the **marquee status line** a surface shows (how many objects a window select keeps, how many a crossing select would add, how much foreground each class carries, and how many holed objects sit in each). It carries the **box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted from the object to its area**. A per-class area sum reads each object's **whole** foreground area, the area `scene_manifest` carries regardless of any rectangle. For the **contained** class that whole area **is** the pixels inside, since every pixel of a wholly-held object lies inside, so `contained_area` is **exact**. For the **clipped** class the whole area **strictly exceeds** the pixels inside, since a clipped object holds some foreground outside by definition, so `clipped_area` **over-counts** and `touched_area` (contained plus clipped) is only an **upper bound** on the true in-rect foreground, tight only where nothing is clipped. Two facts on metal say it. **The contained area is exact, the clipped area over-counts:** over the partition scene (a block area 9, a block area 9, a ring area 8 holed, a block area 8) a rectangle `(0,0)-(6,7)` holds two objects whole and clips one, so `contained_area` reads **17** which a full pixel scan confirms is exactly their in-rect foreground, while `clipped_area` reads the clipped block's whole **8** though a pixel scan finds only **4** of its pixels inside (its right half cut off at `x=6`). **The touched area is an upper bound, tight only at the no-clip fixed point:** `touched_area` reads **25** where the true in-rect foreground is **21**, the gap exactly the clipped-away pixels; when the rectangle holds the whole scene nothing is clipped so `touched_area` (**34**) equals the true in-rect foreground exactly. The three counts partition the scene, `largest_contained` names the biggest object a window select keeps and refuses `NoContained` when nothing is whole, an off-map rectangle is a valid all-outside digest, and a malformed rectangle refuses `BadRect` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the per-class fold of area and holes; `region_partition` owns the classification and the exact box test, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object area and holes -- a **reading over two readings**.
- **Lasso summary (read a free-form loop as a small digest table)** -- `lasso_summary` (`lasso_summary`, `largest_contained`, `LassoSummary`, `LassoSummaryError`), the **free-form** companion of `region_summary` and the **digest** companion of `lasso_partition`: where the region summary reads a **rectangle** query as a digest, this rung reads a **polygon** query the same way -- it walks the manifest once, classifies each object by the even-odd **pixel arbiter** (not a box test), and folds each object's whole-scene **area** and **hole count** into its window-versus-crossing class, filling the **lasso status line** a surface shows. It carries the **box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted from the object to its area one shape freer**, where the concavity now moves **area** between classes. `region_summary` classified by the tight box, the exact arbiter for a **rectangle**, so its `contained_area` was exact by construction. For a **concave lasso** that box test **breaks**: a slot can carve pixels from the middle of an object whose whole box, and both box corners, lie inside the loop, so a box-based digest would fold that object's **whole** area into `contained_area` -- a **lie** the moment any pixel sits in the concavity. This rung classifies by the pixel arbiter instead, keeping `contained_area` honestly **exact** by **demoting** the concavity-carved object to **clipped**. Two facts on metal say it. **The concavity moves area from contained to clipped:** over a notched-square lasso holding a ring whole (area 8), a bar whose whole box `(3,5)-(9,5)` and both box corners lie inside the loop yet whose middle sits in the slot (area 7), and a block in the slot (area 4), a box-approximation would call the bar **contained** and inflate `contained_area` to a value a pixel scan could never confirm; the pixel arbiter instead reads `contained_area` **8**, which a full pixel scan confirms is exactly the ring's in-poly foreground, and demotes the bar to `clipped_area` **7** though a pixel scan finds only **5** of its pixels inside (its two middle carved by the slot). **The touched area is an upper bound, tight only at the no-clip fixed point:** `touched_area` reads **15** where the true in-poly foreground is **13**, the gap exactly the carved pixels; a convex whole-scene lasso holds all three whole so `touched_area` (**19**) equals the whole scene's foreground exactly. The three counts partition the scene, `largest_contained` names the biggest object a window select keeps and refuses `NoContained` when nothing is whole, an off-map lasso is a valid all-outside digest, and a two-vertex degenerate polygon refuses `DegeneratePoly` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the per-class fold of area and holes; `lasso_partition` owns the containment set and the pixel arbiter, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object area and holes -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Selection summary (read a selection as a small table)** -- `selection_summary` (`total_selected_area`, `largest_in_selection`, `holed_in_selection_count`, `largest_holed_in_selection`, `sort_selection_by_area`, `SelectionSummaryError`), what a surface **does** with a selection once it has one: where the selection ladder (`hit_test` a point, `region_select` a rectangle, `lasso_select` a polygon) each returns an **ascending set of dense labels**, this rung joins `scene_manifest`'s per-object rows over just that subset and reads the selection as a whole -- how much is selected, which selected object is largest, which are holed, and the selection sorted by size. It composes over **any** selector, since all three speak the same label-set language. The decisive fact it makes plain: a selection is a **subset**, so a read over it is strictly sharper than the same read over the whole scene -- the scene's largest object, and its largest holed object, can **both** lie outside the selection. Over the manifest's four-object scene (a solid block area 25 no hole, a small ring area 8 holed, a large ring area 16 holed, a small block area 4 no hole), the selection `{small ring, small block}` **excludes** both the scene's largest (the block) and its `largest_holed` (the large ring), so `largest_in_selection` names the **small ring** (area 8) and `largest_holed_in_selection` names the **small ring** too -- neither whole-scene read lands there. The filter is sharper than the scene's: that selection holds **one** holed object though the scene holds two (the large ring, holed yet unselected, does not count), and a non-empty selection of only unholed objects refuses `NoHoledInSelection` (a fact about holes, not emptiness). And the sort **reverses the label order**: the selection `{small ring, large ring}` arrives ascending by label `2, 3` yet `sort_selection_by_area` returns the large ring first, labels `3, 2`, since area descending disagrees with label ascending. The whole selection agrees with the scene's aggregates, an empty selection is valid (zero area) yet `largest_in_selection` refuses `EmptySelection`, and a malformed label set (background, past the count, out of ascending order, too long) refuses `BadSelection` at the edge having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the reads over a selection; `scene_manifest` owns the per-object rows, the selectors own the label set -- a **reading over a reading**.
- **Selection bounds (frame a selection)** -- `selection_bounds` (`selection_bbox`, `box_contains_object`, `Box`, `SelectionBoundsError`), the **extent** companion of `selection_summary`'s facts: where the summary reads how much a selection adds up to, this rung reads how far it **spans** -- `selection_bbox(manifest, selection)` returns the smallest axis-aligned box containing every selected object's bounding box, the **group-handle frame** a surface draws to zoom to, drag, or handle a set of chosen objects at once. The decisive fact it makes plain -- the box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted to the **selection**: a union box **contains more than it selects**. Over three 2x2 blocks at the corner `(1,1)`, the middle `(5,5)`, and the far corner `(9,9)`, the selection of the two corners (labels `1, 3`, skipping the middle) frames the box `(1,1)-(10,10)`, and `box_contains_object` reads **true** for the **unselected** middle block -- its box `(5,5)-(6,6)` sits wholly inside the union -- so the frame holds an object the selection never named; **containment of a box is never membership of the set**, only the label set says what is chosen. The union exceeds either selected object's own box on both axes, and its area `100` covers the background the two 4-pixel blocks never fill; a single-object selection frames exactly that object's box and holds nothing else; an empty selection has no extent and refuses `EmptySelection` rather than inventing a degenerate box, and a malformed label set refuses `BadSelection` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the box union and the reads over it; `scene_manifest` owns the per-object boxes, the selectors own the label set -- a **reading over a reading**.
- **Object relation (how two objects sit)** -- `object_relation` (`object_relation`, `boxes_overlap`, `nearest_chebyshev`, `Relation`, `ObjectRelationError`), the **pair** read beneath the whole scene-read family: where `hit_test` names one object and the selectors name a set, this rung reads how **two** objects sit relative to each other -- whether their bounding boxes overlap, which of the four **strict directions** holds (`a_left_of_b`, `a_right_of_b`, `a_above_b`, `a_below_b`, each true only when a's box sits wholly to that side of b's), and the **exact nearest-pixel Chebyshev distance** between the two components. The decisive fact it makes plain -- the box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted to a **relation**, in both directions: a box comparison **bounds** the objects' true nearness yet never **determines** it. An L (box `(1,1)-(5,5)`) and a small block tucked in its concavity (box `(3,2)-(4,3)` wholly inside the L's) have **overlapping** boxes, yet their nearest pixels are Chebyshev distance `2` apart -- the box **over-claims nearness**, the objects do not touch. Two diagonal 2x2 blocks are box-**disjoint** with `a_left_of_b` and `a_above_b` both true (a to the upper-left of b), yet their nearest pixels are also `2` apart, exactly the **tightest** two distinct 8-connected components can sit -- the box **over-claims farness**, strict directional separation never means the objects are far. The distance is a real **graded** measure, not a touch flag: a far dot reads Chebyshev distance `5` from the first block, strictly larger than the `2` between the tight pair; and its floor is **structural** -- two different labels are two distinct components, so no pixel of one is 8-adjacent to a pixel of the other and the nearest Chebyshev distance is always at least `2`. The mirror relation reads the symmetric direction and the same distance; a self-relation refuses `SameObject` and a background or past-the-count label refuses `BadObjectLabel` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the pair relation and the nearest-pixel scan; `label` owns the `at` arbiter and 8-connectivity, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object boxes -- a **reading over a reading**.
- **Scene graph (the whole scene's pairwise structure)** -- `scene_graph` (`build_scene_graph`, `relation_of`, `nearest_neighbor`, `box_contains`, `box_parent`, `is_root`, `SceneGraph`, `scene_graph_max_objects`, `SceneGraphError`), the **whole-scene** read above the pair: where `object_relation` reads how **two** objects sit, this rung joins that relation over **every** pair into one matrix, so a surface asks the scene-level questions no single pair can answer -- who is nearest to whom, and which object nests inside which. The decisive fact it makes plain -- the box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted from a **pair** to the scene's whole **structure**, in two shapes at once. **The nesting forest over-claims enclosure:** `box_parent(a)` names the object whose bounding box is the **tightest** box wholly containing a's box -- the immediate parent in a box-nesting forest. Three bracket-L / dot objects whose boxes nest (big-L box `(1,1)-(9,9)` contains middle-L box `(3,3)-(7,7)` contains dot box `(5,5)-(5,5)`) build a clean **three-level** forest, `box_parent` reading the dot's parent as the tighter **middle-L** (not the big-L that also contains it) and the middle-L's parent as the big-L, a **root** -- yet `object_relation` proves each child sits Chebyshev distance `2` **apart** from its box parent, disjoint, touched by neither; the box hierarchy is a hierarchy of **frames**, never of **substance**. **The nearest neighbor is directed, not symmetric:** `nearest_neighbor(a)` weighs every other object (a whole-scene read no pair holds), reading the dot's nearest as the middle-L (`2` apart), yet the middle-L's nearest is the big-L (`2` apart and the earlier label on the tie), **not** the dot -- an object can be someone's nearest without their being its own. The nearest gap honors the **structural floor** of `2` (distinct 8-connected components are never 8-adjacent); a self relation and a past-the-count label refuse `BadObjectLabel`, a single-object graph refuses `NoNeighbor` having no neighbor to name, a scene past `scene_graph_max_objects` refuses `TooManyObjects`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the pairwise matrix, the nearest-neighbor read, and the box-containment forest; `object_relation` owns each pair's overlap, direction, and exact distance, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object boxes -- a **reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Reading order (the whole scene's page order)** -- `reading_order` (`reading_order`, `reads_before`, `reading_rank`, `entry_reads_before`, `ReadingOrderError`), the **sequence** read above the pairwise structure: where `scene_graph` holds the four **strict** directional flags for every pair, this rung folds them into one **total** top-to-bottom, left-to-right page order a surface reads to lay objects out, narrate a scene, or number a form. The decisive fact it makes plain -- the box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted one last step, from a **partial** relation to a **total** order. The strict flags are a **partial** order: two objects whose boxes overlap on both axes are strictly neither, so every flag reads false and the pair is left **unordered**; a reading order must seat **every** object, so it cannot be a topological sort of the flags -- a key-based total order (top edge `min_y`, then left edge `min_x`, then dense `label`) is what seats them. **The reading order outruns the strict flags:** an L (box `(1,1)-(5,5)`) and a block tucked in its concavity (box `(3,2)-(4,3)`) have **overlapping** boxes, so all four flags read false and the scene graph leaves the pair unordered, yet the L's top edge sits above the block's so `reads_before(L, block)` is **true** -- the total order names a first where every strict flag is silent. **The reading order refines, it never contradicts:** where the boxes **are** strictly separated (the L wholly above a lower object, `a_above_b` true), the order **agrees** and seats the upper object first, adding order where the flags were silent and reversing none they asserted. **The order earns its keep:** a flag-shaped object whose top row starts at `x=8` (labeled **after** a small block at `x=4`) yet whose body reaches to `x=3` (leftward of the block's `x=4`) reads **before** that block, so the sequence `1,2,4,3` genuinely reorders the raster labels `1,2,3,4` -- no mere relabeling. The result is a **permutation** and a **strict total order** (for every distinct pair exactly one of `reads_before` holds, and it is transitive); `reading_rank`, read independently by counting predecessors, agrees with each object's seat; a reads-before of an object with itself and a background or past-the-count label refuse `BadObjectLabel` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the total-order comparator, the permutation, and the rank; `scene_graph` owns the pairwise flag matrix the order refines, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object boxes the comparator reads -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Nesting depth (the box-nesting forest's depth)** -- `nesting_depth` (`nesting_depth`, `forest_height`, `is_leaf`, `NestingDepthError`), the **transitive** read above `scene_graph`'s **immediate** `box_parent`: where `box_parent(a)` names only the tightest frame one step above `a`, this rung walks that tightest-parent chain to a root and counts it, so a surface reads how deeply each object is framed to indent an outline, size a frame by its depth, or find a nesting's tips. The decisive fact it makes plain -- two shapes a step past what `box_parent` alone can say. **The depth is the box-ancestor chain, which the immediate parent hides:** a branching three-object forest under one root -- a big-L (box `(1,1)-(11,11)`, a root, depth 0) framing a middle-L (box `(3,3)-(7,7)`, depth 1) framing a dot (box `(5,5)-(5,5)`, depth 2) -- reads the dot's depth **2** as the **two** frames stacked above it, the middle-L **and** the big-L, though `box_parent` named only the tightest, the middle-L; the chain the single immediate read never showed, and `forest_height` **2** the deepest such chain. **The forest branches, so same depth is neither nearness nor nesting:** the middle-L (depth 1) and a lone dot dropped elsewhere in the big-L (box `(9,3)-(9,3)`, also depth 1, its parent the big-L directly) share a depth under the one root, yet `object_relation` proves them disjoint and box-unnested, neither box containing the other and their nearest pixels apart -- depth counts frames above you, never who stands beside you. And the family's lesson carries up whole: the depth is a depth of **frames**, never of **substance** -- every parent-child step is a box wholly containing a box, yet `object_relation` proves each child sits Chebyshev distance `2` **apart** from its box parent, touched by neither. The **leaves** are the tips (the two dots, nothing nesting under them); the walk is bounded by the object count, a longer chain refusing `NestingCycle`, and a background or past-the-count label refusing `BadObjectLabel`; a single-object scene reads height 0, depth 0, a leaf and a root at once. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the depth walk, the forest height, and the leaf read; `scene_graph` owns `box_parent` and the pairwise matrix, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object boxes, `object_relation` owns each pair's exact distance -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Overlap degree (the scene's box-overlap adjacency graph)** -- `overlap_degree` (`overlap_degree`, `is_isolated`, `total_overlap_edges`, `most_overlapping`, `OverlapDegreeError`), a **whole-scene graph fact** no pair and no chain holds: how many other objects each object's box overlaps, read straight from `scene_graph`'s precomputed matrix. The decisive fact it makes plain -- the box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted to a **graph degree**, in two shapes. **The hub overlaps all yet touches none, and the graph is a star, never a clique:** a big bracket-L (box `(1,1)-(9,9)`) framing two dots in its concavity (dot A at `(4,4)`, dot B at `(6,6)`) has the highest overlap degree **2**, its box overlapping both dots' boxes, yet `object_relation` proves it sits Chebyshev distance `2` or more **apart** from each dot, touched by neither; and the two dots, both overlapping the hub, do **not** overlap each other (their single-pixel boxes are disjoint), so the overlap graph is a **star** centered on the hub with exactly **two** edges, never the three-edge **clique** a transitive relation would build -- box overlap is not transitive, and degree is a **local** count, so the high-degree object can be the loneliest. **The degree is symmetric, unlike the nearest neighbor:** box overlap is symmetric, `a` overlapping `b` exactly when `b` overlaps `a`, so the adjacency is an **undirected** graph and the degree is well-defined without a direction -- the sharp contrast to `scene_graph`'s `nearest_neighbor`, which reads a **directed** pointer over the same matrix. A far dot outside the hub's box (box `(11,11)-(11,11)`) is **isolated**, degree zero, standing alone though it shares the scene; `most_overlapping` names the hub, the box that frames the most never the object that touches the most; a background or past-the-count label refuses `BadObjectLabel`, the busiest read of an empty scene refuses `NoObjects`, a lone scene reads degree zero and zero edges. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the degree count, the edge total, the busiest read, and the isolated read; `scene_graph` owns the pairwise matrix each overlap flag is read from, `object_relation` owns each pair's overlap and exact distance, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object boxes -- a **reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Overlap clusters (the box-overlap graph's connected components)** -- `overlap_clusters` (`overlap_clusters`, `cluster_of`, `same_cluster`, `cluster_size`, `largest_cluster`, `cluster_count`, `object_count`, `Clustering`, `OverlapClustersError`), the **global** shape the local degrees build: gather objects whose boxes overlap, directly or through a chain, into connected clusters, read straight from `scene_graph`'s precomputed matrix. Where `overlap_degree` counts one object's edges, this rung reads the components those edges form. The decisive fact it makes plain -- the box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted to **connectivity**, in two shapes. **Connectivity is the transitive closure of a non-transitive relation:** a big bracket-L hub (box `(1,1)-(9,9)`) framing two dots in its concavity (dot A at `(4,4)`, dot B at `(6,6)`) overlaps both, so the hub, dot A, and dot B all land in **one** cluster; the two dots share that cluster **through** the hub though their own single-pixel boxes never overlap, so a cluster holds a pair that shares no edge -- box overlap is not transitive (the star is never a clique, its three inner objects bound by only **two** edges), while connectivity is exactly its transitive closure. **The cluster over-claims a touching group:** `object_relation` proves every object in the star's cluster sits Chebyshev distance `2` or more **apart** from every other (hub-dot and dot-dot alike disjoint), so the connected cluster is a group of **frames**, never of touching substance, and only `object_relation` says who truly touches. A far dot outside the hub's box (box `(11,11)-(11,11)`) overlaps nothing, so it stands as its own **singleton** cluster; the cluster count is **2**, strictly fewer than the object count **4**, exactly because connectivity merged the star's three boxes into one; `largest_cluster` names the star of three, `cluster_size` the far cluster exactly one; a background or past-the-count object label refuses `BadObjectLabel`, a zero or past-the-count cluster id refuses `BadClusterId`, a lone scene is one cluster of size one. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the connectivity -- the union across overlap edges and the dense cluster labeling; `scene_graph` owns the pairwise matrix, `overlap_degree` the degree and edge count, `object_relation` each pair's overlap and exact distance, `scene_manifest` the per-object boxes -- a **reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster facts (the union frame and joined facts of a connected cluster)** -- `cluster_facts` (`cluster_bbox`, `cluster_area`, `cluster_holed_count`, `largest_in_cluster`, `box_contains_object`, `Box`, `ClusterFactsError`), the payoff of the connected clustering: where `overlap_clusters` answers **who** belongs to which cluster, this rung answers **what** each cluster is -- its bounding **frame** (the union of its members' boxes, the group-handle a surface draws around a connected region) and its joined facts (total foreground area, holed-member count, largest member), a `selection_summary`-style read over a cluster instead of a hand-picked selection. The decisive fact it makes plain -- the box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted to the **cluster**, in two shapes. **The cluster frame over-claims membership across clusters:** two bracket-L's reaching apart (A box `(1,1)-(6,6)`, B a ring box `(4,4)-(9,9)`) whose boxes overlap through the shared corner `(4,4)-(6,6)` connect into **one** cluster whose union frame spans `(1,1)-(9,9)`, and `box_contains_object` reads **true** for a lone block C (box `(8,1)-(9,2)`) sitting in the top-right gap outside either member's box, though C belongs to a **different** singleton cluster -- the frame a surface draws around a connected group holds an object the cluster never named, so containment of a box is never membership of a cluster, only `cluster_of` says who belongs. **The cluster is frames, not substance:** `object_relation` proves A and B sit Chebyshev distance `2` or more **apart** (connected only by their overlapping boxes, touched by neither), so the cluster's total foreground area **31** (A's 11 plus the ring's 20) is far less than its frame's area **81**, the frame covering all the background between the standing-apart members; `largest_in_cluster` names the ring of area 20, `cluster_holed_count` reads exactly one holed member, and the singleton cluster is the lone block of area 4 with no holed member; a zero or past-the-count cluster id refuses `BadClusterId`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the per-cluster join -- the union of member boxes and the aggregate reads over that subset; `overlap_clusters` owns the connectivity and dense cluster labeling, `scene_graph` the manifest, `scene_manifest` the per-object boxes and areas, `object_relation` each pair's exact distance -- a **reading over a reading over a reading**.

- **Click-nearest (turn three points into three whole groups, then read which candidate is nearer the anchor)** -- `click_nearest` (`nearer_group`, `nearest_candidate_gap`, `is_equidistant`, `Nearer`), the ternary read that the two-click family (`click_relation`, `click_order`) sets up: where those weigh **two** tapped groups, this adds the third tap -- an **anchor** and two **candidates** -- and answers "of these two, which is closer to that one." It resolves each click to its connected group through `hit_test` and `overlap_clusters`' `cluster_of`, then compares the anchor-to-candidate gap for each through `click_relation`'s `click_pair_distance`. `nearer_group` names the verdict (`first_nearer`, `second_nearer`, or `tie`), `nearest_candidate_gap` the true distance to the closer of the two, `is_equidistant` whether the two candidates tie. The decisive fact it makes plain -- nearness is a **group** fact, not a **pixel** fact, so the candidate whose **group** sits nearer the anchor's group can be the candidate whose tapped **pixel** sits **farther** from the anchor's tapped pixel, in three shapes over four singleton objects (A the anchor block box `(1,1)-(2,2)`, B a large block box `(4,1)-(9,6)` group gap 2 from A, C a small block box `(7,8)-(8,9)` group gap 6 from A, F a block box `(1,4)-(2,5)` group gap 2 from A). **The group-nearer candidate is the pixel-farther tap:** an anchor tap on A `(1,1)`, a candidate tap on B's far corner `(9,6)`, and a candidate tap on C `(7,8)` read `nearer_group` **first** (B's group gap 2 beats C's 6) yet the B tap sits Chebyshev `8` from the anchor pixel while the C tap sits only `7` -- the group-nearest candidate is the pixel-farthest tap, only the groups the taps expand to deciding the nearest. **The verdict is invariant to where inside a group a keeper taps:** tapping B's near corner `(4,1)` reads the **same** verdict first and the **same** nearest gap 2 though the clicked pixel now sits `3` from the anchor -- `nearer_group` weighs whole groups, so the tap site never moves the answer. **Two distinct groups can tie:** an anchor tap on A, a candidate tap on B (group gap 2), and a candidate tap on F (group gap 2) read `nearer_group` **tie** and `is_equidistant` **true** -- two visibly separate groups equidistant from the anchor, the tie a real ternary outcome rather than an error. A candidate sharing the anchor's group reads gap `0` (the nearest possible, no refusal), a background tap refuses `EmptyClick` by name, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds` having read nothing, and the same triple reads the same verdict twice. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the ternary comparison of two anchor-to-candidate gaps; `click_relation` owns `click_pair_distance`, `hit_test` owns the point-to-object arbiter, `overlap_clusters` owns `cluster_of`, `cluster_adjacency` owns the true `cluster_distance`.
- **Click-between (turn three points into three whole groups, then read whether the middle sits between the endpoints)** -- `click_between` (`between_in_order`, `between_in_space`, `classify_betweenness`, `Betweenness`, `ClickBetweenError`), the ternary companion `click_nearest` sets up: where that reads which of two candidates is **nearer** an anchor, this reads whether a candidate is **between** two others -- two **endpoint** taps and one **middle-candidate** tap -- and reads it in the two registers the click family already speaks, the reading **sequence** (`click_order`'s `click_group_min_rank`) and **space** (`click_bounds`'s `click_bbox`). `between_in_order` is true when the middle group's page rank falls in the open interval between the endpoint ranks; `between_in_space` is true when the middle group's frame sits wholly inside the box the two endpoint frames span; `classify_betweenness` names where the two agree and disagree (`both`, `order_only`, `space_only`, `neither`). The decisive fact it makes plain -- reading-order betweenness and spatial betweenness are **different** relations that differ in **kind**, a bounding-box span **over-claiming** the between the total reading order names exactly, over six singleton objects (P box `(1,1)-(2,2)` rank 1, V a tall block box `(11,1)-(12,12)` rank 2, W box `(18,1)-(19,2)` rank 3, Q box `(4,4)-(5,5)` rank 4, S box `(15,4)-(16,5)` rank 5, R box `(7,7)-(8,8)` rank 6). **Both:** Q reads between P and R **and** its box sits inside their span box `(1,1)-(8,8)`. **Order-only:** V reads between P and R (rank 2 in `(1,6)`) yet its tall box escapes their span box on x. **Space-only:** S's box sits inside the V..W span box `(11,1)-(19,12)` (the tall V stretching the box down over it) yet S reads **after** both endpoints (rank 5 outside `(2,3)`). **Neither:** R reads after both P and Q and its box escapes their span box. And the two relations part in **kind**: **totality of the reading middle** -- over any three distinct groups exactly one is the reading-between (of P, Q, R only Q), while **no spatial middle at all** can hold -- over Q, R, S (an L in the plane) none sits inside the span of the other two, yet the reading order still names S the middle, the total order always seating a middle where the plane need not. The reads are symmetric in the endpoints; two endpoint taps sharing a group refuse `SameGroup` by name (no interval to be between), a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds` having read nothing, and the same triple reads the same verdict twice. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the ternary betweenness tests over the two registers; `click_order` owns `click_group_min_rank` and `click_group_id`, `click_bounds` owns `click_bbox`, `hit_test` owns the point-to-object arbiter, `overlap_clusters` owns `cluster_of`.
- **Click-collinear (turn three points into three whole groups, then read whether they line up)** -- `click_collinear` (`click_group_center_doubled`, `three_collinear`, `share_row_band`, `share_col_band`, `axis_aligned`, `classify_collinear`, `CenterD`, `Collinearity`, `ClickCollinearError`), the third and **closing** ternary spatial relation the click family climbs to: where `click_nearest` reads which of two candidates is nearer an anchor and `click_between` whether a candidate sits between two others, this reads whether all three **line up** -- and reads it in two registers, the **exact** register (the cross product of the three group-frame centers, zero iff truly collinear) and the **axis** register (whether the three frames share a row or column band, the alignment guide a layout surface draws). `click_group_center_doubled` reads a group frame's exact **doubled** center (`min_x + max_x`, `min_y + max_y`, so the midpoint stays an integer with no float), `three_collinear` tests the center cross product in i64, `share_row_band` / `share_col_band` / `axis_aligned` test the shared bands, `classify_collinear` names where the two registers agree and disagree (`both`, `exact_only`, `band_only`, `neither`). The decisive fact it makes plain -- exact center-collinearity and axis-band alignment are **different** relations, **neither a subset of the other**, the box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted to a **triple** and split by **dimension** (the band a 1-D projection-overlap fact, collinearity a 2-D area fact -- the cross product is twice the signed area of the center triangle, zero iff degenerate), over six singleton objects (P box `(1,1)-(2,2)` center-doubled `(3,3)`, Q box `(5,1)-(6,2)` center `(11,3)`, R box `(9,1)-(10,2)` center `(19,3)`, D box `(5,5)-(6,6)` center `(11,11)`, E box `(9,9)-(10,10)` center `(19,19)`, F a tall block box `(13,1)-(14,4)` center `(27,5)`). **Both:** P, Q, R are collinear on the top row **and** share the row band `y in [1,2]`. **Exact-only:** P, D, E are collinear on the diagonal yet share no row or column band -- a line the axis snap misses entirely. **Band-only:** P, Q, F share the row band (F's top edge reaches it) yet their centers are **not** collinear (F's center rises off the P-Q line) -- the band **over-claims** a line that is not there. **Neither:** P, D, R form a real triangle sharing no band. And the two relations part in **kind**: a **diagonal** line escapes every band while a **band** holds no line, and both relations are **symmetric** in the unordered triple, invariant under every permutation of the three taps -- a line does not care which end you name first.
