Three taps sharing a group refuse `SameGroup` by name (three things that line up must be three things), a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds` having read nothing, and the same triple reads the same verdict twice. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the ternary collinearity tests over the two registers; `click_bounds` owns `click_bbox`, `click_order` owns `click_group_id`, `hit_test` owns the point-to-object arbiter, `overlap_clusters` owns `cluster_of`.
- **Click-side (two anchor taps name a directed line, a third tap reads which side)** -- `click_side` (`which_side`, `is_on_line`, `same_side`, `Side`, `ClickSideError`), which **lifts** `click_collinear`'s zero-or-not collinearity read to the full three-way **orientation**: name a directed line by two anchor taps (from A toward B), then read whether a third query tap's group falls to the **left**, **on**, or **right** of it, from the **sign** of the same cross product `click_collinear` reads. `which_side` returns the three-way `Side`, `is_on_line` the zero convenience (its `on` set exactly `three_collinear`'s collinear set, cross-checked on metal), `same_side` whether two queries share an open half-plane. The decisive fact it makes plain -- this relation is **directed** and **role-bearing** where collinearity was symmetric: `which_side` is **antisymmetric** in the anchors (swapping A and B flips `left` and `right` while `on` stays fixed) where `three_collinear` was invariant under every permutation, the cross product twice the signed triangle area and its sign the orientation, so a **diagonal** line splits the plane too (`left`/`right` the orientation against the actual line, not merely above/below), while `same_side` is **invariant** under the anchor swap (both queries flip together) and reads `false` if either query is `on` (a point on the divider takes no side). Two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup` by name (a line needs two points); the query may coincide with an anchor (reads `on`); a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`; a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the sign reads and the same-side test; `click_collinear` owns `click_group_center_doubled` and `three_collinear`, `click_order` owns `click_group_id`, `hit_test` the point-to-object arbiter, `overlap_clusters` owns `cluster_of`.
- **Line partition (a directed line sorts the whole scene left, on, and right)** -- `line_partition` (`object_side`, `left_of_line`, `on_line_objects`, `right_of_line`, `partition_counts`, `is_in`, `LineCounts`, `LinePartitionError`), which **lifts** `click_side`'s per-query which-side read from one tapped group to the **whole scene**, exactly as `region_partition` lifted `region_select`'s one-rectangle crossing to a scene-wide partition: given a directed line named by two anchor taps, it sorts every object into three disjoint classes -- **left**, **on**, and **right** -- by which side of the line its **group** center falls. It carries the **exact inversion** of `region_partition`'s lesson. `region_partition` proved the tight bounding box is the **exact arbiter** of containment, a per-object **geometric** fact read pixel-for-pixel; this partition reads each object's side from the center of the **group** it belongs to, a **connectivity** fact, deliberately **coarser** -- so **a line never splits a connected group**. Two facts on metal say it. **The group is never split:** a connected group whose member P1's own box center sits **above** the line (its own cross negative, left) and P2's own box center **below** it (right) classifies **both** members to the group center's side, the directed line passing geometrically between the members' own centers while the group stays whole -- the group-fact-not-pixel-fact lesson at the whole-scene partition, where `region_partition` would clip an object its rectangle straddles. **The on set is `click_side`'s zero set lifted:** an object is `on` exactly where `click_side.which_side` reads `on` for a representative tap (cross-checked object by object), and the two anchor groups are always `on` (a group center on the line it defines). `left + on + right` always sums to the component count, and reversing the anchors to B toward A flips the sign of every cross, so the **left** and **right** sets **swap** while **on** stays fixed -- the whole-scene analog of `which_side`'s antisymmetry. Two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup` by name, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the whole-scene side partition; `click_side` owns the `Side` enum and the single-query which-side cross-check, `overlap_clusters` owns `cluster_of`, `cluster_facts` owns `cluster_bbox`, `click_order` owns `click_group_id`, `click_collinear` owns `click_group_center_doubled` -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Line summary (read a directed line as a small digest table)** -- `line_summary` (`line_summary`, `heavier_side`, `LineSummary`, `LineSummaryError`), the **digest** companion of `line_partition`'s three-side split and the **line** twin of `region_summary`: where the partition reads the three side **sizes**, this rung reads the three side **facts** -- for a directed line named by two anchor taps it walks the manifest once, classifies each object by `line_partition`'s exact side arbiter, and folds each object's whole-scene **area** and **hole count** into its side (left, on, right), then `heavier_side` names which open half-plane carries more foreground. It carries the **exact inversion** of `region_summary`'s lesson. `region_summary` proved a rectangle's `clipped_area` **over-counts**, because a rectangle splits an object's **pixels** -- a clipped object holds some foreground outside the box, so its whole area exceeds the pixels the marquee covers. A line is the opposite in kind: a line splits whole **groups**, never pixels (`line_partition`'s lesson), so **every** object lands wholly on one side and contributes its **whole** area there. No side holds a fraction of an object, so **every per-side area is exact** -- there is no clipped class to over-count, and `total_area` (left plus on plus right) equals the whole scene's foreground **exactly**, for every line. Two facts on metal say it. **The straddling group's area stays whole on one side:** over `line_partition`'s own scene (U above area 4, a straddling group P1 area 5 + P2 area 13, anchors A and B on, N on, W a ring below area 8 holed) the horizontal line reads left `4`, on `12`, right `26`, and though P1's own box center sits **above** the line its group lands **right**, so `right_area` carries both P1's and P2's areas (`5 + 13`) and `left_area` carries none of them -- an own-center digest would bleed P1's 5 to the left (left 9, right 21), yet the group keeps them together (left 4, right 26). **Every per-side area is exact:** a full pixel scan tallying each foreground pixel to its object's group side finds `left_area` **4**, `on_area` **12**, and `right_area` **26** exactly, the left region holding exactly U's pixels and not one pixel of the straddling group. `heavier_side` names the right half-plane the heavier and refuses `Balanced` on an equal-area tie; reversing the anchors swaps the two sides while the on facts stay fixed; two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup`, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the per-side fold of area and holes and the `heavier_side` balance; `line_partition` owns the exact side arbiter `object_side` and its refusals, `click_side` owns the `Side` enum, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object area and holes -- a **reading over two readings**.
- **Line order (read a directed line as a reading order along its own direction)** -- `line_order` (`line_order`, `project_label`, `reads_before`, `along_tie`, `rank_of`, `first_along`, `last_along`, `LineOrderError`), the **orthogonal twin** of `line_partition`: where the partition reads the **sign** of the cross product `(B - A) x (C - A)` -- the position **perpendicular** to the line, which side each object falls on -- this rung reads the **dot** product `(B - A) . (C - A)` of the same two anchors, the position **parallel** to the line, how far **forward** each object's group center projects along it, and ranks the whole scene front-to-back by that projection. It carries the **exact integers** the family keeps: scaling the dot product by the line's length is constant per line, so the raw dot ranks objects with no division and no float. The decisive crux -- along-line **order** and across-line **side** are **orthogonal**, the dot and the cross of one directed line. Two facts on metal say it. **Two objects tie in order yet split to opposite sides:** over a horizontal line `A -> B` object U (above) and object D (below) share the column, so `along_tie(U, D)` is **true** -- one along-line depth -- yet `line_partition` places U **left** and D **right**: identical rank-key, opposite sides, the label tie-break settling the total order (U before D) while `along_tie` names the projection itself as tied. **The order reads the drawn direction, and the tie moves with it:** the **same** six objects under the vertical line `U -> D` reorder to the y-order of the centers, and now the former anchors A and B tie (same row) while U and D no longer do -- a `line_order` is not a fixed axis read, it **generalizes** `reading_order`'s top-to-bottom sweep to **any** direction a keeper draws, every tie a property of the line and not the scene. Reversing the anchors to `B -> A` negates every projection so the whole order reverses, `first_along` and `last_along` name the two ends, `rank_of` a label's 0-based place; two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup`, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds`, a label past the object count refuses `BadTarget` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the along-line projection and the sort it induces; `line_partition` owns the cross-product side arbiter the crux cross-checks against, `click_order` owns `click_group_id`, `click_collinear` owns `click_group_center_doubled`, `cluster_facts` owns the group frame whose doubled midpoint is the exact center -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Line distance (read a directed line by how far each object sits from it)** -- `line_distance` (`line_distance`, `distance_of`, `closer_to_line`, `same_distance`, `on_line`, `rank_by_distance`, `nearest_to_line`, `farthest_from_line`, `LineDistanceError`), the rung that **closes the three-read decomposition** of one directed line over the same two anchors: `line_partition` reads the cross product's **sign** (which side, perpendicular direction), `line_order` reads the **dot** (how far along, parallel position), and this rung reads the cross product's **magnitude** `|(B - A) x (C - A)|` (how far **off** the line, perpendicular distance). Side is the sign, along is the dot, distance is the magnitude -- the complete reading, and this is the third and last. It keeps the family's **exact integers**: the true perpendicular distance divides the magnitude by the line's length, and that length is constant per line, so the raw magnitude ranks objects with no square root and no divide. The decisive crux -- distance and side are the **magnitude** and the **sign** of the **same** cross product, so a `line_distance` is the exact **dual** of a `line_order`. Three facts on metal say it. **Two objects are equidistant yet split to opposite sides:** over `line_order`'s own six-object scene the horizontal line `A -> B` gives distances A `0`, B `0`, P `168`, Q `168`, D `224`, U `280`, so `same_distance(P, Q)` is **true** (168 each) yet `line_partition` places P **left** and Q **right** -- equal magnitude, opposite sign, the label tie-break settling the order (P before Q) while `same_distance` names the distance itself as tied. And the cruxes **trade partners** over one scene -- U and D, along-**tied** in `line_order` (one column), **differ** in distance (280 vs 224); P and Q, distance-**tied** here, **differ** in along-line order. **Distance is blind to the drawn direction:** reversing to `B -> A` negates the cross so `line_partition`'s sides swap and `line_order` reverses, yet the magnitude stands still and the distance order is **identical** -- of the three reads, distance is the one that does not care which end a keeper drew from. **The equidistance is a property of the line, not the scene:** drawn vertically (`U -> D`) P and Q fall at different distances and the former anchors A and B stand **off** the line, `on_line` reading zero only for the line's own anchors -- exactly `line_partition`'s `.on` class read as a distance of zero. `nearest_to_line` and `farthest_from_line` name the closest and farthest, `rank_by_distance` a label's 0-based place; two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup`, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds`, a label past the object count refuses `BadTarget` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the distance magnitude and the sort it induces; `line_partition` owns the cross-product side arbiter the crux cross-checks against, `line_order` owns the along-line order the dual trades partners with, `click_collinear` owns `click_group_center_doubled`, `cluster_facts` owns the group frame whose doubled midpoint is the exact center -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Line band (a directed line becomes a corridor marquee)** -- `line_band` (`line_band`, `in_band`, `band_count`, `nearest_outside_band`, `band_max_half_width`, `LineBandError`), the rung that **turns the three-read decomposition into a gesture**: `line_partition` reads the cross product's **sign** (which side), `line_order` reads the **dot** (how far along), `line_distance` reads the **magnitude** (how far off), and this rung reads that magnitude against a **threshold** -- the answer to *what the distance is for*. A keeper drops a line and lassoes a corridor of chosen half-width around it, and `line_band` returns every object whose group center sits inside, nearest-first. It keeps the family's **exact integers**: in doubled coordinates the perpendicular distance is `key / (2 * line_length)`, so `distance <= half_width` squares both sides into `key * key <= 4 * half_width^2 * len2`, a sharp closed threshold with **no square root and no divide**. The decisive crux -- a corridor is a **sublevel set** of the sorted distance key, so **`line_band` is a prefix** of `line_distance`'s nearest-to-farthest order. Three facts on metal say it. **The threshold is closed and exact:** over `line_distance`'s own six-object scene the horizontal line `A -> B` gives distances A `0`, B `0`, P `3`, Q `3`, D `4`, U `5`, so a corridor of half-width 3 selects exactly `{A, B, P, Q}`, the prefix of the order, with P at distance **exactly** 3 held at width 3 and dropped at width 2 -- the boundary a sharp integer proven by an object standing on it, no float fuzz. **The band nests:** because membership is a threshold on one ascending scalar, a wider corridor holds every object a narrower one held, `band_count` reading 2, 4, 5, 6 across widths 0, 3, 4, 5, and width 0 collapsing the corridor to the **line itself** (`line_band(0)` equal to `line_distance`'s `on_line` set). **The band is blind to the drawn direction:** reversing to `B -> A` selects the identical corridor though `line_partition` swaps the sides of the very same objects -- a side has a handedness, a corridor has none. `nearest_outside_band` names the closest object the corridor missed (the one a wider band catches next) and refuses `BandCatchesAll` when the corridor holds the whole scene; two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup`, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds`, a label past the object count refuses `BadTarget`, a half-width past `band_max_half_width` refuses `BandTooWide` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the threshold and the prefix it induces; `line_distance` owns the distance magnitude, the order, and the on-line floor the corridor is built from, `line_partition` owns the side arbiter the direction-blind crux cross-checks, `click_collinear` owns `click_group_center_doubled` -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Line span (a directed line read as a finite stroke, not an infinite line)** -- `line_span` (`span_of`, `on_segment`, `before_segment`, `on_segment_objects`, `beyond_segment`, `span_counts`, `is_in`, `SpanClass`, `SpanCounts`, `LineSpanError`), the **bounded twin** of `line_order` that corrects a latent over-reach in the whole line family: `line_partition`, `line_order`, `line_distance`, and `line_band` all read the two anchors as an **infinite** line, yet a keeper draws a **finite** stroke. `line_span` clamps `line_order`'s unbounded projection to the segment's own two integer thresholds -- the A end at projection `0`, the B end at `len2 = (B - A) . (B - A)` -- so the same exact dot that ranked the objects now splits every one into **before_a**, **on_span**, or **beyond_b** by the closed test `0 <= proj <= len2`, with **no float and no tolerance**. The decisive crux -- **the stroke is bounded where the order is unbounded**. Three facts on metal say it. **The off-the-ends objects rank like any other:** over a horizontal segment `A -> B`, object X (left of A) reads `line_order` rank **0** (frontmost) and object Y (right of B) reads rank **5** (backmost) on the infinite line, yet `line_span` classes X `before_a` and Y `beyond_b` -- both off the drawn stroke -- and the `on_span` set `{A, N, M, B}` is exactly the **contiguous middle** of the order `X, A, N, M, B, Y` from the A anchor's rank to the B anchor's rank inclusive, the stroke a window cut out of the unbounded order. **The span is orthogonal to the side and the off-line distance:** X sits exactly **on** the infinite line (`line_partition` reads it `on`, `line_distance` reads it `0`) yet **before** the stroke, while N sits **above** the line (`line_partition` reads it `left`, off-line distance nonzero) yet **on** the stroke -- on the line off the stroke, off the line on the stroke, the along-segment read caring only about the parallel coordinate the side and the distance are blind to. **Reversal swaps the ends and fixes the middle:** reading the same segment `B -> A` reflects every projection (`proj' = len2 - proj`), so `before_a` and `beyond_b` **trade places** while `on_span` is **invariant** -- where `line_partition`'s reversal swaps the two sides and `line_order`'s flips the whole order, the segment's middle class stands still, because a segment is one set of points read from either end. `span_counts` sums to the component count; two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup`, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds`, a label past the object count refuses `BadTarget` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the along-segment classification and the two thresholds it clamps to; `line_order` owns the along-line projection every object is cross-checked against, `line_partition` owns the side arbiter and `line_distance` the off-line magnitude the orthogonality crux reads, `click_collinear` owns `click_group_center_doubled` whose squared delta is `len2` -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Line segment distance (the true nearest gap to a finite stroke, not an infinite line)** -- `line_segment_distance` (`segment_key_of`, `agrees_with_line`, `on_stroke`, `nearer_segment`, `same_segment_distance`, `line_segment_distance`, `rank_by_segment_distance`, `nearest_to_segment`, `farthest_from_segment`, `LineSegmentDistanceError`), the **corrected twin** of `line_distance` that closes the gap `line_span` opened: `line_distance` ranks objects by their distance to the **infinite** line (the cross-product magnitude), which **under-counts** once a foot falls past an end, since the nearest point on a **finite** stroke is then the **endpoint**, not the perpendicular foot. This rung **fuses** `line_distance`'s off-line cross with `line_span`'s along-segment clamp into one exact-integer key -- the true squared distance to the segment, times the constant `len2`, is `cross^2 + overshoot^2`, where `overshoot = proj - clamp(proj, 0, len2)` is how far `line_order`'s projection overshoots the segment's two thresholds. Multiplying by the constant `len2` clears the only divide a finite-segment distance would need, keeping the family's **exact integers** with no square root and no divide. The decisive crux -- **the finite segment separates what the infinite line ties**. Three facts on metal say it. **The infinite line under-counts exactly off the ends:** `segment_key_of` equals `distance_of` **squared** for every object whose foot lands **on** the span, and **strictly exceeds** it off the ends by exactly the overshoot squared -- over a horizontal segment `A -> B`, P (on the span) and Q (beyond the B end) share one cross magnitude so `line_distance` ties them (`same_distance` true, `160` each), yet Q overshoots the B end (`proj` `520`, `len2` `400`) so `nearer_segment(P, Q)` is **true** and `same_segment_distance(P, Q)` **false** (P `160^2 = 25600`, Q `160^2 + 120^2 = 40000`), the drawn length deciding what the line could not; the objects the line under-counts are **exactly** `line_span`'s `before_a` and `beyond_b` sets, `agrees_with_line` reading its `on_span` as a distance fact. **An object on the infinite line can be far from the finite stroke:** X reads `line_distance` **0** (exactly on the line) yet a large segment key `25600`, its nearest stroke point the A endpoint, so it moves from the **front** of the line order (rank 0) to the **middle** of the segment order (rank 4) -- the line order is `X, A, M, B, P, Q` and the segment order `A, M, B, P, X, Q`. **Reversal fixes the gap:** reading the same segment `B -> A` leaves every true distance and the whole nearest-to-farthest order **identical**, a segment being one set of points read from either end -- where `line_order`'s reversal flips the order and `line_partition`'s swaps the sides, the true gap stands perfectly still. `on_stroke` reads the zero-gap objects, `nearest_to_segment` and `farthest_from_segment` the ends of the order; two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup`, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds`, a label past the object count refuses `BadTarget` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the fusion key and the order it induces; `line_distance` owns the off-line cross magnitude every key is cross-checked against, `line_order` owns the along-line projection the overshoot is measured from, `line_span` owns the along-segment class naming which objects the line under-counts, `click_collinear` owns `click_group_center_doubled` whose squared delta is `len2` -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Line project (the exact nearest point on a finite stroke, the foot the family measured to but never named)** -- `line_project` (`foot_of`, `line_foot_of`, `foot_is_whole`, `foot_doubled`, `foot_at_end`, `same_foot`, `foot_gap_scaled`, `feet_scaled`, `FootScaled`, `LineProjectError`), the rung that returns the **point** the whole line family measured **to** yet never named: `line_distance` reads how far **off** the line a center sits, `line_segment_distance` the true gap to the finite stroke, `line_span` which side of the ends the foot falls -- `line_project` returns the **foot itself**, the exact nearest point on the stroke, so a surface can **draw** it. The decisive crux -- **the exact foot is rational**. The perpendicular foot `A + (proj / len2) * (B - A)` is a **lattice point** in doubled coordinates only when `len2` divides `proj * (B - A)` on both axes -- always at the two anchors, only sometimes in the interior -- so `line_project` holds the foot **times `len2`** as an exact `i128` pair, `foot_scaled = A * len2 + clamp(proj, 0, len2) * (B - A)`, the same clear-the-divide move `line_segment_distance` made, keeping the family's **exact integers** with no float and no square root. Three facts on metal say it. **The foot is whole only at lattice points:** over a **diagonal** segment `A -> B` (`len2 = 720`), object M sits **off** the line (its center is not on the stroke) yet its exact foot is the lattice point `(17,13)` that `foot_doubled` names, while object F on the span has a genuinely **fractional** foot (scaled `(12816, 9648)`, which `720` divides on neither axis), so `foot_doubled` refuses `FootNotWhole` rather than rounding a lie -- the honest "no integer foot exists" over the rounded guess. **The scaled foot satisfies the exact distance identity:** `foot_gap_scaled` -- the squared gap from the center to its scaled foot -- equals `segment_key_of` **times** `len2` for every object, an integer identity with no divide (F: `36864 * 720 = 26542080`), so the foot returned is **provably** the nearest point `line_segment_distance` measured its gap to. **The segment foot differs from the infinite-line foot exactly off the ends:** for M on the span the clamp does nothing (segment foot equals the unclamped line foot, `foot_at_end` **false**), for X before the A end the clamp pins the segment foot to the **A endpoint** while the infinite-line foot runs past (`foot_at_end` **true**) -- exactly `line_span`'s "not `on_span`" read as a foot fact. And **two objects can share one foot:** X and Z, both before the A end at different perpendicular distances, fold onto the **same** A endpoint (`same_foot` true), Y sharing the B endpoint with B itself -- the drawn stroke folds a whole half-plane of centers onto one point. `feet_scaled` reads the whole scene's feet at once; reading the same segment `B -> A` leaves every foot **identical**, a stroke being one set of points; two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup`, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds`, a label past the object count refuses `BadTarget`, a fractional foot asked as a lattice point refuses `FootNotWhole` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the foot point and the lattice test on it; `line_order` owns the projection the foot is placed along, `line_span` owns the along-segment class naming when the clamp fires, `line_segment_distance` owns the fused gap key the distance identity cross-checks, `click_collinear` owns `click_group_center_doubled` whose squared delta is `len2` -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Line corridor summary (a directed line read as a distribution over half-widths)** -- `line_corridor_summary` (`corridor_histogram`, `CorridorHistogram`, `LineCorridorSummaryError`), the **`line_band` twin** of `cluster_graph_profile`: where `line_summary` folds the scene by **handedness** (left, on, right) and `line_band` selects one corridor of a chosen half-width, this rung folds the whole scene by **radius** -- one bin per distinct distance key, each carrying its object **count**, its whole **area**, and its **holed-object** count, the distance distribution off the drawn line. The decisive crux -- **cumulating the histogram reproduces `line_band.band_count` by two independent paths**, so the distribution is a step function whose jumps are the bin multiplicities (over a scene where P and Q share a count-2 bin at key `168`, the cumulative curve steps by two there and the two paths agree bin for bin), the exact-integer proof that a corridor is a prefix of the sorted distance and a distribution is that prefix read all at once. Two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup`, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the radius fold; `line_distance` owns the squared distance key each bin is keyed by, `line_band` owns the corridor prefix the cumulative reproduces, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object area and holes -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Line pierce (a directed line read as a box crossing, the object's extent not its center)** -- `line_pierce` (`pierces`, `line_pierces`, `pierce_only_infinite`, `pierce_flags`, `pierce_count`, `pierced_labels`, `LinePierceError`), the rung that reads the object's **actual box** where every earlier line read approximated it by its **group center**: `line_distance` reads how far off the line a center sits, `line_project` the exact foot, `line_band` a corridor of half-width the center may fall inside -- `line_pierce` asks whether the **drawn stroke crosses this thing's box**, the read a stroke-selection surface wants the day a keeper drags a line across the scene and means every thing it truly grazed, not every thing whose midpoint sat within some radius. The decisive crux -- **a segment-versus-box crossing is exact with integers by the separating axis theorem in two dimensions**: a finite segment and an axis-aligned box are separated exactly on one of three axes, the box's two face normals (the segment's own bounding box failing to overlap the box range) and the segment's own perpendicular (all four corner **cross products** `(B - A) x (corner - A)` sharing one strict sign), so `pierces` is bounding-box overlap **and** straddle, `line_pierces` drops the segment bound and keeps only the straddle. Three facts on metal say it. **The object's box, not its center:** over a `26x10` scene of six singletons the horizontal stroke `A -> B` along doubled-y `13` pierces A, P, B and misses H, D above it, the anchors pierced **because** a group's box holds its own center -- `line_distance` equal to zero (a center exactly on the line: A, P, B, E all on doubled-y `13`) forces `line_pierces` **true**. **The finite stroke differs from the infinite line exactly beyond the ends:** E beyond the B end is `line_pierces` true yet `pierces` false, `pierce_only_infinite` naming exactly that gap -- the box twin of `line_project`'s foot clamping to an endpoint. **Bounding-box overlap alone is not enough -- the straddle does real work:** read against a **diagonal** stroke `A -> D`, P's box overlaps the segment bounding box on both axes yet all four corner crosses share one sign, so P sits entirely to one side and is **not** pierced, the straddle separating a true crossing from a mere shared box. `pierce_flags` reads the whole scene's crossings at once, `pierce_count` folds them (3: A, P, B), `pierced_labels` lists them ascending; reversing to `B -> A` pierces the identical objects, a stroke being one set of points; two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup`, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds`, a label past the object count refuses `BadTarget` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the box-crossing test over three separating axes; `scene_manifest` owns the per-object box the stroke is tested against, `click_collinear` owns `click_group_center_doubled` naming the stroke, `line_distance` owns the off-line magnitude the zero-distance cross-check leans on, `line_span` owns the along-segment class the anchors read `on_span` -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Line cross (two directed lines read for how they relate)** -- `line_cross` (`directions_parallel`, `lines_cross`, `segments_cross`, `cross_only_infinite`, `cross_point_scaled`, `cross_is_lattice`, `cross_point_doubled`, `CrossScaled`, `CrossPoint`, `LineCrossError`), the rung that **lifts the whole family from one line and its objects to two lines and each other**: every earlier line read measured a scene of objects against one directed stroke -- `line_distance` how far off it a center sits, `line_project` the exact foot, `line_pierce` whether the stroke crosses an object's box -- and `line_cross` reads how a **second** stroke relates to the first, the answer a surface wants the day a keeper draws two lines and means their intersection. The decisive crux -- **a segment-versus-segment crossing is exact with integers, and the meeting point of two lines is rational, held exact without a divide**: the two finite strokes cross exactly when the four orientations `d1 = orient(C,D,A)`, `d2 = orient(C,D,B)`, `d3 = orient(A,B,C)`, `d4 = orient(A,B,D)` split into two opposite-sign pairs (a collinear endpoint touch caught by the on-segment bounding-box test), and the infinite lines meet at `A + (t_num / den) * (B - A)` where `den` is the cross of the two directions, held **times `den`** as an exact `i128` pair -- the same clear-the-divide move `line_project` made for its foot. Three facts on metal say it. **Two finite strokes can miss where their infinite lines meet:** over a `30x26` scene a horizontal stroke `A -> B` crosses a vertical stroke `C -> D` at the lattice point `(25,21)` doubled, yet the vertical stroke `C -> F` stopped short above the horizontal one has `segments_cross` **false** while `lines_cross` is **true**, `cross_only_infinite` naming exactly that pair -- and `cross_point_doubled` still returns `(25,21)`, the point the infinite lines meet at, computed off the lines whether or not the drawn strokes reach it, the two-stroke twin of `line_pierce`'s `pierce_only_infinite`. **The meeting point is rational, held exact:** over a `14x14` scene two diagonal strokes cross at `(31/3, 47/3)` doubled, so `cross_point_scaled` holds `(3968, 6016, 384)` with `384` dividing neither numerator, `cross_is_lattice` reads **false**, and `cross_point_doubled` refuses `CrossNotWhole` rather than rounding a lie -- the scaled point provably lies on **both** lines, the cross product of each direction with `(point - anchor)` exactly zero in scaled terms. **Parallel lines never meet at a point:** a second horizontal stroke `G -> H` sharing `A -> B`'s direction reads `directions_parallel` **true**, `lines_cross` **false**, and `cross_point_scaled` refuses `Parallel`. Reading either stroke from its other end leaves the crossing verdict and the meeting point identical, a stroke being one set of points; two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup`, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the two-stroke crossing test over four orientations and the rational meeting point; `click_order` owns the group a tap lands in, `click_collinear` owns `click_group_center_doubled` naming each stroke, `line_pierce` owns the finite-versus-infinite reading this lifts from one stroke to two -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Line slice (one directed line read as an ordered cut across the scene)** -- `line_slice` (`line_slice`, `slice_count`, `is_sliced`, `slice_rank`, `first_sliced`, `last_sliced`, `slice_reads_before`, `LineSliceError`), the rung that reads a **stroke as a cut**: the ordered sequence of objects the finite stroke truly pierces, front-to-back along the drawn direction, the read a surface wants the day a keeper drags a cutting line across a scene and means every thing it sliced, in the order the stroke reached them. The decisive crux -- **the slice is a filter of the order, not a new geometry**: `line_pierce` answers whether the stroke crosses each object's box yet returns an unordered ascending-label set, `line_order` ranks every object along the stroke yet includes the objects the stroke misses, and `line_slice` takes `line_pierce`'s `pierced_labels` and re-sorts them by `line_order`'s `project_label` with the **same** `(projection, label)` tie-break -- so it is provably the **subsequence of `line_order` restricted to the pierced set**. Three facts on metal say it. **The slice is the order with the misses removed:** over a `30x14` scene whose full along-line order is `[A, P, U, Q, B, Z]`, the finite stroke `A -> B` slices exactly `[A, P, Q, B]`, and walking `line_order` while keeping only the pierced labels reproduces the slice label for label. **Two objects leave the order for two different reasons:** U sits **off** the stroke's line entirely (`line_pierces` false, its box never straddling the line) while Z sits **on** the line's order yet **beyond** the finite stroke's far end (`line_pierces` true, `pierce_only_infinite` true) -- both present in `line_order` and absent from `line_slice`, one dropped for being off the line, the other for being past the end, and asking the slice rank of a missed object refuses `NotPierced`. **The cut reverses with the stroke, the selection does not:** reading `B -> A` reverses the slice to `[B, Q, P, A]` exactly, since a box crossing is blind to the drawn direction while the along-line order flips, so `first_sliced` and `last_sliced` (the object the cut enters first and the one it leaves last) trade places while the sliced set stays invariant; two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup`, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds`, a label past the object count refuses `BadTarget`, the entry or exit of an empty cut refuses `SliceEmpty` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the ordered filter -- taking the pierced labels and sorting them by the along-line key; `line_pierce` owns `pierced_labels` and the finite crossing, `line_order` owns `project_label` and the along-line projection -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Line raycast (one directed line read as a ray cast: the true entry order into each box)** -- `line_raycast` (`line_raycast`, `entry_param`, `hit_count`, `hit_rank`, `first_hit`, `last_hit`, `hit_reads_before`, `LineRaycastError`), the rung that reads a stroke the way a drag physically enters things: the objects the finite stroke pierces, ordered by the exact parameter at which the stroke crosses into each object's **own box** -- the nearest crossing leading. The decisive crux -- **the box you enter first is not the one the cluster orders first, and the entry parameter is exact by the slab method**: `line_slice` orders the same pierced set by each object's **cluster** center, so two objects whose boxes overlap into one cluster share a projection and fall back to label order, while `line_raycast` orders by the **near face** the stroke reaches first, the slab-method parameter `t_enter = max(t_x_lo, t_y_lo)` held as an exact rational `(num, den)` with every compare a cross-multiply, no float and no rounding. Three facts on metal say it. **The box you enter first is not the one the cluster orders first:** over a `28x14` scene where a narrow block Q is nested in the x-span of a wide L shape P (their boxes overlapping into one cluster), the horizontal stroke `A -> B` casts `[A, P, Q, B]` -- P leading because the stroke reaches its left face first -- while `line_slice` orders the same set `[A, Q, P, B]`, tying P and Q on their shared cluster center and falling back to ascending label where Q rasterized first; P and Q trade places at ranks 1 and 2, the near face read on each own box seeing what the cluster read is blind to. **The entry parameter is exact and rational:** `entry_param` of the start anchor A is **negative** (the stroke begins inside its own anchor box, entered before `t = 0`), the entries strictly ascend along the cast (`-1/44, 7/44, 21/44, 43/44` over `dx = 44`), and along an oblique stroke `A -> D` the entry of A is the **later** of its two axis entries, `max(-1/18, -1/14) = -1/18`, the x face winning the max exactly. **The ray cast is direction-truthful, not a mirror:** `line_slice` reverses cleanly under `B -> A` because a center projection negates, yet `line_raycast` casts `[B, P, Q, A]`, **not** the reverse `[B, Q, P, A]` -- the narrow Q nested inside the wide P is entered **after** P from both ends, since entry depends on the near face and the near-face order is not the mirror of the far-face order; the pierced set stays invariant while the order stays the honest one the drawn direction produces. Asking the entry or rank of a missed object refuses `NotHit`, two anchors sharing a group refuse `SameGroup`, a background tap refuses `EmptyClick`, a point off the map refuses `PointOutOfBounds`, a label past the object count refuses `BadTarget`, an empty cast refuses `HitEmpty` having read nothing. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the entry-parameter geometry and the rational order over it; `line_pierce` owns `pierced_labels` and the finite crossing that names the set, `scene_manifest` owns the per-object box, `click_collinear` owns the anchor centers that name the stroke -- a **reading over readings**.
- **Cluster adjacency (how two whole connected clusters sit relative to each other)** -- `cluster_adjacency` (`cluster_distance`, `cluster_frames_overlap`, `nearest_cluster`, `ClusterAdjacencyError`), the **cluster graph above the object graph**: where `object_relation` reads how a pair of objects sit and `scene_graph` reads each object's nearest neighbor, this rung lifts the pair read to the clusters themselves -- the true inter-cluster **distance** (the min exact nearest-pixel Chebyshev gap over every member pair), whether two union **frames** overlap, and each cluster's directed **nearest** other cluster. The decisive fact it makes plain -- the box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted to the **cluster pair**, in two shapes. **Two distinct clusters share no edge yet their frames can overlap:** a corner-L A and a ring B reaching apart connect into cluster 1 (frame `(1,1)-(9,9)`), a lone block C is cluster 2 (frame `(8,1)-(9,2)`) sitting wholly inside cluster 1's frame, and a far block D is cluster 3 (frame `(12,12)-(13,13)`); `cluster_frames_overlap` reads **true** for clusters 1 and 2 though they share no member edge (they are distinct clusters exactly because no box overlaps between them) and stand apart -- frame overlap over-claims a contact the members never make, while the far cluster 3's frame overlaps neither. **The true cluster distance is a real graded gap, floored at 2:** `cluster_distance` grades `2`, `3`, `10` over the three cluster pairs, so a frame overlap never determines the true nearness -- clusters 1 and 2 overlap in frame yet sit exactly `2` pixels apart, the floor for distinct 8-components; the distance is symmetric. And `nearest_cluster` is **directed**, exactly as `scene_graph`'s `nearest_neighbor` -- cluster 3's nearest is cluster 1 (gap 3 under cluster 2's gap 10), yet cluster 1's nearest is cluster 2 (gap 2 under cluster 3's gap 3), **not** cluster 3, so a cluster can be another's nearest without that other being its own; a zero or past-the-count cluster id refuses `BadClusterId`, a cluster against itself refuses `SameCluster`, a lone-cluster scene refuses `NoOtherCluster`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the cluster-pair reads; `overlap_clusters` owns the connectivity and labeling, `cluster_facts` each cluster's union frame, `scene_graph` the precomputed pair matrix, `object_relation` each pair's exact distance -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster graph (the whole cluster-neighbor graph above the cluster pair)** -- `cluster_graph` (`build_cluster_graph`, `cluster_count`, `graph_distance`, `graph_frames_overlap`, `clusters_within`, `cluster_degree_within`, `within_edges`, `frame_overlap_edges`, `ClusterGraph`, `ClusterGraphError`), which is to `cluster_adjacency` exactly what `scene_graph` is to `object_relation`: it **joins** the cluster-pair read over every cluster pair into one matrix, then reads the whole-graph questions no pair can answer -- which other clusters lie **within** a distance threshold (`clusters_within`), the thresholded **degree** of a cluster (`cluster_degree_within`), and the total edge counts of two **different** graphs over the same clusters (`within_edges`, `frame_overlap_edges`). The decisive fact it makes plain -- the box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted to the **whole cluster graph**, in two shapes. **The frame-overlap graph and the true-distance graph are different graphs, neither a subset of the other:** over the same three-cluster scene (corner-L A and ring B into cluster 1, lone block C as cluster 2 with its frame inside cluster 1's, far block D as cluster 3), the frame-overlap graph holds one edge (clusters 1 and 2), yet the true-distance graph at threshold `1` holds **none** (the floor is `2`, so frame overlap over-claims a contact past the threshold) and at threshold `3` holds **two** (clusters 1-2 at gap `2` and clusters 1-3 at gap `3`, an edge with disjoint frames the frame graph misses, so frame overlap under-claims a real nearness) -- neither edge set contains the other, a box comparison over frames only **bounding** the true adjacency graph, never determining it. **Threshold adjacency is a star, never a clique, not transitive:** cluster 1 lies within `3` of both cluster 2 (gap `2`) and cluster 3 (gap `3`), yet clusters 2 and 3 stand `10` apart, so cluster 1 is a hub of **degree 2** while each other is degree 1, and `clusters_within` reads clusters 2 then 3 ascending for the hub yet only cluster 1 for each leaf; a zero or past-the-count cluster id refuses `BadClusterId` across every read, an empty clustering refuses `NoClusters`, a lone-cluster graph has zero edges and an empty neighbor set. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the whole-cluster-graph reads (the matrix join, the threshold membership, the thresholded degree, the two edge counts); `overlap_clusters` owns the connectivity and labeling, `cluster_adjacency` each cluster pair's true gap and frame-overlap test, `scene_graph` the precomputed pair matrix -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster graph profile (the cluster graph as a distribution over thresholds)** -- `cluster_graph_profile` (`pair_total`, `distance_histogram`, `cumulative_edges`, `bin_total`, `min_pair_distance`, `max_pair_distance`, `DistanceBin`, `ClusterGraphProfileError`), which reads the whole `cluster_graph` as a **function of the threshold** rather than at one reach: the distance **histogram** (`distance_histogram`, one bin per distinct true inter-cluster distance carrying its **multiplicity** -- the count of unordered cluster pairs sitting exactly that far apart) and the cumulative **edge profile** it generates (`cumulative_edges`, the pairs within a threshold, read by folding the histogram). The decisive fact it makes plain -- the histogram is the edge-curve's **derivative**, its cumulative sum the **integral**, and the two paths agree exactly. **Cumulating the histogram reproduces `within_edges`, by two independent paths:** over the same three-cluster scene (distances `2`, `3`, `10`, all distinct, three bins of count `1`) `cumulative_edges` -- a distribution fold -- equals `cluster_graph.within_edges` -- a raw-matrix scan -- at **every** threshold, so the edge-count curve is a **step function** that changes only at a pairwise distance and by exactly that distance's multiplicity: `within_edges(d) - within_edges(d - 1)` is precisely the histogram's count at `d`, zero where no pair sits. **The multiplicity a sorted list could not show:** over a symmetric three-block row whose two (center, side) gaps are both `3` while the outer gap is `7`, the histogram holds a bin of **count 2** at distance `3`, and the curve jumps by **two** at threshold `3` -- both pairs crossing at once, a height the raw within-edges count agrees with. **The curve is bounded by the closest gap and the diameter:** `min_pair_distance` is the first bin's distance and the threshold the curve first rises above zero, `max_pair_distance` the last bin's distance and the threshold it saturates at `pair_total`, and the sum of the bin multiplicities (`bin_total`) is exactly `pair_total`, so the fold loses no pair and double-counts none; a lone-cluster graph has no pairs, so its histogram is empty, its `pair_total` zero, and `min_pair_distance` / `max_pair_distance` refuse `NoPairs`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the distribution reads (the histogram fold, the cumulative sum, the closest gap and diameter, the pair total); `cluster_graph` owns the precomputed matrix and the raw edge scan, `cluster_adjacency` each cluster pair's true gap, `overlap_clusters` the connectivity -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster connectivity (when the whole layout becomes one connected region)** -- `cluster_connectivity` (`connectivity_threshold`, `component_count_at`, `is_connected_at`), which reads the one question the edge **count** can never answer: not how many edges a reach holds, but whether those edges **connect** the whole layout, and the smallest reach at which they do. `component_count_at` unions every cluster pair within a reach (a bounded union-find over `cluster_graph`'s matrix) and counts the pieces; `is_connected_at` asks whether that count is one; `connectivity_threshold` sweeps the ascending distinct distances, accreting edges until every cluster shares a root, and returns the distance that closed the last gap -- the **MST bottleneck**. The decisive fact it makes plain -- connectivity is **not** the edge count and **not** the diameter; it needs the graph's **structure**, which pair bridges which. **The connectivity threshold is the MST bottleneck, below the diameter when a hub bridges:** over the three-cluster scene (distances `2`, `3`, `10`) the layout becomes one connected region at reach `3` -- the **median** edge, not the diameter `10` -- because cluster 1 is the hub clusters 2 and 3 reach through, so their direct edge of `10` is never needed; the component count falls `3`, `2`, `1` as reach passes `2` then `3`, `is_connected_at` flips exactly once at `3`, and the threshold sits **strictly between** the closest gap `2` and the diameter `10`, a genuinely new quantity distinct from both (exactly the star-not-clique lesson read as a single reach). **More edges is not more connected:** over a four-cluster scene -- a tight triangle of three clusters all mutually `3` apart, plus a far outlier `9` away -- the edge count at reach `3` is already **three** (the whole triangle wired), yet the layout is still **two** components (the triangle and the lone outlier), so `within_edges` reads three while `component_count_at` reads two; the count curve rises while connectivity waits on the single bridge to the outlier, so the connectivity threshold (`9`, the outlier's gap and the diameter here) is decided by the one far edge, not the many near ones -- a box comparison, an edge count, a distance histogram, none decides connectivity, only the union of the real edges does. A lone-cluster layout is already one whole: zero threshold, one component, connected at every reach. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the connectivity reads (the union-find over thresholded edges, the component count, the bottleneck sweep); `cluster_graph` owns the precomputed matrix, `cluster_graph_profile` the ascending distinct distances, `overlap_clusters` the object connectivity a level below -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster minimum spanning tree (the skeleton that holds a layout together)** -- `cluster_minimum_spanning_tree` (`build_mst`, `mst_weight`, `mst_bottleneck`, `edge_count`, `is_bridge`, `MstTree`, `MstEdge`, `MstError`), which reads the tree `cluster_connectivity` named only the **value** of: where `connectivity_threshold` returns the single number of the **MST bottleneck**, this rung reads the minimum spanning tree **itself** -- the actual **bridge edges** (which cluster pair bridges which), so a surface can **draw** the skeleton, not only know at what reach it closes. `build_mst` grows the tree by **Prim** over `cluster_graph`'s precomputed matrix (start from cluster 1, repeatedly attach the nearest not-yet-in-tree cluster, ties to the lowest id for determinism), `mst_weight` sums its bridge gaps, `mst_bottleneck` reads its largest edge, `edge_count` names its size (`clusters - 1`), `is_bridge` asks whether a cluster pair is one of the skeleton's edges. The decisive crux, in two shapes. **The MST is the skeleton, its bottleneck the connectivity threshold:** over the three-cluster scene (distances `2`, `3`, `10`) the tree picks edges `1-2` (gap `2`) and `1-3` (gap `3`) and **never** the diameter edge `2-3` (gap `10`), a **star** on the hub cluster 1, its bottleneck (`3`) **equal to** `cluster_connectivity`'s connectivity threshold -- yet the tree also names **which** two edges close the layout where the threshold named only the value; its total weight `5` is far below the sum of all three pairwise gaps (`15`), the diameter edge never spent because the hub bridges the far pair for less. **The tree drops the redundant cycle edge, fewer edges than the thresholded graph:** over a four-cluster scene -- a tight triangle of three clusters all mutually `3` apart, plus a far outlier `9` away -- the thresholded graph at reach `3` holds **three** edges (the whole triangle, a cycle), yet the MST over four clusters holds exactly `n - 1 = 3` edges (two triangle edges plus the one bridge to the outlier), the third triangle edge `2-3` **dropped** though its gap (`3`) is well within the tree's bottleneck (`9`) and it sits in the thresholded graph -- because a spanning tree carries no cycle, so `is_bridge(2, 3)` reads **false** for an edge the connectivity read would have counted; the bottleneck (`9`) still equals the connectivity threshold, the skeleton and the threshold meeting at the largest edge and parting on every other. A lone-cluster layout is already one whole: an empty tree, zero weight and bottleneck, matching the threshold; a zero or past-the-count id refuses `BadClusterId`, a self pair `SameCluster`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the spanning-tree reads (Prim's growth, the weight sum, the bottleneck, the bridge test); `cluster_graph` owns the precomputed matrix through `graph_distance`, `cluster_connectivity` owns the connectivity threshold cross-checked here and never re-derived -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster tree diameter (the longest reach across the skeleton)** -- `cluster_tree_diameter` (`tree_diameter`, `diameter_endpoints`, `tree_eccentricity`, `is_diameter_endpoint`, `DiameterPath`, `DiameterError`), which reads the one whole-tree number `cluster_minimum_spanning_tree` never named: where `mst_weight` sums every bridge edge and `mst_bottleneck` reads the largest single one, this rung reads the tree's **diameter** -- the greatest weight of any unique path between two clusters through the bridge edges, the longest walk along the skeleton -- so a surface can draw the **span** of a connected group, or place the two clusters that bound its reach. `tree_diameter` searches the longest path (a breadth-first walk over the tree's own edges from each cluster), `diameter_endpoints` names the two poles and the weight between them, `tree_eccentricity` reads one cluster's farthest reach, `is_diameter_endpoint` asks whether a cluster is a pole. The decisive crux -- the diameter is a **third** tree invariant, distinct from the bottleneck and the weight, coinciding with the weight only when the tree is a single path. **The diameter equals the weight yet exceeds the bottleneck:** over the three-cluster star (edges `1-2` gap `2`, `1-3` gap `3`) the longest path runs `2 -> 1 -> 3`, weight `5`, spending **both** edges, so the diameter (`5`) **equals** the tree's whole weight (`5`) -- a two-edge tree is a single path and every edge lies on it -- yet strictly **exceeds** the bottleneck (`3`), the longest reach spanning two bridges; the endpoints are the **leaves** (clusters 2 and 3), never the hub, and the hub cluster 1 holds the **smallest** eccentricity (`3`), the **center** the whole layout reaches through. **The diameter sits strictly between the bottleneck and the weight when a third arm branches off:** over the four-cluster star (edges `1-2` gap `3`, `1-3` gap `3`, `1-4` gap `9`) the longest path runs `2 -> 1 -> 4`, weight `12`, spending the `9`-arm and one `3`-arm while the other `3`-arm branches off the path unwalked, so the diameter (`12`) is strictly **less** than the weight (`15`), the third arm lost, and strictly **more** than the bottleneck (`9`), the two longest arms summed -- three distinct numbers, `9 < 12 < 15`; the far outlier cluster 4 is a pole, the hub cluster 1 still the center. A lone-cluster layout is already one whole: an empty tree, zero diameter and eccentricity, no poles (`diameter_endpoints` refuses `EmptyTree`); a zero or past-the-count id refuses `BadClusterId`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the diameter and eccentricity reads (the breadth-first walk, the longest-path search, the endpoint test); `cluster_minimum_spanning_tree` owns `build_mst` and the tree's weight and bottleneck cross-checked here and never re-derived, `cluster_graph` owns the matrix a level below -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster tree radius (the center of the skeleton and the reach from it)** -- `cluster_tree_radius` (`tree_radius`, `tree_center`, `is_center`, `RadiusError`), which reads the same eccentricity vector `cluster_tree_diameter` reads from its **other** end: where the diameter is the **greatest** farthest-reach of any cluster (its two poles the leaves), this rung reads its **minimum**, the **radius**, and the cluster that holds it, the **center** -- so a surface can hang a connected group's **handle** from the cluster nearest to all of them at once, not a pole of its span. `tree_radius` reads the whole-tree smallest eccentricity, `tree_center` names the cluster reaching every other for the least (the lowest id on a tie), `is_center` asks whether a cluster is a center. The decisive crux -- the radius and the diameter are **one eccentricity vector read from its two ends**, the center the hub where the poles are the leaves. **The radius meets the bottleneck while the diameter exceeds it:** over the three-cluster star (edges `1-2` gap `2`, `1-3` gap `3`) the hub's eccentricity is its single longest bridge (`3`), the smallest, so the radius (`3`) **equals** the tree's bottleneck (`3`) while the diameter (`5`) strictly exceeds it; the center is the hub cluster 1, exactly the cluster the diameter read proves is **never** a pole -- `is_center(1)` reads **true** while `is_diameter_endpoint(1)` reads **false** -- and the vector is bounded on both sides, radius (`3`) <= diameter (`5`) <= twice the radius (`6`). **The center is invariant yet the radius climbs when a far member joins:** over the four-cluster star (arms `3`, `3`, `9`) the center stays the hub cluster 1 while the radius climbs from `3` to `9`, the hub's new longest arm meeting the new bottleneck -- a distant member lifts the radius without moving the center. **The center is a middle id, not the lowest; a symmetric path meets the upper bound:** over a three-cluster symmetric **path** (chain `1-2-3`, edges both gap `3`) the center is the **middle** cluster 2, not cluster 1, proving the center is genuinely found and never assumed the lowest id, and the diameter (`6`) **equals** twice the radius (`6`), the tight bound met exactly where a star falls strictly short (`5 < 6`, `12 < 18`). A lone-cluster layout is already one whole: zero radius, its own center (`tree_center` reads cluster 1); a zero or past-the-count id refuses `BadClusterId`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the radius and center reads (the minimum over the eccentricities, the argmin, the center test); `cluster_tree_diameter` owns `tree_eccentricity`, `tree_diameter`, and `is_diameter_endpoint` cross-checked here and never re-derived, `cluster_minimum_spanning_tree` owns `build_mst` and the bottleneck, `cluster_graph` owns the matrix a level below -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster diameter path (the actual edge list the diameter walks)** -- `cluster_diameter_path` (`diameter_path`, `diameter_path_hops`, `is_on_diameter_path`, `DiameterWalk`, `PathError`), which reads the edge sequence `cluster_tree_diameter` never named: where the diameter gave only the **weight** of the longest reach and its two **poles**, this rung reconstructs the **whole walk** between them -- the clusters visited in order and the exact **bridge edges** crossed in order -- so a surface can **draw** the chain of links that carries a connected group's span, or step a viewer along it link by link, not only weigh it. `diameter_path` runs a parent-recording breadth-first walk from pole to pole, `diameter_path_hops` reads how many edges the walk spends (distinct from its weight), `is_on_diameter_path` asks whether a cluster pair is one of the crossed edges. The decisive crux -- the diameter path is a **subset** of the tree's bridge edges, equal to the whole tree only when the tree is a single path. **Every tree edge is on the path when the tree is a single path:** over the three-cluster star (edges `1-2` gap `2`, `1-3` gap `3`) the walk runs `2 -> 1 -> 3` crossing **both** bridges, the path holds two edges (exactly the tree's whole edge count) whose weights sum to `5`, the diameter itself, `is_on_diameter_path` **true** for both `1-2` and `1-3` and `is_bridge` agrees -- a star of two edges is also a path, so the path **is** the tree. **The path drops the branching arm, fewer edges than the tree when a third arm branches off:** over the four-cluster star (edges `1-2` gap `3`, `1-3` gap `3`, `1-4` gap `9`) the walk runs `2 -> 1 -> 4` crossing the `9`-arm and one `3`-arm (two edges summing to `12`, the diameter), yet the tree holds **three** edges -- the third arm `1-3` branches off the walk and is never crossed, so `is_on_diameter_path(1, 3)` reads **false** for an edge `is_bridge(1, 3)` reads **true**, the path a **strict subset** of the tree's bridges where the diameter's single number `12` could never say which two. A lone-cluster layout is already one whole: an empty tree, no two poles, `diameter_path` and `diameter_path_hops` refusing `EmptyTree`; a zero or past-the-count id refuses `BadClusterId`, a self pair `SameCluster`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the path reconstruction (the parent-recording walk, the ordered node and edge sequence, the on-path test); `cluster_tree_diameter` owns `diameter_endpoints` and `tree_diameter` cross-checked here and never re-derived (the path's edge weights proven to sum to the diameter), `cluster_minimum_spanning_tree` owns `build_mst`, `is_bridge`, and `edge_count`, `cluster_graph` owns the matrix a level below -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster tree centroid (the balance point of the skeleton)** -- `cluster_tree_centroid` (`centroid_max_subtree`, `centroid_balance`, `tree_centroid`, `is_centroid`, `CentroidError`), which reads a **different** balance point off the same tree `cluster_tree_radius` read: where the **center** is the cluster of least eccentricity, nearest to all others by **reach**, the **centroid** is the cluster whose removal splits the tree into the most even pieces by **count** -- so a surface can pick the cluster that **halves** a connected group for a fold, a partition, or a two-column layout, not the one nearest by distance. `centroid_max_subtree` reads the largest component left when a cluster is pulled out of the tree, `centroid_balance` the smallest such largest-piece over every cluster, `tree_centroid` names the cluster that achieves it (the lowest id on a tie), `is_centroid` asks whether a cluster is a balance point. The decisive crux -- the center weighs **reach** and the centroid weighs **balance**, so on a lopsided tree they **part** yet on a balanced tree they **coincide**. **On a broom they part -- the center stays in the handle, the centroid moves to the bristles:** over a handle chain (clusters `4-5-6`, gaps `3`) into a wide bar (cluster `7`) with three bristles (clusters `1,2,3`, gaps `2`) massed on the bar, the centroid is the **bar** cluster 7 -- pulling it drops each bristle into its own singleton and leaves the handle `{4,5,6}` as one modest remainder, the largest piece `3`, the most even split any cluster offers -- while the center by reach is a **handle** cluster 6 up the chain, and pulling cluster 6 leaves a lopsided `4`; so `tree_center` (`6`) and `tree_centroid` (`7`) name **two different clusters**, each the other's non-answer -- `is_center` true for the handle and false for the bar, `is_centroid` the exact reverse. **On a star or a path they meet:** over the three-cluster star the hub is **both** center and centroid (pulling it drops both leaves to singletons, balance `1`, and it reaches each across one bridge), and over the symmetric path the middle cluster 2 is both (it halves the count and minimizes the reach) -- only when mass and reach pull different ways do the two part. A lone-cluster layout is already one whole: zero balance, its own centroid; a zero or past-the-count id refuses `BadClusterId`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the removal-split reads (the largest component left when a cluster is pulled, the argmin over every cluster, the centroid test); `cluster_minimum_spanning_tree` owns `build_mst` and the edges, `cluster_tree_radius` owns `tree_center` and `is_center` cross-checked here to prove the divergence and never re-derived, `cluster_graph` owns the matrix a level below -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster MST leaves (the tips and joints of the skeleton)** -- `cluster_mst_leaves` (`tree_degree`, `is_leaf`, `is_internal`, `leaf_count`, `internal_count`, `LeavesError`), which reads the plainest structural fact underneath every special-cluster read the tree family named: which clusters are **tips** of the skeleton and which are **joints** -- so a surface can draw an endpoint of a connecting line differently from a branch-point, a tip getting the drag-handle for the whole limb, a joint getting the splitter to fold a subtree. `tree_degree` counts the bridges a cluster meets, `is_leaf` names a tip (one bridge, or the lone cluster with none), `is_internal` names a joint (two or more bridges), `leaf_count` and `internal_count` read the whole partition, which always sums to the cluster total by the handshake lemma (the degrees of a tree sum to twice its edge count). The decisive crux -- the tip/joint partition is **coarser** than the pole/center/centroid reads, and **bounds** every one. **The poles are always leaves, yet not every leaf is a pole:** over a **sprout** (a main path `1-2-3-4` with a fifth cluster sprouting off the interior joint 2, MST edges `1-2`, `2-3`, `3-4`, `2-5`) the diameter poles are the path ends 1 and 4, both tips, while cluster 5 is a genuine leaf whose farthest reach (`8`) falls short of the diameter (`9`), so `is_diameter_endpoint(5)` reads **false** though `is_leaf(5)` reads **true** -- the pole set a **strict subset** of the tips; on a **path** the two coincide, the two tips exactly the two poles, the sprout's strict-subset case reduced to equality. **The center and the centroid are always joints** (on a tree of three or more clusters): on the sprout both land on the interior joint cluster 2, cross-checked against `cluster_tree_radius` and `cluster_tree_centroid` and never re-derived, because pulling a leaf leaves one lopsided piece (the worst balance) and a leaf's reach runs the whole tree (the worst eccentricity) -- so the special clusters live among the joints while the tips hold the handles. A single-edge tree has two tips and no joint, a lone-cluster layout is one tip and no joints (degree zero, read as a leaf); a zero or past-the-count id refuses `BadClusterId`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the degree read and the tip/joint partition over the tree's edges; `cluster_minimum_spanning_tree` owns `build_mst` and the edges, `cluster_tree_diameter` owns the poles, `cluster_tree_radius` the center, `cluster_tree_centroid` the centroid, each cross-checked here to prove the bound and never re-derived -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster MST subtrees (the branches of the skeleton)** -- `cluster_mst_subtrees` (`edge_split`, `edge_balance`, `edge_separator_balance`, `most_balanced_edge`, `is_balanced_edge`, `EdgeSplit`, `SubtreesError`), which reads what a joint's splitter actually cuts: pull one **bridge edge** and the tree falls into exactly **two** pieces -- so a surface can drag a group apart at a seam, grab the connecting line between two regions, cut it, and the layout splits into the two halves that line was carrying. `edge_split` reads the two component sizes of a severed bridge (`near` the low-id side, `far` the high-id side, summing to the cluster count), `edge_balance` the larger of the two, `edge_separator_balance` the smallest such larger-piece over every bridge (the most even single cut), `most_balanced_edge` names the seam that achieves it, `is_balanced_edge` the test. The decisive crux -- an **edge** cut and a **vertex** removal are two different splits of the same tree, and the edge cut is the **blunter** of the two. **Every edge cut yields exactly two complementary pieces, where a vertex removal yields as many as the vertex's degree:** over the same **sprout** (`1-2-3-4` with a fifth off joint 2, MST edges `1-2`, `2-3`, `3-4`, `2-5`) cutting edge `2-3` splits into `{1,2,5}` (near `3`) and `{3,4}` (far `2`), two pieces summing to `5`, yet pulling the **vertex** 2 of degree 3 drops the tree into **three** pieces, so `cluster_tree_centroid`'s largest-piece read is only `2` -- the edge cut blunter than the vertex; and a **leaf edge** cuts off a singleton on its tip side (cutting `2-5` splits `{5}` from `{1,2,3,4}`), agreeing with `cluster_mst_leaves` that cluster 5 is a tip. **The edge separator and the centroid vertex are different balance points, and the edge cut can never balance below the larger half:** on the sprout the most even single edge (`2-3`) leaves a larger piece of `3`, exactly `ceil(5/2)`, while the centroid vertex 2 leaves only `2`; on a **star** the gap is widest, every edge cutting off one leaf so the separator is `n-1` while the centroid hub leaves singletons a balance of `1`; only over a **single edge** do the two meet, the cut leaving `1` and `1` and pulling either endpoint leaving the other alone, both reading `1`. A lone-cluster layout has no bridge to cut, so the separator reads refuse `NoEdges`; a non-bridge pair refuses `NotABridge`, a zero or past-the-count id `BadClusterId`, a self pair `SameCluster`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the edge-cut split; `cluster_minimum_spanning_tree` owns `build_mst`, the edges, and `is_bridge`, `cluster_tree_centroid` owns the vertex-removal balance cross-checked here to prove the divergence, `cluster_mst_leaves` owns `is_leaf` cross-checked on the leaf-edge cut -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster subtree frames (what the branches hold)** -- `cluster_subtree_frames` (`subtree_members`, `subtree_frame`, `subtree_area`, `frames_overlap`, `MemberSplit`, `SubtreeFramesError`), the geometric **payoff** of the subtree read: where `cluster_mst_subtrees` gave only the two component **sizes** a severed bridge yields, this rung reads what each limb **is** -- its member cluster set and its union **frame**, the two halves a surface draws when a keeper drags a group apart at a seam. `subtree_members` names the exact cluster ids on each side of a cut (`near` the low-id endpoint, `far` the high-id endpoint), `subtree_frame` the union group-handle box that side spans, `subtree_area` its total foreground substance, `frames_overlap` whether the two limbs' frames intersect in the plane. The decisive crux -- the two limbs **partition** the clusters cleanly, yet their frames **need not**: the box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted from one cluster (`cluster_facts`) to the two halves of a cut. **The member sets always partition, the frames sometimes overlap:** over five singleton clusters (a square `L1 L2 L3 L4` whose mutual gaps tie so Prim builds a **star** on hub `L1` with edges `L1-L2`, `L1-L3`, `L1-L4`, plus a far outlier `L5` hanging off arm `L4` with edge `L4-L5`) cutting the hub-arm edge `L1-L4` leaves the near limb `{L1,L2,L3}` and the far limb `{L4,L5}`, disjoint members, yet the near frame `(1,1)-(6,6)` and the far frame `(5,5)-(13,13)` **overlap** in the `(5,5)-(6,6)` corner where `L4` sits, so `frames_overlap` reads **true** though not a single cluster is shared -- the frame over-claiming a contact the membership never makes. **A cut can also split cleanly in space:** cutting the outlier edge `L4-L5` of the same tree leaves the near limb `{L1,L2,L3,L4}` spanning `(1,1)-(6,6)` and the far limb `{L5}` at `(12,12)-(13,13)`, the frames **disjoint**, so `frames_overlap` reads **false** -- the member partition invariant while the frame overlap is not, and the two limbs' areas always sum to the whole layout's foreground on either cut. A non-bridge pair refuses `NotABridge`, a zero or past-the-count id `BadClusterId`, a self pair `SameCluster`, a lone-cluster layout has no bridge to cut. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the limb reads; `cluster_minimum_spanning_tree` owns `build_mst`, the edges, and `is_bridge`, `cluster_facts` owns `cluster_bbox` and `cluster_area` read here over each side's members -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Cluster seams (the whole-tree seam decomposition)** -- `cluster_seams` (`seam_decomposition`, `is_clean_seam`, `clean_seam_count`, `most_balanced_seam_is_clean`, `SeamFacts`, `SeamsError`), which reads **every** bridge of the skeleton at once as a **seam** a surface could cut to pull the layout into two halves -- so a surface offers the drag-apart handle at the **right** seam rather than an arbitrary one. Where `cluster_subtree_frames` read **one** bridge cut, this rung joins the count read and the spatial read over all of them: `seam_decomposition` names per bridge its pair, gap, count-balance (the larger of the two limb sizes), and whether the cut splits the layout **cleanly** in space (the two limb frames disjoint); `is_clean_seam` is the per-seam spatial test; `clean_seam_count` how many seams split cleanly; `most_balanced_seam_is_clean` whether the count-most-balanced seam is also a clean spatial split. The decisive crux -- a **count** balance and a **spatial** cleanliness are two different optima, so the seam that splits the counts most evenly and the seam that splits the plane most cleanly can be **opposite edges of the same tree**. **On a star-and-tail the two optima diverge:** over five singleton clusters (a square `L1 L2 L3 L4` whose mutual gaps tie so Prim builds a **star** on hub `L1` with edges `L1-L2`, `L1-L3`, `L1-L4`, plus a far outlier `L5` on arm `L4` with edge `L4-L5`) the count-most-balanced seam is the hub-arm edge `L1-L4` (cut leaves `{L1,L2,L3}` of 3 and `{L4,L5}` of 2, larger 3, the only cut below 4), yet its two limb frames `(1,1)-(6,6)` and `(5,5)-(13,13)` **overlap** in the `(5,5)-(6,6)` corner -- not a clean split -- while the **only** clean seam is the outlier edge `L4-L5` (near `(1,1)-(6,6)`, far `(12,12)-(13,13)`, disjoint) and it is the count-**worst** with larger 4, so `most_balanced_seam_is_clean` reads **false** and `clean_seam_count` reads 1: the count optimum and the spatial optimum name opposite edges, the box-is-a-prefilter lesson lifted from one cut to the whole-tree **choice** of where to pull apart. **On a collinear path the two optima coincide:** over four blocks in a left-to-right row (MST a path `1-2-3-4`) every seam separates a left frame from a right frame, so `clean_seam_count` reads 3 and the count-most-balanced middle seam `2-3` (larger 2, `ceil(4/2)`) is among the clean ones, so `most_balanced_seam_is_clean` reads **true** -- the divergence a fact about the layout, not a flaw in either read. A lone-cluster layout has no seam to cut, so the whole-tree reads refuse `NoEdges`; a per-seam test of a non-bridge pair refuses `NotABridge`, a zero or past-the-count id `BadClusterId`, a self pair `SameCluster`. Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the whole-tree join; `cluster_minimum_spanning_tree` owns `build_mst` and the edges, `cluster_mst_subtrees` owns `edge_balance` and `most_balanced_edge` (the count optimum), `cluster_subtree_frames` owns `frames_overlap` over the two limbs (the spatial read) -- a **reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading over a reading**.
- **Universal roundness proof (exact inscribed radius)** -- `roundness_universal` (`universal_bounds`, `exact_inradius_sq`, `inner_within_outer_universal`, `chamfer_overcredits`, `UniversalBounds`), the **closing** of the horizon `roundness` named: where `roundness` proved `r_in^2 <= r_out^2` **exactly for orthogonal geometry** and named a universal proof for arbitrary diagonal-limited shapes the honest next horizon, this rung closes it by reading the **exact** Euclidean inscribed radius at the deepest pixel, so the inequality holds for **every** shape -- verified by containment rather than approximated by the chamfer. The exact inner is the **nearest-background-square distance** in the shared doubled convention: the minimum, over every background pixel square and the image exterior, of the exact squared distance from the center `(2cx+1, 2cy+1)` to the nearest point of that square (each coordinate clamped into the square's doubled bounds) -- an exact integer in doubled^2, **no float, no tolerance**. It is universal by a positive geometric lemma: the open disk of that radius contains **no** background point (any point nearer lies in no background square and outside no image edge, so it lies in a foreground square), so it sits inside the region; the region sits inside the outer enclosing disk; a disk inside a region inside a covering disk has radius no larger than the covering radius -- so `inner_within_outer_universal` decides `exact_inner <= outer` exactly and is **always true**, for diagonal, concave, and holed shapes alike. The decisive **overcredit** crux: an orthogonal 5x5 block reaches its nearest background **straight**, so the exact inner **25** equals `roundness`'s chamfer inner `225/9` (the chamfer exact for orthogonal geometry, as it claimed); a filled **diamond** reaches its nearest background **diagonally** (deepest pixel `(2,2)`, nearest background at pixel offset `(1,2)`, chamfer depth 7), so the chamfer **overcredits** an inner of `(2*7-3)^2/9 = 121/9` ~ 13.44 doubled^2 while the true inscribed disk reaches only the **corner** of that background pixel, exact squared radius **exactly 10** -- the chamfer disk of the claimed radius pokes past that corner into background, not truly inscribed. `chamfer_overcredits` reads **true** on the diamond (`121 > 90` by exact i128 cross-multiplication) and **false** on the block and ring wall (`25 == 25`, `1 == 1`) where the chamfer is exact. The exact inner still separates the block (25) from the ring wall (1) the outer bound cannot; an all-background mask refuses `NoForeground` and a zero-size source `EmptySource` before a background pixel is scanned.
