Compose not braid: this rung owns **only** the exact nearest-background-square scan and the universal comparison; `roundness` owns the doubled convention, the outer bound, and the deepest-pixel center -- a **reading**, not a render.
- **Light and color** — `adjust` (brightness · contrast around the 128 midpoint, alpha untouched), `saturate`, hue rotation, and a book of named **filter presets** that travels as a `format filter-presets-v1` record.
- **Non-destructive editing** — `photo_edits.rye` records the verbs **as data** (an `Edit` union, a bounded `EditList`, `max_edits = 64`) and `apply` **replays** them over a source to a fresh image; replay is deterministic, so an edited picture never needs its pixels stored — only the pair *(source, edit-list)*. The list travels as a `format photo-edits-v1` record; `../pond/apps/edit_store.rye` beads it content-addressed; `photo_revert.rye` reverts by simply dropping edits off the end, never losing what a keeper might redo.
- **The glass surface** — `../brushstroke/` carries the whole app onto Skate: a crop tool (drag → frame → commit → chain), a filter strip and picker (read the book at a glance, swipe to preview, keep one), a filmstrip of the edit history (jump, scrub, track), a scrolled screen view, and the **Photos library** — a wall of content-addressed tiles where a tap resolves backward through the wall's geometry to fetch and open exactly the picture under the finger. The loop closes end to end: **library → tap → open → edit → save → library**, the wall growing by one when an opened picture is saved back.

### Open Media Primitives — the color and font floor beside the codec (Season G)

The `image/` directory also holds the opening rungs of **Season G (Open Media Primitives)** — the open, bounded, permissively-licensed media family the image codec began. They finish for color and type what QOI finished for pixels: a value held exactly, every reading of it deterministic, every malformed input refusing by name.

- **`color.rye`** — the open **sRGB color algebra**. A color is four bytes (red · green · blue · alpha) held exactly; hex (`#rgb` … `#rrggbbaa`) and functional `rgb()/hsl()` texts are *readings* of those bytes, so `parse(format(c))` recovers a color byte-for-byte, and a malformed text refuses by name (`BadHexLength` · `BadHexDigit` · `BadFunctionForm` · `ChannelOutOfRange`). All arithmetic is integer fixed-point, deterministic across targets; the module states its measured HSL round-trip tolerance rather than claiming an exactness integer math cannot hold. The color model **Brushstroke and Skate read the day the design system lands** — color math waits on nothing, only rendering is paused. Witness: `tools/color_algebra_witness.rish`.
- **`color_adjust.rye`** — the **palette toolkit** over the color algebra: the everyday moves a design system makes from one seed color, written single-stranded as a consumer of color's own HSL round trip. `lighten`/`darken` steer the lightness band and clamp at white/black; `saturate`/`desaturate` steer the saturation band; `grayscale` yields an equal-channel grey (equal to a full desaturation); `rotate_hue` turns the wheel (a full 360 turn returns the hue) and `complement` is a half turn. Every amount is a bounded percentage-point step or degree turn, each verb clamps at the band edge rather than wrapping, and alpha rides through untouched — read-adjust-write over the existing algebra, so color's own proofs carry through. The scale **Brushstroke and Skate grow the day the design system lands**. Witness: `tools/color_adjust_witness.rish`.
- **`color_scale.rye`** — the **design-system ramp** one rung above the palette toolkit: grow a whole even tonal **scale** from **one** seed color — the Tailwind 50..900 / Material tonal row, tones from lightest tint to darkest shade that share the seed's hue and saturation while their lightness steps evenly down a named band (`scale_light_max` 95 down to `scale_light_min` 10). `tonal_scale(seed, dst)` fills a caller's buffer lightest-first; `tone_at(seed, i, count)` reads a single tone; `tone_lightness(i, count)` is the pure integer lerp beneath both. Single-stranded over color's HSL round trip — color decides the algebra, this module only sets each tone's lightness — so a red seed keeps its hue, a grey seed grows a pure-grey scale, alpha rides through every tone, and a scale is deterministic. A length under two or over `scale_max_tones` (32) refuses `ScaleTooShort`/`ScaleTooLong` by name, having written nothing. Witness: `tools/color_scale_witness.rish`.
- **`color_gradient.rye`** — the **two-endpoint ramp**, the sibling of the one-seed tonal scale: grow a whole even gradient between **two** arbitrary colors — the CSS `linear-gradient` stop list. `gradient(a, b, dst)` fills a caller's buffer with evenly-spaced stops, `dst[0]` the start color exactly and `dst[len-1]` the end color exactly; `sample(a, b, i, count)` reads a single stop; `stop_t(i, count)` is the even integer lerp of the mix parameter (0..255) beneath both. Single-stranded over `color.mix` — color decides the blend, this module only **spaces** the stops — so a red-to-blue mid stop carries both channels (a true blend, not a hard cut), a black-to-white ramp ascends strictly in luminance, a color-to-itself ramp stays flat, alpha rides the same lerp so an opaque-to-clear fade steps its alpha down, and a ramp is deterministic. A length under two or over `gradient_max_stops` (64) refuses `GradientTooShort`/`GradientTooLong` by name, having written nothing. Witness: `tools/color_gradient_witness.rish`.
- **`color_ramp.rye`** — the **multi-stop gradient**, the general form of `color_gradient` across **many** control colors — the CSS `linear-gradient(red, yellow, green)` stop list. `ramp(control, dst)` fills `dst` with an evenly-spaced piecewise-linear ramp through the `control` colors (`dst[0]` the first control exactly, `dst[len-1]` the last, and a stop that lands on a control reads it byte-for-byte). Single-stranded over `color.mix` — color decides each blend, this module only **routes** each stop to its segment and local fraction, floor-matching `color_gradient.stop_t` — so the general contains the special: a **two-control ramp equals `color_gradient.gradient` byte-for-byte**. A black-grey-white ramp ascends non-decreasing in luminance across both segments; a color-to-itself ramp stays flat; a control list under two or over `ramp_max_control` (16), or a `dst` under two or over the shared `gradient_max_stops` (64), refuses `RampTooFewControls`/`RampTooManyControls`/`RampTooShort`/`RampTooLong` by name. Witness: `tools/color_ramp_witness.rish`.
- **`color_ramp_stops.rye`** — the **positioned multi-stop gradient**, the general form of both `color_gradient` and `color_ramp`: each control carries its own **position** in `0..pos_max` (255), strictly ascending from 0 to `pos_max` — the CSS `linear-gradient(red 0%, yellow 80%, green 100%)` stop list, where the warm half can bunch into the last fifth. `sample_at(control, positions, pos)` reads the blended color at any global position (a position on a control reads it byte-for-byte; a position between carries both channels); `ramp_positioned(control, positions, dst)` fills `dst` with evenly-spaced **output** stops sampling the arbitrarily-positioned ramp. Single-stranded over `color.mix` — color decides the blend, this module only **routes** a global position to its segment and local fraction — so the general contains the special: a **two-control ramp anchored at `{0, pos_max}` equals `color_gradient.gradient` byte-for-byte**, while `color_ramp` is a **sibling** (it routes in segment units where this routes in the shared position domain, so uneven positions bend the ramp where even spacing cannot). A black-grey-white ramp ascends non-decreasing in luminance whatever the spacing; a length mismatch, non-ascending positions, a first off zero, a last off `pos_max`, a control list under two or over `ramp_max_control` (16), or a `dst` under two or over `gradient_max_stops` (64) refuses `StopCountMismatch`/`StopsNotAscending`/`FirstStopNotZero`/`LastStopNotMax`/`RampTooFewStops`/`RampTooManyStops`/`RampTooShort`/`RampTooLong` by name. Witness: `tools/color_ramp_stops_witness.rish`.
- **`color_swatch.rye`** -- the **on-color ramp** one rung above the tonal scale: it joins `color_scale.rye`'s scale with `color.rye`'s readable ink, so every tone carries the black or white ink that stays legible on it -- the design-system "on-color" token (Material's on-primary, Tailwind's paired text color). A `Swatch` is a fill plus its ink; `readable_scale(seed, dst)` grows a whole row of swatches from one seed, and `swatch_at(seed, i, count)` reads a single one. Single-stranded -- `color_scale` decides each fill, `color` decides each ink, this module only **pairs** them -- so the fills are byte-for-byte the ramp and each ink is exactly `color.readable_ink` of its own fill. Because the ramp's lightness steps down monotonically and `readable_ink` flips at one luminance threshold, the crossover is clean: black on the light tones above, white on the dark tones below, never a black tone beneath a white, and both endpoints clear WCAG AA. Text ink is fully opaque while a translucent seed keeps its fill glass; a length under two or over `swatch_max_tones` (32, shared with the tonal scale) refuses `ScaleTooShort`/`ScaleTooLong` by name. And `branded_scale(seed, ink_light, ink_dark, dst)` inks the same ramp from a keeper's own brand **pair** rather than pure black and white -- each swatch's ink is `color.best_ink` of its fill among the two -- the general form of `readable_scale`: passing pure white then black recovers `readable_scale` byte-for-byte (both break a tie toward the lighter ink), so the general verb contains the special one. Witness: `tools/color_swatch_witness.rish`.
- **`color_harmony.rye`** -- the **named color-wheel palettes** one rung above the palette toolkit: the classical harmonies a design system offers as a swatch rather than an angle -- `complementary`, `analogous`, `triadic`, `split_complementary`, `tetradic`, and `square` -- each grown from **one** seed by composing `color_adjust.rotate_hue` at the centuries-old wheel angles. Every scheme returns a fixed, small array (two to four colors, bounded by construction) that **leads with the keeper's own seed byte-for-byte**; `complementary`'s partner is exactly `color_adjust.complement`, so the module composes the toolkit rather than re-deriving it. Single-stranded -- `color_adjust` decides the hue turn (and its wrap, held saturation and lightness, carried alpha), this module only **picks** the classical angles -- so a translucent seed grows a translucent palette and a grey seed, having no hue to turn, collapses every scheme back to the same grey. The even-spacing claims (triadic thirds, square quarters) hold within a stated 4-degree integer-round-trip tolerance, stated rather than claimed exact. Witness: `tools/color_harmony_witness.rish`.
- **`color_contrast.rye`** -- the **WCAG contrast table** a design system ships beside its swatches, one rung above the on-color ramp: for every swatch of a palette, the exact contrast of its ink against its fill (`ratio_x100`, so 4.50:1 is 450) and which conformance bars that ink clears -- `meets_aa`, `meets_aa_large`, `meets_aaa`. `reading_for(swatch)` reads a single row; `read_scale(swatches, dst)` reads a whole palette into a caller's buffer, refusing `ReportLenMismatch`/`ReportTooShort`/`ReportTooLong` by name. Single-stranded -- `color_swatch` decides each fill-and-ink pair, `color` measures each contrast, this module only **reads** one measurement per swatch -- so each reading agrees byte-for-byte with `color.contrast_ratio_x100` and `color.meets_aa`/`meets_aaa`, and the three bars **nest** every row (AAA implies AA-normal implies AA-large). The table is **honest rather than flattering**: it reports a legible pair and a deliberately low-contrast grey-on-grey pair with equal faithfulness, so a design system reads which tones are body-text legible and which need large text rather than a blanket claim. Witness: `tools/color_contrast_witness.rish`.
- **`color_names.rye`** -- the **named color table** the algebra needed: the sixteen HTML4 / CSS Level 1 color keywords (the oldest, most durable named set on the web, stable since ~1996), each bound to its canonical hex. `resolve(name)` reads a name to its four bytes -- `"red"` to `{255,0,0,255}` -- case-insensitively, refusing `UnknownColorName` by name rather than guessing; `name_of(color)` reads a color back to its exact keyword, or `null` when none matches. Single-stranded -- this module **names** each color and `color` **decides** the bytes through its own `parse_hex`, so no private parser and no stored channel byte -- every entry is proven to round-trip through `color.parse_hex` and back through `color.format_hex`. A Simple, Lovable, Complete set: bounded, sorted strictly ascending, duplicate-free, and **block-complete** at the whole HTML4 sixteen (the full CSS set is the sibling `color_names_css.rye`, never folded in here so this stays the durable sixteen); `name_of` matches only opaque colors, so it never claims a name it cannot honestly hold. `resolve_any(text)` is the **universal front door** one rung up: an all-letters text resolves through the table, anything else delegates to `color.parse` (hex or `rgb()`/`hsl()`), spaces trimmed -- so a stylesheet or theme file reads `"red"`, `"#ff0000"`, and `"rgb(255,0,0)"` through one call, names from this module and every other form from color's own proven parser. Witness: `tools/color_names_witness.rish`.
- **`color_names_css.rye`** -- the **full CSS named-color table**, the sibling that carries the whole modern set the HTML4 sixteen only opens: the **148 CSS Color keywords**, `aliceblue` through `yellowgreen`, each bound to its canonical hex. `resolve(name)` reads any CSS keyword to its four bytes case-insensitively -- `"rebeccapurple"` to `{102,51,153,255}` -- refusing `UnknownColorName` by name; `name_of(color)` reads a color back to its **earliest matching** keyword, so a color with a synonym reads the alphabetically first (the set carries nine `gray`/`grey`-style pairs -- `#00ffff` reads `"aqua"` before `"cyan"`, `#808080` reads `"gray"` before `"grey"`), deterministically. Single-stranded exactly as its HTML4 sibling -- this module **names**, `color` **decides** the bytes through `parse_hex`, no private parser or stored channel. The two tables stand apart on purpose: `color_names.rye` stays the block-complete sixteen, this the block-complete 148, and the selftest proves this a **strict superset** of the sixteen (every HTML4 name resolves here to identical bytes), so the older witness guards the overlap by construction. Values cross-read from the W3C CSS Color spec and an independent recall of the same standard, agreeing byte-for-byte -- verified two ways rather than transcribed once. `color_nearest` and `color_name_group` can read this richer table to name any color more finely. Witness: `tools/color_names_css_witness.rish`.
- **`color_nearest.rye`** -- the **nearest named color to ANY color**, one rung above the named table. Where `color_names.name_of` reads a color to its *exact* keyword and `null` for everything else, `nearest(c)` answers the wider question a design system asks -- *what do we call this?* -- for a color that need not land on a keyword: it scans the sixteen HTML4 names and returns the closest by squared sRGB distance, so a near-red reads `"red"`, a dark blue reads `"navy"`, and every color has a name. A `Match` carries the name, the exact color it stands for, and the squared distance (0 when the query *is* a named color). Single-stranded -- `color_names` **owns** the names and their hex, `color` **decides** the bytes through its own `parse_hex`, this module only **measures** distance and **picks** the least. Alpha is outside the metric (the named set is opaque), so a translucent red still reads `"red"` where `name_of` returns `null`; ties break to the earlier sorted entry, so a color midway between `black` and `navy` reads the alphabetically-first `"black"` deterministically. Bounded (the whole scan is the fixed sixteen) and integer (the squared distance of three byte channels never leaves `u32`, bounded by `max_dist_sq`). `nearest_name(c)` reads the word alone. Witness: `tools/color_nearest_witness.rish`.
- **`color_nearest_css.rye`** -- the **nearest CSS-named color** to any color, the fine sibling of `color_nearest`. Where that scans the sixteen HTML4 names and a cornflower blue reads only `"blue"`, this scans the full **148 CSS names** (`color_names_css.rye`) and reads `"cornflowerblue"` exactly -- the richer table as a finer ruler, the payoff the 148-name set was built for. Same single-stranded shape: `color_names_css` **owns** the names, `color` **decides** the bytes, this module only **measures** squared sRGB distance and **picks** the least; a tie breaks to the alphabetically-earlier entry, so the nine synonym pairs read their first name (`#00ffff` reads `"aqua"` before `"cyan"`). The selftest proves the **payoff against the sixteen-name sibling**: for the same color the CSS reading is strictly nearer and finer-named, imported and compared on metal rather than asserted. Every one of the 148 is its own nearest at distance zero; alpha stays outside the metric, so a translucent cornflowerblue still reads its name where `name_of` returns `null`. Witness: `tools/color_nearest_css_witness.rish`.
- **`color_name_group.rye`** -- the **coarse color family** of any color, a sibling of the naming pair one rung above `color.rye`. Where `color_names` reads a color to its *exact* keyword and `color_nearest` to the *closest* named swatch, `group_of(c)` answers the coarser, older question a palette organizer asks first -- *what family is this?* -- and returns one of eleven plain words: the eight hue families `red` `orange` `yellow` `green` `cyan` `blue` `purple` `magenta`, plus the three achromatic `black` `white` `gray`. Single-stranded -- `color` **decides** the hue, saturation, and lightness through its own proven `rgb_to_hsl`, and this module only **reads** those three numbers against named bands (each a constant with a why, this module's own design, not a transcribed table). Tone speaks first: a very dark color reads `black` and a very light one `white`, whatever its hue, because at the lightness edges the eye sees almost no color; a washed-out color reads `gray`; a chromatic color reads its hue family, red wrapping the wheel to own both ends. Bounded (eleven families, a fixed sorted scan), integer (degrees and percents only), deterministic -- a photo tagger buckets by hue, a palette sorts by family, a filter keeps "the blues". Witness: `tools/color_name_group_witness.rish`.
- **`color_tone.rye`** -- the **coarse tone** of any color -- `dark`, `mid`, or `light` -- and the deliberate sibling of `color_name_group.rye`. Where `group_of` reads a color's *hue* family, `tone_of(c)` reads its *lightness* alone, ignoring hue and saturation. The two stay **separate modules on purpose** (single-stranded, each about one thing), so a caller **composes** them into a plain human description -- "dark blue", "light green", a bare "red" for the mid tone -- without either braiding the other's concern. Single-stranded -- `color` **decides** the lightness through its own proven `rgb_to_hsl`, and this module only **reads** that one number against two named edges (`tone_dark_max`, `tone_light_min`): at or below the dark ceiling reads `dark`, at or above the light floor reads `light`, everything between reads the neutral `mid` (the tone a description leaves unspoken). Because it reads lightness alone, a red and a gray of the same lightness read the same tone. Bounded (three tones, a fixed sorted scan), integer (a percent only), deterministic. Witness: `tools/color_tone_witness.rish`.
- **`color_describe.rye`** -- the **plain human description** of any color, the crest of the naming family and the rung `color_tone` and `color_name_group` were built to enable. `describe(c, buf)` **composes** the two siblings into the phrase a person actually says -- "dark blue", "light green", a bare "red" -- by three named rules of English color speech: a *mid* tone leaves its word unspoken (`"red"`, not `"mid red"`); a *black* or *white* family already carries its tone so the word is dropped (`"black"`, never `"dark black"`); every other case joins tone and family with one space (`"dark gray"`, `"light magenta"`). Compose not braid -- `tone_of` **decides** the tone, `group_of` **decides** the family, this module only **joins** their two words. Bounded to thirteen bytes (`describe_max`, the widest phrase "light magenta"); a buffer one byte too narrow refuses `DescribeBufferTooSmall` by name rather than truncating. Deterministic, and proven byte-for-byte against an independent rebuild from the siblings so the phrase can never drift from its parts. Witness: `tools/color_describe_witness.rish`.
- **`color_format.rye`** -- **write any color to its CSS functional string**, the writer that closes the round trip `color.parse_func` opened. The algebra could **read** `rgb()/rgba()/hsl()/hsla()` (`parse_func`) and **write** only hex (`format_hex`); a design system that reads a stylesheet color could not emit one back. `format_rgb(c, buf)` spells the four bytes in decimal -- `"rgb(255, 0, 0)"` when opaque, `"rgba(255, 0, 0, 128)"` when alpha departs, in this module's integer alpha dialect matching `parse_func` -- so `parse_func(format_rgb(c))` recovers the color **byte-for-byte**. `format_hsl(c, buf)` spells exactly `color.rgb_to_hsl(c)` -- `"hsl(0, 100%, 50%)"`, `"hsla(240, 100%, 50%, 128)"`; because integer HSL is not byte-exact, the honest claim here is **text fidelity** (the string never drifts from `rgb_to_hsl`, proven against an independent rebuild), not color exactness. Single-stranded -- it only **formats**, inventing no color fact. Bounded to twenty-six bytes (the widest, `"hsla(360, 100%, 100%, 255)"`); a buffer one byte too narrow refuses `BufferTooSmall` by name at both doors. Witness: `tools/color_format_witness.rish`.
- **`color_readable.rye`** -- **nudge a color to meet a WCAG floor**, the accessibility sibling of `color.rye`'s `readable_ink`. Where `readable_ink(bg)` throws away a caller's foreground and returns pure black or white, this module **keeps** the caller's color and moves it as little as it can -- along lightness alone -- until it clears a named WCAG floor against a background: a brand red stays red, a link blue stays blue, only its lightness shifting and only as far as the floor demands. `fit(fg, bg, target_x100)` returns `fg` unchanged when it already clears the bar (the smallest move is no move); otherwise it walks lightness **outward** from the seed by growing distance -- lighter side first, then darker -- and returns the **nearest** clearing value, a tie going to the warmer lighter ink (matching `readable_ink`). When no lightness clears the floor, not even the extremes that give the most contrast any ink can against a fill, it refuses `ContrastUnreachable` by name rather than pretend. Compose not braid -- `color` **decides** contrast through `contrast_ratio_x100` and the round trip through `rgb_to_hsl`/`hsl_to_rgb`, this module only **searches** lightness. `fit_aa`/`fit_aa_large`/`fit_aaa` name the three common floors (4.50/3.00/7.00). Bounded (at most 101 lightness steps), integer (a percent only), deterministic. Witness: `tools/color_readable_witness.rish`.
- **`glyph.rye`** — the open **glyph atlas** (`gfa1`), a font as a table of small coverage grids keyed by codepoint. It is QOI's twin one family over: `decode(encode(atlas))` recovers every codepoint, advance, and coverage byte **byte-for-byte**, a two-op run-length stream (RUN value+count, LIT count+bytes) compressing the long background/ink runs, and every corruption refusing by name (`BadMagic` · `BadCellSize` · `UnsortedCodepoints` · `CoverageOverrun` · `BadEndMarker` · `TrailingData`). `lookup(codepoint)` is the smallest honest reader a surface needs. Painting the coverage into pixels is a later, paused Skate/Brushstroke rung; this is the container beneath it. Teacher, thanked clean-room: `../gratitude/bdf-bitmap-font.md`. Witness: `tools/glyph_atlas_witness.rish`.
- **`frames.rye`** -- the open **intra-frame video container** (`gfv1`), a video as a table of images: a fixed header (shared width, height, orientation, fps, frame count), a length-delimited table of whole QOI frames, and the family's eight-byte end marker. Resting on the proven ground of `qoi.rye` (the growing-a-language method), `decode(encode(video))` recovers every frame's RGBA bytes **byte-for-byte** and preserves orientation, fps, and frame count; the container compresses (encoded < raw RGBA of all frames stacked); and every corruption refuses by name (`BadMagic` - `BadEndMarker` - `TrailingData` - `Truncated` - `BadOrientation` - `FrameDimsMismatch`). Orientation is a **checked claim** derived from the dimensions, never free metadata; content-addressing stays external (Tablecloth keys it, as for images), so `image/` keeps zero dependency on `crypto/`. Intra-frame only -- the inter-frame codec below is its motion sibling. Teacher, thanked clean-room: the container lineage of Ogg/Matroska (public shape only). Witness: `tools/frames_video_witness.rish`.
- **`frames_delta.rye`** -- the open **inter-frame video codec** (`gfd1`), the motion floor above the container: where `gfv1` stores every frame whole, `gfd1` stores the first frame as a whole QOI keyframe and every later frame **adaptively** -- whichever is smaller, a sparse strictly-ascending `(pixel index, RGBA)` delta against the frame before, or a fresh keyframe. A barely-moving clip (a talking head, a slide, a still scene with a moving cursor) shrinks to a keyframe plus a handful of edits; a **scene cut**, whose delta would touch every pixel and outweigh a plain keyframe, is stored intra-frame instead, so heavy motion never costs more than a keyframe. The decoder already accepts a keyframe at any position, so adaptive selection is a pure encoder gain with no format change. `decode(encode(video))` recovers dimensions, orientation, fps, frame count, and every frame's RGBA bytes **byte-for-byte**; every corruption refuses by name (`BadMagic` - `BadEndMarker` - `TrailingData` - `Truncated` - `FirstFrameNotKey` - `BadOrientation`, and the delta's own `UnsortedChanges` - `ChangeOutOfRange` - `TooManyChanges`). Rests on `qoi.rye` (the keyframe) and `frames.rye` (the container shape, orientation); content-addressing stays external. Witness: `tools/frames_delta_video_witness.rish`.

- **The `font5x7` corpora** -- the open, hand-drawn font content that fills the `gfa1` atlas above, every glyph a 5x7 coverage grid whose ASCII line art *is* the specification (the bytes are lifted from the drawing, never guessed). Each corpus builds a `glyph.GlyphAtlas` the encoder accepts, so `decode(encode(atlas))` recovers every glyph byte-for-byte, the marks read present and distinct, and each sits inside an honest legibility band: **`font5x7.rye`** the ten numerals, **`font5x7_alpha.rye`** the uppercase `A`-`Z`, **`font5x7_lower.rye`** the lowercase `a`-`z`, **`font5x7_punct.rye`** the thirteen common punctuation marks, and **`font5x7_symbol.rye`** the nineteen remaining printable-ASCII symbols (`"` `#` `$` `%` `&` `'` `<` `>` `@` `[` `\` `]` `^` `_` `` ` `` `{` `|` `}` `~`). Together they cover **every printable ASCII codepoint from `!` to `~` except the space** -- a full ASCII font set of our own hand, permissively ours, no external font to license. Witnesses: `tools/font5x7_witness.rish` and its `_alpha` / `_lower` / `_punct` / `_symbol` siblings.
- **`font5x7_ascii.rye`** -- the unifying font, the finishing rung of the family: it merges all five corpora with a freshly drawn blank space into **one** `gfa1` atlas covering **every printable ASCII codepoint from the space (32) to `~` (126)** -- ninety-five glyphs, contiguous and complete. Beyond the byte-for-byte round-trip each corpus already proves, the composed font proves the band runs **strictly ascending and contiguous 32..126** (no hole where a glyph was forgotten, no seam where two corpora overlap) and that the **space is the one blank glyph** (zero ink, carrying the shared advance so text lays out evenly) while every other glyph carries ink. This is the single font a surface reaches for, rather than five corpora to stitch by hand. Witness: `tools/font5x7_ascii_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_box.rye`** -- the first corpus **beyond ASCII**, opening the wider-symbol rung: the eleven **light box-drawing** marks -- horizontal, vertical, the four corners, the four tees, and the cross -- at their real Unicode codepoints `U+2500`..`U+253C`, drawn on the same 5x7 body with the pen riding the middle row and middle column. These are exactly the marks a terminal surface (the Dexter/Scooter family) reaches for to frame a panel, rule a line, or box a table. The source stays plain ASCII (the codepoints are numeric literals, each named in an ASCII comment) even though the glyphs cover a non-ASCII range. Like every corpus it proves `decode(encode(atlas))` byte-for-byte, all eleven marks present and distinct, each inside an honest legibility band (a plain rule is five cells, the cross eleven). Witness: `tools/font5x7_box_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_boxdouble.rye`** -- the **double-line** twin of the light box set, the second corpus beyond ASCII: the eleven **double box-drawing** marks -- horizontal, vertical, the four corners, the four tees, and the cross -- at their real Unicode codepoints `U+2550`..`U+256C`, drawn on the same 5x7 body with **two parallel rails** (horizontal rails on rows 2 and 4, vertical rails on columns 1 and 3, straddling the single-line pen's middle row and column). These are the marks a terminal surface reaches for to frame a heavier panel, rule a bold divider, or box a titled table. The source stays plain ASCII (numeric codepoint literals, each named in an ASCII comment) though the glyphs cover a non-ASCII range. Like every corpus it proves `decode(encode(atlas))` byte-for-byte, all eleven marks present and distinct, each inside an honest legibility band (a plain double rule is ten cells, the double cross twenty). Witness: `tools/font5x7_boxdouble_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_boxdiag.rye`** -- the **diagonal** set that completes the box-drawing family: the three **light diagonal** marks -- the forward slash, the backslash, and their cross -- at their real Unicode codepoints `U+2571`..`U+2573`, drawn on the same 5x7 body with the pen stepping corner to corner so a straight-line diagonal crosses the cell. These are the marks a terminal surface reaches for to strike through a cell, hatch a region, or mark an X. The source stays plain ASCII (numeric codepoint literals, each mark named in an ASCII comment) though the glyphs cover a non-ASCII range. Beyond the byte-for-byte round-trip and the present/distinct/legible band (ink 7..13 of 35), one structural property earns its own proof: the **cross is exactly the two single diagonals overlaid** -- a cell inks in the cross when either single diagonal inks it, and only then -- which is what makes the three a true family rather than three unrelated drawings. Witness: `tools/font5x7_boxdiag_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_arrow.rye`** -- the **directional arrows**, where the box corpora frame and rule this one **points**: the six basic arrows -- left, up, right, down, and the two axis double-heads (left-right, up-down) -- at their real Unicode codepoints `U+2190`..`U+2195`, drawn on the same 5x7 body with a shaft and a **filled-triangle head** for the four cardinals and **outward chevrons** for the two double-heads. These are the marks a terminal surface reaches for to mark a selection, scroll a list, or point at a field. The source stays plain ASCII (numeric codepoint literals, each arrow named in an ASCII comment) though the glyphs cover a non-ASCII range. Like every corpus it proves `decode(encode(atlas))` byte-for-byte, all six arrows present and distinct, each inside an honest legibility band (a chevron double-head is nine cells, a filled cardinal twelve). Witness: `tools/font5x7_arrow_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_arrowdiag.rye`** -- the **diagonal corner arrows** that complete the arrow family: the four off-axis arrows -- north-west, north-east, south-east, south-west -- at their real Unicode codepoints `U+2196`..`U+2199`, drawn on the same 5x7 body with a diagonal shaft stepping corner to corner and a two-prong head (one horizontal stub, one vertical stub) opening at the pointed corner. Where `font5x7_arrow.rye` points along the axes, these point to the corners, so the **eight-direction arrow set stands whole** -- the marks a terminal surface (the Dexter/Scooter family) reaches for to resize a corner, drag diagonally, or point off-axis. The source stays plain ASCII (numeric codepoint literals) though the glyphs cover a non-ASCII range. Beyond the byte-for-byte round-trip and the present/distinct/legible band (ink 11 of 35), one **structural** property earns its own proof on metal: the four are a **left-right reflected family** -- north-west mirrored column `c` to `4-c` is exactly north-east, and south-east is exactly south-west -- proven both ways so the relation is a true reflection, which is what makes them one hand rather than four unrelated drawings and catches a copy slip that mirrored the wrong pair. Witness: `tools/font5x7_arrowdiag_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_arrows.rye`** -- the **unifying arrow font**, the first unifier on the *symbol* side of the family, the sibling to the two letter unifiers (`font5x7_ascii.rye` gathers the printable ASCII glyphs, `font5x7_latin1_block.rye` the Latin-1 letters -- neither holds a symbol). It merges the two arrow corpora -- the six basic directions (`font5x7_arrow.rye`, `U+2190`..`U+2195`) and the four diagonals (`font5x7_arrowdiag.rye`, `U+2196`..`U+2199`) -- into **one** gfa1 atlas of the whole eight-direction arrow set, the single lookup a terminal surface (the Dexter/Scooter family) reaches for the day it points a cursor, resizes a corner, or scrolls. Beyond the byte-for-byte round-trip and the ascending-and-unique disjointness the letter unifiers prove, its crux is **contiguous block completeness** -- stronger than the Latin-1 unifier's, whose block carried two math-sign holes: the arrow span has **no** hole, so the selftest walks the whole ten-codepoint span `U+2190`..`U+2199` and shows every codepoint present, the run unbroken, the merged count (`6 + 4 = 10`) equal to the span itself, so a hole or a lost corpus is a stopped line. Witness: `tools/font5x7_arrows_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_genpunct.rye`** -- the **smart-punctuation** marks real rendered text carries above ASCII: the en dash and em dash (`U+2013` `U+2014`), the curly single and double quotation marks (`U+2018` `U+2019` `U+201C` `U+201D`), and the horizontal ellipsis (`U+2026`), drawn on the same 5x7 body. Where `font5x7_punct.rye` draws the straight marks a keyboard types directly, this corpus draws the typographic ones a surface must be able to **show** -- a document, a caption, a quoted foreign name -- even though our own prose stays plain ASCII by the ascii-first rule. The source stays plain ASCII (numeric codepoint literals) though the glyphs cover a non-ASCII range. Beyond the byte-for-byte round-trip and the present/distinct/legible band (ink 3..8 of 35, and the corpus even compresses: encoded 161 < raw 245), three **structural** properties earn their own proof on metal: the en dash is a strict **subset** of the em dash (the short dash inks only cells the long dash also inks, and the em inks strictly more, so a dash of the wrong length is a stopped line), each double quote **out-inks** its single twin (a double is two marks, a single one), and the ellipsis is exactly three evenly spaced dots on the baseline row -- what makes it read as three dots rather than a bar. Witness: `tools/font5x7_genpunct_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_block.rye`** -- the **block elements**, where the box corpora frame and the arrows point this one **fills and shades**: the four half blocks (upper, lower, left, right), the full block, and the three shade densities (light, medium, dark) at their real Unicode codepoints `U+2580`..`U+2593`, drawn on the same 5x7 body. These are the marks a terminal surface reaches for to draw a shaded panel, a progress bar, or a low-fi image preview at half-cell resolution. The source stays plain ASCII (numeric codepoint literals, each block named in an ASCII comment) though the glyphs cover a non-ASCII range. Two honest properties beyond the byte-for-byte round-trip earn their own proof on metal: the half blocks **partition** the cell (upper with lower, left with right -- each pair inks every cell exactly once, no overlap and no gap, which is what lets them double a terminal's resolution), and the shades form a strictly rising **ramp** under the full block (light `10` < medium `18` < dark `25` < full `35` of 35 cells) -- the grayscale ladder a surface reads to render an image in a text cell. This corpus even compresses (encoded 274 < raw 280), its glyphs run-dominant where the sparser marks were not. Witness: `tools/font5x7_block_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_boxarc.rye`** -- the **rounded box corners**, the soft-corner companion to the light box set: the four **arc corners** -- down+right, down+left, up+left, up+right -- at their real Unicode codepoints `U+256D`..`U+2570`, drawn on the same 5x7 body. Where `font5x7_box.rye` draws the four square corners that meet at a hard right angle, these draw the four rounded corners a modern terminal surface (the Dexter/Scooter family) frames a channel with. The rounding is the honest low-resolution ease every bitmap font uses -- the exact elbow cell left blank, the horizontal stub stepped one column in, so the pen reads as a soft diagonal rather than a claim of a true curve at 5x7. The source stays plain ASCII (numeric codepoint literals) though the glyphs cover a non-ASCII range. Like every corpus it proves `decode(encode(atlas))` byte-for-byte, all four corners present and distinct (the four elbows point four ways), each inside an honest legibility band (ink 5 of 35). Witness: `tools/font5x7_boxarc_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_boxdrawing.rye`** -- the **unifying box-drawing font**, the *second* unifier on the symbol side of the family (the arrow font was the first), the single lookup a terminal surface (the Dexter/Scooter family) reaches for the day it frames a panel, rules a table, or draws a soft or diagonal edge. It merges the four box corpora -- the eleven light marks (`font5x7_box.rye`, `U+2500`..`U+253C`), their eleven double-line twins (`font5x7_boxdouble.rye`, `U+2550`..`U+256C`), the four rounded corners (`font5x7_boxarc.rye`, `U+256D`..`U+2570`), and the three diagonals (`font5x7_boxdiag.rye`, `U+2571`..`U+2573`) -- into **one** gfa1 atlas of twenty-nine marks. Beyond the byte-for-byte round-trip and the ascending-and-unique disjointness (the merged count `11 + 11 + 4 + 3 = 29` with strictly-ascending codepoints proves the four corpora share no mark), its crux is **sparse block completeness** -- the deliberate contrast to the arrow font's contiguous span. The Box Drawing block is holey: twenty-nine marks scattered across a span of one hundred sixteen codepoints, the heavy-line variants not marks this open font draws. So the selftest walks the whole span `U+2500`..`U+2573` and proves **exactly** twenty-nine hits and eighty-seven misses -- the exact sparse shape, spot-checking two named holes (the heavy horizontal `U+2501` and heavy vertical `U+2503`) absent, so a phantom mark or a dropped corner breaks the tally. Witness: `tools/font5x7_boxdrawing_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_geometric.rye`** -- the **geometric shapes**, where the box corpora frame and the arrows point this one **marks and selects**: the ten marks a list reaches for -- the bullet, the filled and hollow square, the four filled triangles (up, right, down, left), the diamond, and the hollow and filled circle -- at their real Unicode codepoints `U+2022` and `U+25A0`..`U+25CF`, drawn on the same 5x7 body. These are exactly the marks a terminal surface (the Dexter/Scooter family) reaches for as a list bullet, an unread badge, a radio dot, a selection caret, or a menu marker. The source stays plain ASCII (numeric codepoint literals, each mark named in an ASCII comment) though the glyphs cover a non-ASCII range. Beyond the byte-for-byte round-trip and the present/distinct/legible band (ink 11..21 of 35), one **family order** earns its own proof on metal: the filled twin out-inks its hollow twin (black square over white square, black circle over white circle) and the small bullet under-inks the large filled disc -- which is what lets a list mark a leaf and a node, or a read and an unread item, distinctly, rather than as ten unrelated drawings. Witness: `tools/font5x7_geometric_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_subscript.rye` and `font5x7_superscript.rye`** -- the **off-baseline digit twins**, where the numerals corpus counts full-height these two set the same ten digits small and off the line: **`font5x7_subscript.rye`** draws them **low** (three cells wide over the bottom five rows, the top two blank) at the contiguous run `U+2080`..`U+2089` -- the numbers a chemical formula sets below the line (`H(2)O`, `CO(2)`) or a term is indexed by (`x(1)`, `a(n)`); **`font5x7_superscript.rye`** draws them **high** (over the top five rows, the bottom two blank) -- the numbers an exponent raises (`x(2)`, `10(6)`) or a footnote marks with. Beyond the byte-for-byte round-trip both prove present/distinct/legible glyphs, the digit family order (the dense eight out-inks the sparse one), and the off-baseline property (subscript's top two rows blank, superscript's bottom two blank). The superscript corpus proves one property the subscript twin cannot: its codepoints are a genuine Unicode quirk -- **non-contiguous and digit-scrambled**, since one, two, and three sit in the Latin-1 Supplement (`U+00B9`, `U+00B2`, `U+00B3`) far below zero and four-through-nine (`U+2070`, `U+2074`..`U+2079`), so the table opens on the digit *two* at its lowest codepoint rather than zero, and the corpus carries an explicit ascending table rather than deriving each from a base. Witnesses: `tools/font5x7_subscript_witness.rish` and `tools/font5x7_superscript_witness.rish`.

- **`font5x7_greek_upper.rye`** -- the **distinct Greek capitals** a formula sets, the uppercase companion to the lowercase Greek marks `font5x7_greek.rye` already carries (the capitals Delta, Sigma, Omega and the nine lowercase alpha..omega). More than half the Greek uppercase alphabet is drawn identically to a Latin capital (Alpha=A, Beta=B, Rho=P, Chi=X, and the rest), so a science surface spells those from the ASCII font it already loads. What ASCII cannot fold are the seven that carry a shape of their own -- **Gamma, Theta, Lambda, Xi, Pi, Phi, Psi** (`U+0393` `U+0398` `U+039B` `U+039E` `U+03A0` `U+03A6` `U+03A8`) -- the capitals an equation actually needs: the gamma function, a summation's index set, the empty product Pi, a wavefunction Psi, a magnetic flux Phi, a grand partition Xi. Its crux matches the family: the seven are not one shared body but **seven distinct constructions**, each read by its own toothed guard -- Gamma's bar over a lone left stem (no right leg, the mark that tells it from Pi), Theta's oval crossed by a middle bar, Lambda's spread apex with an open foot, Xi's three stemless bars, Pi's two full legs, Phi's stem poking above and below a crossed bowl, and Psi's three prongs gathering to a single stem. The source stays plain ASCII (numeric codepoint literals) though the glyphs cover a non-ASCII range; like every corpus it proves `decode(encode(atlas))` byte-for-byte, all seven present and distinct, each inside an honest legibility band (ink 11..19 of 35). Witness: `tools/font5x7_greek_upper_witness.rish`.
- **The accented-Latin family** -- the marks a Romance or German name reaches for beyond ASCII, drawn on the same 5x7 body at their real Latin-1 codepoints. The **lowercase** set is whole across `font5x7_latin1.rye` (the acute vowels, c cedilla, n tilde, u diaeresis), its grave/circumflex/german siblings, and their union in `font5x7_accented.rye`; **`font5x7_tilde.rye`** then draws the two nasal vowels a Portuguese name reaches for -- a tilde and o tilde (`U+00E3` `U+00F5`), the lowercase twins of the uppercase `U+00C3` -- filling the one gap the accented family had carried (they appeared before only as glyphs a corpus proved absent). Its crux is the tilde as the **fourth lean**: where the acute leans right, the grave left, and the circumflex peaks dead-center, the tilde is a wave spanning both halves of the top two rows and cresting right of center. **`font5x7_diaeresis.rye`** then draws the three diaeresis vowels the German umlaut set left undrawn -- e diaeresis, i diaeresis, y diaeresis (`U+00EB` `U+00EF` `U+00FF`), the letters French and the wider Latin world spell (Noel, naive, L'Hay) -- completing the vowel-diacritic taxonomy as the **fifth lean**: two parted dots on one row (the top row carries exactly two inked cells with the center column blank between them, the second mark row blank), the same two-dot mark `font5x7_german.rye` drew over a/o/u, proven here byte-identical to it so the accent family stays one consistent hand. Beyond the accents, **`font5x7_nordic.rye`** draws the three letters a Danish or Norwegian alphabet appends after z -- a with a ring, ae ligature, o with a stroke (`U+00E5` `U+00E6` `U+00F8`), the vowels that spell Aero, Bjorn, Kobenhavn -- closing the lowercase Latin-1 letter set (every alphabetic codepoint in that block now stands drawn). Its crux is that these three are not one shared mark but **three distinct constructions**, each read by its own toothed guard: the a ring wears a hollow ring above its body (a mark present, its center a hole -- a sixth diacritic beside the five leans), the ae ligature carries no mark and a full crossbar where the a and the e join, and the o stroke inks the center of its bowl where a plain o stays hollow. **`font5x7_icelandic.rye`** then draws the last three lowercase letters the block held -- eth, y acute, thorn (`U+00F0` `U+00FD` `U+00FE`) -- closing the lowercase Latin-1 letter set outright: every alphabetic codepoint in `U+00DF`..`U+00FF` now stands drawn. Its three are built three ways, each read by its own guard: eth wears a crossed ascender (a crossbar spanning the upper cell over a bowl), thorn stands a full-height stem (the left column inked from ascender to descender with a bowl at its middle), and y acute leans its stroke right on the lowercase y body. The **uppercase** set closes in two corpora: `font5x7_upper_accented.rye` draws the acute-and-tilde eight (`U+00C1` `U+00C7` `U+00C9` `U+00CD` `U+00D1` `U+00D3` `U+00DA` `U+00DC`), and **`font5x7_upper_latin1.rye`** the thirteen that complete it (`U+00C0` `U+00C2` `U+00C3` `U+00C4` `U+00C8` `U+00CA` `U+00CC` `U+00CE` `U+00D2` `U+00D4` `U+00D6` `U+00D9` `U+00DB`) -- the grave, circumflex, tilde, and diaeresis capitals -- so every accented lowercase letter the tree draws now has its capital and a civic surface prints a full name in all-caps with its true marks. The uppercase crux, since a capital fills the whole body: each letter is compressed into the bottom five rows with its mark on the top two, proven honest by a top-row placement check, and the five grave capitals lean left (top-row ink in the left half, none in the right) exactly as the lowercase grave corpus proves. Beyond the accented capitals, **`font5x7_upper_nordic.rye`** draws the three Danish-Norwegian capitals a name reaches for at its head or in ALL-CAPS -- A with a ring, the AE ligature, O with a stroke (`U+00C5` `U+00C6` `U+00D8`), the capital twins of the lowercase Nordic corpus -- built the same three distinct ways (a hollow ring above the compressed A, a fused AE ligature with a full crossbar and no mark, an O with a stroke through its bowl), each read by its own toothed guard. **`font5x7_upper_icelandic.rye`** then draws the three Icelandic capitals a name reaches for in ALL-CAPS -- Eth, Y acute, Thorn (`U+00D0` `U+00DD` `U+00DE`), the capital twins of the lowercase Icelandic corpus -- built three distinct ways, each read by its own guard: Eth is a full-height capital D crossed by a bar (a top bar and a bottom bar closing the bowl, a crossbar on the middle row crossing the stem into it), Thorn stands a full-height stem open at top and bottom with a bowl at its middle, and Y acute leans its stroke right on the compressed capital Y. **`font5x7_upper_close.rye`** then draws the three capitals that CLOSE the uppercase set -- E diaeresis, I diaeresis, O tilde (`U+00CB` `U+00CF` `U+00D5`), the capital twins of lowercase letters the diaeresis and tilde corpora already carry -- so **every alphabetic codepoint in the whole Latin-1 Supplement block, lowercase and uppercase alike, now stands drawn** in our own open hand. Its crux is two marks read by their own guards: the E and I diaeresis wear two parted dots on the top mark row (center blank, second row clear), proven byte-identical to each other so the mark stays one hand, and the O tilde wears a two-row wave cresting right. Two **unifying fonts** finish each half the way `font5x7_ascii.rye` finishes the printable set: **`font5x7_accented.rye`** merges the four lowercase corpora into one gfa1 atlas of twenty-one accents (`U+00DF`..`U+00FC`), and **`font5x7_upper.rye`** merges the two uppercase corpora into its exact capital twin -- twenty-one accented capitals (`U+00C0`..`U+00DC`) -- each proving the merged codepoints run strictly ascending and unique and the count equals the sum of its corpora, so the sets are disjoint and no letter is lost. One level above both, **`font5x7_latin1_block.rye`** gathers **all thirteen** leaf corpora into one gfa1 atlas of the whole Latin-1 Supplement letter block -- sixty-two letters (`U+00C0`..`U+00FF`), thirty-two lowercase and thirty uppercase -- and proves **block completeness**: it walks the whole 64-codepoint block and shows every codepoint present except exactly the two that are not letters, the multiplication sign (`U+00D7`) and the division sign (`U+00F7`), which live in `font5x7_math.rye`, so the font holds the whole letter block and nothing but it, the thirteen corpora disjoint. One level above every unifier, **`font5x7_latin1_text.rye`** merges the two proven sub-fonts -- `font5x7_ascii.rye` (the 95 printable ASCII glyphs, contiguous `0x20`..`0x7E`) and `font5x7_latin1_block.rye` (the 62 Latin-1 letters) -- into one gfa1 atlas of **157 glyphs**, the single font a Western European surface loads for a document, a caption, or a name. Beyond the round-trip and disjointness, its crux is the **clean gap**: the ASCII half ends at `~` and the Latin-1 half opens at A grave (`U+00C0`), so the range `0x7F`..`0xBF` between them carries no glyph (spot-checked at DEL, the copyright sign, and the inverted question mark), the ASCII half is proven contiguous, the Latin-1 half block-complete, and the space stays the one blank glyph. The source stays plain ASCII (numeric codepoint literals) though the glyphs cover a non-ASCII range; like every corpus each proves `decode(encode(atlas))` byte-for-byte, all present and distinct, each inside an honest legibility band. Witnesses: `tools/font5x7_latin1_witness.rish` and its `_grave` / `_circumflex` / `_tilde` / `_diaeresis` / `_german` / `_nordic` / `_icelandic` / `_accented` / `_upper_accented` / `_upper_latin1` / `_upper_nordic` / `_upper_icelandic` / `_upper_close` / `_upper` / `_latin1_block` / `_latin1_text` siblings.
- **`text_layout.rye`** -- the **measure half** that makes the corpora usable: given an atlas and a byte string of codepoints, it places each glyph along a line at its running pen origin and sums the advances into a total width. `layout_line` returns one `PlacedGlyph` (codepoint, pen origin, atlas glyph) per byte; `measure_width` sizes a line without a buffer; `covers` asks whether the atlas carries every byte; `wrap_lines` breaks a paragraph into lines that stay within a max width, at space boundaries, so each wrapped span (`WrapLine`: start, len, width) lays out directly and its stated width equals a fresh measure of it -- a word wider than the max takes its own line whole (word-boundary wrapping, never a mid-word split). It is pure geometry -- no pixel is touched, so like `color.rye` it is floor code that waits on nothing, and painting the placed coverage into an RGBA grid is the rung it prepares. A byte with no glyph is a named refusal (`UncoveredCodepoint`), a zero max width a named refusal (`MaxWidthTooSmall`), never a silent hole, so a caller composing with `font5x7_ascii.rye` lays out and wraps any printable string and learns of a gap at once. Witness: `tools/text_layout_witness.rish`.

- **`text_paint.rye`** -- the **paint half** that `text_layout.rye` named as the rung it prepares: it turns a laid-out string from measured geometry into a string **seen** on an image. Single-stranded composition over four free parts, braiding none of them: `text_layout` places each glyph, `glyph.rye` hands each a coverage grid (one byte per cell, 0 no ink .. 255 full ink), `color.rye` composites an ink color over the destination (Porter-Duff src-over), and `qoi.rye` owns the RGBA target. A coverage byte is the ink's alpha at that cell (scaled by the ink's own alpha), so full ink over an opaque ink writes the ink exactly, no ink leaves the destination untouched, and a partial cell blends true through `color.over`. `paint_placed` paints an already-placed line; `paint_line` lays out and paints in one call; `solid` mints a one-color canvas to paint on. The line's painted box is bound-checked **once** up front, so the inner loop is proven in-bounds and an overrunning paint refuses `PaintOutOfBounds` by name rather than writing outside the buffer. Where a surface would rather show the visible part of a too-wide label than field a refusal, `paint_placed_clipped` paints only the cells that land inside the target and drops the rest, returning the count it painted; it composites an in-bounds cell **identically** to `paint_placed` (both share one `composite_cell` routine), matches the strict paint pixel-for-pixel where they overlap, and degrades to a clean no-op when the whole line sits off-canvas. `paint_paragraph` wraps a block to a max width and paints each line stepping down, all-or-nothing against the target (`ParagraphOutOfBounds` by name), and `paint_paragraph_aligned` sets that block **left, centre, or right** by a pure sideways offset within each line's slack -- alignment moves ink without adding or dropping a pixel, so the up-front box check and the changed-pixel count hold whatever the alignment, `.left` reproduces `paint_paragraph` byte-for-byte, and `.center`/`.right` match a hand-composed line-by-line offset paint exactly. And `paint_paragraph_clipped` is the paragraph's clip companion to `paint_placed_clipped`: where `paint_paragraph` refuses a block that overruns, this one paints as much of it as a fixed box holds -- a line inside lands identically to the strict paint, a line straddling the bottom lands its visible rows, a line entirely below is skipped -- so a bounded panel shows the text that fits rather than refusing the whole block, returning the count of cells painted (a box the block fits in clips byte-for-byte the strict paint; a box one line-box tall paints just the first line where the strict painter would refuse; a box below the block is a clean no-op). And `paint_paragraph_boxed` is the **vertical** companion to `paint_paragraph_aligned`'s sideways set: given a box taller than the block, it places the whole stack **top, middle, or bottom** by a pure downward offset of the origin, then delegates the wrap-stack-paint to the aligned painter unchanged -- so vertical placement moves the stack down as one, adding no pixel and dropping none (`.top` reproduces the plain paragraph at the box origin; `.middle`/`.bottom` match a plain paint at the shifted origin; the changed-pixel count holds across every set), refusing `ParagraphBoxTooShort` by name when the block is taller than the box (the strict all-or-nothing companion to the clipping painter). And `paint_paragraph_in_box` is the family's 2-D capstone -- a real text region: it positions the content block within a box on **both** axes at once, one `h` set choosing left/centre/right for the block's place in the box *and* each line's set within it, one `v` set choosing top/middle/bottom, delegating the vertical half to `paint_paragraph_boxed` unchanged so every axis stays in one place. Each corner equals a plain aligned paint at the box offset (placement moves the block as one, adding no pixel and dropping none), refusing `ParagraphBoxTooNarrow` on a box narrower than the block and `ParagraphBoxTooShort` on one too short -- all-or-nothing on both axes. No new storage, codec, palette, or geometry -- only the join. Witness: `tools/text_paint_witness.rish`.

- **`shape.rye`** -- the **geometry floor** beneath the paint rungs: two bounded rectangle verbs over an RGBA grid, the shapes a surface frames a picture with before it paints a glyph. Where `text_paint.rye` paints **glyphs** onto a `qoi.Pixmap`, this rung paints **plain area** -- `fill_rect` fills a rectangle, `stroke_rect` draws a rectangle outline (a frame) -- the shapes a panel, a selection highlight, a crop overlay, a table cell, or a progress-bar track are drawn from. Pure geometry over two public APIs, braiding neither: `color.rye` composites the paint over the destination (src-over), `qoi.rye` owns the RGBA target. An opaque paint writes the color exactly; a translucent paint blends true through `color.over`, so a panel tint reads through to the picture beneath. Every rectangle's box is bound-checked **once** up front (in `u64` so the sum can never wrap), so an overrun refuses `RectOutOfBounds` by name rather than writing outside the buffer, and `stroke_rect` paints its border ring in a **single pass** (a pixel belongs to the ring when it lies within the thickness of any edge), so a corner is composited **exactly once** -- a translucent frame reads uniform, never a darker corner where two edge bands would overlap. `fill_rect` refuses `BadRectDims` on a zero extent; `stroke_rect` refuses `BadThickness` on a zero frame and `StrokeTooThick` when two borders would overrun a side; `ring_cells` predicts the painted ring count before a pixel is drawn. Witness: `tools/shape_witness.rish`.
- **`gradient_paint.rye`** -- the rung where the **color ramp meets the pixels**: the twin of what `text_paint.rye` did for the font family (it turned laid-out glyphs into pixels; this turns a positioned gradient into pixels). `paint_gradient_h` paints a **horizontal** positioned gradient into a rectangle -- each column is the color `color_ramp_stops.rye` samples at that column's global position (`c * pos_max / (w-1)`), painted down the whole height -- so a keeper who names a stop list reads back a gradient strip a surface can show; `paint_gradient_v` is the **vertical** twin with the axes swapped (each row the color at its global position, painted across the width, the ramp running top to bottom); `paint_gradient_diag` and `paint_gradient_adiag` are the **diagonal** pair that finish the linear-gradient family (main diagonal: pixel `(cx, cy)` maps to `(cx + cy) * pos_max / ((w-1) + (h-1))`, so the top-left corner is the first control exactly and the bottom-right the last, each anti-diagonal band one constant color; the anti-diagonal twin runs top-right to bottom-left, that painting mirrored left-to-right -- the CSS `linear-gradient(to bottom right, ...)` shape, refusing `StripTooSmall` on a single-pixel box). Compose not braid over three public APIs: `color_ramp_stops` decides each column's color, `qoi.rye` owns the RGBA target, `color.rye` composites src-over (so a translucent gradient tints the picture beneath), and this rung only **routes** each column to its position. The leftmost column is the first control exactly and the rightmost the last, byte-for-byte down the whole height; a mid column carries both channels (a true blend) constant vertically; uneven positions bend the painted strip where even spacing cannot; the strip round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte; a sub-rectangle paints only its own columns (the gutters stay ground). All-or-nothing -- the whole box is bound-checked once up front (in `u64` so a far edge can never wrap) and the ramp validated once before any pixel, so a `StripTooNarrow` (width under two), `BadStripDims` (zero height), `GradientStripOutOfBounds`, or any `color_ramp_stops` ramp refusal leaves the target untouched. Witness: `tools/gradient_paint_witness.rish`.
- **`radial_paint.rye`** -- the **circle** beside the line: the sibling of `gradient_paint.rye` for the other major CSS gradient shape. Where a linear gradient maps a pixel to its distance **along** one axis, `paint_gradient_radial` maps a pixel to its distance **from** the box center -- the center is the first control exactly and every corner the last, each ring between one constant color (the CSS `radial-gradient(circle, ...)` shape, its default `farthest-corner` extent). Compose not braid, unchanged: `color_ramp_stops` decides each pixel's color, `qoi.rye` owns the RGBA target, `color.rye` composites src-over, and this rung only **routes** each pixel to its radial position. The routing stays integer fixed-point so a disc is deterministic: coordinates are doubled (`2*cx - (w-1)`) so the center lands exactly on a pixel for odd dimensions and exactly between two for even ones without a fraction; a pixel's squared distance from the center `d2 = dx2*dx2 + dy2*dy2`, the farthest corner `max_d2 = (w-1)^2 + (h-1)^2` away, and the position `p = isqrt(d2 * pos_max^2 / max_d2)` (a bounded integer square root, no float), so the center (`d2 == 0`) reads 0 and a corner (`d2 == max_d2`) reads `pos_max` exactly. The four edge midpoints share one constant ring color (a true circle); the position grows with the radius; uneven positions bend the disc where even spacing cannot; the disc round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte; a non-square box still centers and corners exactly. All-or-nothing -- the whole box is bound-checked once up front (in `u64`) and the ramp validated once before any pixel, so a `BadStripDims` (zero dimension), `StripTooSmall` (single-pixel box), `RadialOutOfBounds`, or any `color_ramp_stops` ramp refusal leaves the target untouched. Witness: `tools/radial_paint_witness.rish`.

- **`conic_paint.rye`** -- the **sweep** beside the line and the ring: the third and last shape of the CSS gradient triad. Where a linear gradient maps a pixel to its distance **along** an axis and a radial gradient to its distance **from** the center, `paint_gradient_conic` maps a pixel to its **bearing** around the center -- the first control sits at the top (12 o'clock) and the ramp sweeps **clockwise** through east, south, and west back to the top (the CSS `conic-gradient(from 0deg at center, ...)` shape), every ray from the center one constant wedge color, the first and last controls meeting in a single seam at the top. Compose not braid, unchanged: `color_ramp_stops` decides each pixel's color, `qoi.rye` owns the RGBA target, `color.rye` composites src-over, and this rung only **routes** each pixel to its angular bearing. The routing needs no trigonometry: coordinates are doubled (`2*cx - (w-1)`) so the center is exact, and the bearing is an integer **pseudo-angle** -- the four quadrants around the center split the turn into four, and within a quadrant the position grows linearly with the ratio of the two coordinates (`(q*s + numer) * pos_max / (4*s)`, `s = |dx2| + |dy2|`), a monotonic function of the true angle. So the top reads 0, east `pos_max/4`, south `pos_max/2`, west `3*pos_max/4`, each exact. The bearings ascend strictly clockwise; each ray is one constant wedge; the top is the first/last seam; the sweep round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte; uneven positions bend the sweep where even spacing cannot; a non-square box still tops and seams exactly. All-or-nothing -- the whole box is bound-checked once up front (in `u64`) and the ramp validated once before any pixel, so a `BadStripDims` (zero dimension), `StripTooSmall` (single-pixel box), `ConicOutOfBounds`, or any `color_ramp_stops` ramp refusal leaves the target untouched. Witness: `tools/conic_paint_witness.rish`.

- **`repeat_paint.rye`** -- the **ramp that tiles**: the fourth shape of the CSS gradient family, beside the three that run their ramp once. Where a linear gradient runs the ramp **once** from edge to edge, `paint_gradient_repeat_h` runs it over a fixed **period** of columns and **tiles** it across the strip (the CSS `repeating-linear-gradient` shape) -- one tile of `period` cells carries the full ramp (the first control at its start, the last at its end), and the next tile starts over, so the pattern reads first..last, first..last with a hard seam at each boundary; `paint_gradient_repeat_v` is the **vertical** twin, tiling the ramp down the rows. Compose not braid, unchanged: `color_ramp_stops` decides each cell's color, `qoi.rye` owns the RGBA target, `color.rye` composites src-over, and this rung only **routes** each cell to its position **within its tile** via one integer modulo (`(c % period) * pos_max / (period - 1)`). The **period** carries the direction, not the width, so a strip may be any size >= one cell -- a strip narrower than one period shows the ramp's opening, a wider strip shows several tiles. An 8-wide strip over period 4 reads `0,85,170,255` twice; the tile repeats exactly (column 0 equals column `period`); each tile ascends then seams hard (the last cell drops straight back to the first); a horizontal repeat is constant down each column and the vertical twin constant across each row; the tiling round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte; a wider period spreads the ramp over more cells where a narrow one wraps. All-or-nothing -- the whole box is bound-checked once up front (in `u64`) and the ramp validated once before any pixel, so a `BadStripDims` (zero dimension), `PeriodTooSmall` (period under two), `PeriodTooLarge` (period past the dimension ceiling), `RepeatOutOfBounds`, or any `color_ramp_stops` ramp refusal leaves the target untouched. Witness: `tools/repeat_paint_witness.rish`.

- **`repeat_radial_paint.rye`** -- the **rings that tile**: the radial twin of `repeat_paint.rye` and the repeating sibling of `radial_paint.rye`. Where the single radial gradient runs its ramp **once** from the center to the farthest corner, `paint_gradient_repeat_radial` runs it over a fixed radial **band** of pixels and **tiles** it outward as concentric rings (the CSS `repeating-radial-gradient` shape) -- the center is the first control, a ring `band-1` cells out the last, and the next ring wraps back with a hard seam. Compose not braid, unchanged: `color_ramp_stops` decides each pixel's color, `qoi.rye` owns the RGBA target, `color.rye` composites src-over, and this rung only **routes** each pixel to its position **within its ring** (`(radius % band) * pos_max / (band - 1)`). The routing is integer: the center sits at the **integer** cell `((w-1)/2, (h-1)/2)` -- floored for even dimensions, unlike `radial_paint.rye`'s doubled center, so a pixel's radius is a whole number and every ramp position appears at full resolution along an axis; the radius is a bit-by-bit integer square root of the squared distance (no float). A black-to-white ramp over band 4 makes the center read 0 and the row outward `85,170,255,0` -- the ramp tiles as concentric rings; the ring ascends then seams hard; the four points one cell out share one ring color (a true circle); the rings round-trip through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte; a wider band spreads the ramp over more rings where a narrow one wraps. All-or-nothing -- the whole box is bound-checked once up front (in `u64`) and the ramp validated once before any pixel, so a `BadStripDims` (zero dimension), `StripTooSmall` (single-pixel box), `BandTooSmall` (band under two), `BandTooLarge` (band past the dimension ceiling), `RepeatRadialOutOfBounds`, or any `color_ramp_stops` ramp refusal leaves the target untouched. Witness: `tools/repeat_radial_paint_witness.rish`.

- **`repeat_conic_paint.rye`** -- the **sweep that tiles**: the sixth and last shape of the CSS gradient family, closing the set the linear, radial, conic, repeating-linear, and repeating-radial rungs began. It is the conic twin of `repeat_paint.rye` and the repeating sibling of `conic_paint.rye`. Where the single conic gradient runs its ramp **once** around the turn, `paint_gradient_repeat_conic` winds it `reps` times and **tiles** it (the CSS `repeating-conic-gradient` shape) -- `reps` identical pie wedges, a hard seam at each tile boundary, every ray from the center still one constant wedge color. Compose not braid, unchanged: `color_ramp_stops` decides each pixel's color, `qoi.rye` owns the RGBA target, `color.rye` composites src-over, and this rung only **routes** each pixel to its position **within its angular tile**. The routing needs no trigonometry: coordinates are doubled (`2*cx - (w-1)`) so the center is exact, the bearing is held at full precision as the turn fraction `T/D` (`T = q*s + numer`, `D = 4*s`, `s = |dx2| + |dy2|`), and tiling winds and reduces it -- `tile = (T*reps) mod D`, scaled `tile * pos_max / D`. So a black-to-white ramp over reps 2 makes the top read 0, east 127, south 0, west 127 -- the ramp cycles twice around the turn, top and south sharing the first control where the single conic never could; each tile ascends clockwise then seams hard; each ray is one constant wedge; more tiles pack more wedges (reps 4 wraps the east bearing to 0 where reps 2 read 127); the sweep round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte; a non-square box still tops and wraps exactly. All-or-nothing -- the whole box is bound-checked once up front (in `u64`) and the ramp validated once before any pixel, so a `BadStripDims` (zero dimension), `StripTooSmall` (single-pixel box), `RepsTooSmall` (reps under two -- that is the plain conic), `RepsTooLarge` (reps past the dimension ceiling), `RepeatConicOutOfBounds`, or any `color_ramp_stops` ramp refusal leaves the target untouched. Witness: `tools/repeat_conic_paint_witness.rish`.
- **`palette_paint.rye`** -- the **discrete sibling** of the gradient: where `gradient_paint.rye` paints a **continuous** ramp (every column a fresh blend), this rung paints a row (or column) of solid color **swatches** -- the palette legend a design surface shows beside a picker, a color scale, or a gradient. `paint_palette_h` tiles the width with `n` swatches, swatch `i` filling the columns `[i*w/n, (i+1)*w/n)` down the whole height; `paint_palette_v` is the vertical twin, stacking the swatches top to bottom. Compose not braid: `shape.rye` `fill_rect` **owns** the fill (a solid color composited src-over), `qoi.rye` owns the RGBA target, and this rung only **routes** each swatch to its cell -- no new color math, no new codec. The cells tile the strip **exactly**: an integer partition where cell 0 starts at the left edge and cell `n` ends at the right edge, the cells contiguous and disjoint (no gap, no overlap, a hard edge at each boundary), their widths differing by at most one when `n` does not divide the extent evenly (width 8 over 3 swatches -> widths 2,3,3, tiling 8 exactly). Each cell is its color exactly byte-for-byte; a translucent swatch blends src-over uniform across its cell; the palette round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte; a sub-rectangle paints only its own columns (the gutters stay ground). All-or-nothing -- the whole box is bound-checked once up front (in `u64`) and the palette validated once before any pixel, so an `EmptyPalette` (no swatch), `PaletteTooManyColors` (past `palette_max_swatches`, 256), `BadStripDims` (zero dimension), `PaletteStripTooNarrow`/`PaletteStripTooShort` (a strip too small to give every swatch one unit), or `PaletteOutOfBounds` leaves the target untouched. Witness: `tools/palette_paint_witness.rish`.

- **`checker_paint.rye`** -- the **discrete 2D sibling** of `palette_paint.rye`: where the palette tiles **one** axis with a row of solid swatches, this rung tiles **both** axes with an alternating two-color grid -- the **checkerboard** a design surface draws as the **transparency ground** behind a translucent picture (the light-and-white squares an image editor shows so an alpha hole reads as a hole, not as white). `paint_checkerboard` fills the rectangle with `cell`-sized squares, cell `(col, row)` painted its color by `(col + row)` parity so cell `(0, 0)` is the first color, its orthogonal neighbors the second, its diagonal neighbors the first again -- a true checkerboard; `paint_alpha_ground` is the one-call convenience over it with the conventional light/white shades and cell size 8. Compose not braid: `shape.rye` `fill_rect` **owns** the fill, `qoi.rye` owns the RGBA target, and this rung only **routes** each cell to its color -- no new color math, no new codec. The cells tile the box **exactly**: an integer partition where the first cell starts at the corner and a boundary cell the cell size does not divide evenly is simply **clipped** to the rectangle edge (no gap, no overlap, no seam). Each cell is its color exactly byte-for-byte; a translucent color blends src-over uniform; the checker round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte; a sub-rectangle paints only its own box (the gutters stay ground). All-or-nothing -- the whole box is bound-checked once up front (in `u64`), so a `BadCheckerDims` (zero dimension), `BadCellSize` (zero cell), or `CheckerOutOfBounds` leaves the target untouched. Witness: `tools/checker_paint_witness.rish`.

- **`compose.rye`** -- the **compositing verb** the whole surface family leans on: where `shape.rye` composites a **solid color** over the grid and `checker_paint.rye` draws the transparency **ground**, this rung draws the **picture itself** -- a **source image composited over a target image**, each source pixel blended src-over its target pixel, so a translucent photo laid over the checker ground reads as a photo with holes. Two gestures, one verb: `paint_over` lays a whole picture at a point; `paint_over_region` lays a **sub-rectangle** of a larger picture -- the **single-sprite render** an index-into-one-image surface uses (a marketplace that web-hosts one big sprite and draws each product as a window into it, the McMaster-Carr speed trick). Compose not braid: `color.rye` `over` **owns** the blend, `qoi.rye` owns both RGBA buffers, and this rung only **routes** each source pixel to its target pixel -- no new blend math, no new codec, no new color. An opaque source overwrites exactly its footprint and leaves the gutters untouched; a translucent source blends src-over, equal to neither the source nor the ground; a window of a sprite sheet renders exactly the indexed tile; the composited image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. All-or-nothing -- the source window and the destination box are bound-checked once up front (in `u64` so a far edge can never wrap), so a `BadComposeDims` (zero-size window), `SourceOutOfBounds` (a source window past the source edge), or `ComposeOutOfBounds` (a destination window past the target edge) leaves the target untouched. Witness: `tools/compose_witness.rish`.

- **`compose_tiled.rye`** -- the **background-repeat** sibling of `compose.rye`: where `compose` lays **one** picture over a target at a point, this rung lays **many** -- it **tiles** a small source pattern across a rectangle edge to edge, the way a surface repeats a texture, a wallpaper, or a small motif to fill a panel (the CSS `background-repeat` gesture). `paint_tiled` paves the box with copies of the pattern from the corner, a boundary tile the pattern size does not divide evenly simply **clipped** to the box edge (its top-left window) so the fill covers the whole box with no gap and no seam. Compose not braid: `compose.rye` `paint_over_region` **owns** the blit (a source window composited src-over), `qoi.rye` owns both buffers, and this rung only **routes** each tile to its window and its clip -- no new blend math, no new codec, no new color, not even a new pixel loop. An evenly-dividing pattern paves an exact grid of copies (each pixel its pattern color by the pure `(tx % pw, ty % ph)` modulo); a non-dividing box clips the last band and still paves the whole box; a translucent pattern tiles src-over uniform; the tiled image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte; a sub-rectangle paves only its own box; a pattern larger than the box is itself clipped. All-or-nothing -- the whole box is bound-checked once up front (in `u64`), so a `BadTileDims` (zero dimension), `EmptyPattern` (a pattern with no extent), or `TileOutOfBounds` leaves the target untouched. Witness: `tools/compose_tiled_witness.rish`.

- **`place_paint.rye`** -- the **background-position** sibling of `compose_tiled.rye`: where `compose_tiled` **tiles** a pattern edge to edge to fill a box (the CSS `background-repeat` gesture), this rung places **one** copy and **anchors** it -- a single source seated at a named corner, edge, or center of a box (the CSS `background-position` with `no-repeat` gesture: an icon pinned top-right in a panel, a logo centered in a card, a badge in a bottom corner). `paint_placed` takes a box, a source, and a horizontal and vertical `Anchor` (`start`, `center`, `end`) and composites one window of the source at the anchored spot, the box's remaining area left as the ground beneath. Two axes, one rule (`axis_window`, all unsigned): when the source **fits** the box the whole source is drawn at an anchored offset (start at 0, center on the slack, end flush to the far edge); when the source **overflows** the box a box-sized window of the source is drawn from the box corner, the anchor choosing which window shows -- so an oversize source is **clipped** to the box with no spill. Compose not braid: `compose.rye` `paint_over_region` **owns** the blit, `qoi.rye` owns both buffers, and this rung only **routes** the anchor to its one window and its one clip -- no new blend math, no new codec, no new color, not even a new pixel loop. A fitting source seats on the slack (center), flush to a corner (start / end), or at a mixed corner; an oversize source is clipped by its anchor; a translucent source blends src-over uniform; the placed image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. All-or-nothing -- the box is bound-checked once up front (in `u64`), so a `BadPlaceDims` (zero dimension), `EmptySource` (a source with no extent), or `PlaceOutOfBounds` leaves the target untouched. Witness: `tools/place_paint_witness.rish`.
