- **`space_paint.rye`** -- the **background-repeat: space** sibling that completes the repeat family: where `compose_tiled` tiles a pattern edge to edge and **clips** the boundary copy (`repeat`), and `place_paint` places **one** copy at an anchor (`no-repeat`), this rung tiles only **whole** copies and never clips one -- it fits as many whole patterns as the box holds, pins the first and last flush to the two edges, and shows the leftover as **even ground gaps** between them (the CSS `background-repeat: space` gesture: a row of evenly-spaced badges, a strip of logos gapped to the margins, a motif that fills a panel without a seam **and** without a cut). `paint_spaced` takes a box and a pattern and composites each whole copy at its spaced offset, the ground showing between and never through them. One pure per-axis rule (`plan_axis` / `axis_offset`, all unsigned): `count` is `floor(box / unit)` whole copies, and copy `i` seats at `i*unit` plus its share of the total gap (`i*total_gap / (count-1)`, integer-rounded), so copy 0 pins flush at 0 and copy `count-1` flush at `box - unit`, the copies always disjoint. Two honest edges: when only **one** whole copy fits it seats at the start with the leftover trailing as ground; when the pattern is **larger** than the box a single copy is clipped to the box (its top-left window), so the family's oversize rule holds here too. Compose not braid: `compose.rye` `paint_over_region` **owns** the blit, `qoi.rye` owns both buffers, and this rung only **routes** each whole copy to its spaced offset -- no new blend math, no new codec, no new color, not even a new pixel loop. An exact fit fills with no ground; a leftover axis shows the gap as ground; a translucent copy blends src-over uniform; the spaced image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte; a sub-rectangle spaces only its own box. All-or-nothing -- the box is bound-checked once up front (in `u64`), so a `BadSpaceDims` (zero dimension), `EmptySource` (a pattern with no extent), or `SpaceOutOfBounds` leaves the target untouched. Witness: `tools/space_paint_witness.rish`.

- **`round_paint.rye`** -- the **background-repeat: round** member that completes the quartet, the one the resize floor unlocked: where `compose_tiled` tiles a pattern and **clips** the boundary copy (`repeat`), `space_paint` tiles whole copies and leaves ground **gaps** (`space`), and `place_paint` places **one** anchored copy (`no-repeat`), this rung tiles whole copies and **scales** each so a whole count paves the box exactly -- **no gap and no clip** (the CSS `background-repeat: round` gesture: a texture stretched a hair so the tiles land flush, a motif that fills a panel with whole copies and no seam, an oversize badge **shrunk** to fit rather than cropped). `paint_rounded` takes an allocator, a box, and a pattern; it fits the nearest whole `count` of copies per axis (`round_count`: `round(box / unit)`, at least one), splits the box into that many contiguous cells whose extents differ by at most one pixel (a fair integer partition, exactly as `palette_paint` splits a strip), resizes each distinct cell size **once** (at most four tiles across the two axes), and blits each cell its matching resized copy. Compose not braid: `scale.rye` `scale_nearest` **owns** the resize, `compose.rye` `paint_over` **owns** the blit, `qoi.rye` owns the buffers, and this rung only **routes** the box into whole cells -- no new blend math, no new codec, no new color, no new resample. The oversize rule is round's own: a pattern **larger** than the box becomes **one** copy **shrunk** to the box (`count` one), never a clipped window. An exact fit rounds to a clean identity repeat; a non-dividing box scales the copies so the whole box is paved with no gap and no clip; an oversize pattern shrinks to one copy sampling across its whole extent; a translucent copy blends src-over uniform; the rounded image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte; a sub-rectangle rounds only its own box. All-or-nothing -- the box is bound-checked once up front (in `u64`) **before** any tile is resized, so a `BadRoundDims` (zero dimension), `EmptySource` (a pattern with no extent), or `RoundOutOfBounds` leaves the target untouched and allocates nothing. Witness: `tools/round_paint_witness.rish`.

- **`scale.rye`** -- the **resize floor** the paint rungs lean on, the verb a surface reaches for whenever a box and a picture disagree on extent: a thumbnail shrunk from a photo, a pixel-art sprite doubled to read at arm's length, a logo fit to a card, a responsive image scaled to its column. Where `compose.rye` lays one picture over another at its **native** size, `scale_nearest` **remakes** the picture at a named `dst_w` x `dst_h` -- each destination pixel takes the color of the **nearest** source pixel, so an upscale doubles whole pixels into clean blocks and a downscale samples one source pixel per destination cell, never inventing a between-color. Nearest, not blended: this rung owns the one new verb the family lacked -- a per-axis integer map from a destination index to its source index (`src_index`: `sx = dx * src_w / dst_w`, the classic nearest-neighbor floor, seen once per axis so a resize is the same rule twice) -- and writes each destination pixel by **copying** its nearest source pixel whole, so it adds no color math (nothing is mixed), no codec, no blend; `qoi.rye` owns the buffers, and this rung only **routes** each destination pixel to its nearest source pixel. Integer only: no float ever enters the map, so a resize is deterministic byte-for-byte. An identity resize reproduces the source byte-for-byte; a 2x upscale doubles the four source pixels into four clean 2x2 blocks; a 2x downscale samples the nearest source columns (0 and 2), never an average; a non-integer 2-to-3 scale bends the map so the middle cell repeats the first source column; a translucent source keeps its exact alpha byte through the scale (carried, never blended); the scaled image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. All-or-nothing -- the whole destination is bound-checked once up front (its pixel count against the module's named ceiling, its dims against the codec's max) **before** any allocation, so a `BadScaleDims` (zero dimension), `EmptySource` (a source with no extent), or `ScaleTooLarge` (a target past the codec max or the pixel ceiling) refuses by name having allocated nothing. This is the nearest-neighbor path the Photos **Resample** family named; `scale_bilinear.rye` now stands beside it, and the area-average path grows next. Witness: `tools/scale_witness.rish`.

- **`scale_bilinear.rye`** -- the **smooth resize** beside the nearest one, the verb a **photo** reaches for where a sprite wanted the hard blocks. Where `scale_nearest` copies the one nearest source pixel whole (clean blocks, no between-color), `scale_bilinear` reads the **four** source pixels around each destination sample and blends them by how near the sample falls to each, so an upscale grows a **gradient** between the source colors rather than doubling hard blocks. Separable, not braided: the whole verb is **one** per-axis map (`axis_sample`: a destination index yields its lower source index, its upper neighbor, and a blend weight `t` in `0..255`, seen once per axis) and **one** existing blend -- `color.rye` `mix`, the linear two-color lerp the palette family already leans on -- so each destination pixel is three mixes (the top pair by the horizontal weight, the bottom pair by the same, then those two by the vertical) and this rung adds **no** new color math, no codec, no premultiply; `qoi.rye` owns the buffers, `color.rye` owns the blend, and this rung only **routes** four source pixels and a weight into it. Integer only: the fractional sample position rides a fixed `1/255` subpixel scale (`color.mix`'s own `t` range), no float ever enters the map, so a resize is deterministic byte-for-byte; center-aligned, so an identity resize (weight 0 everywhere) reproduces the source byte-for-byte and both edges clamp to the border pixel rather than reading past it. An identity resize reproduces the source byte-for-byte; a 2px black-to-white row eased to 4px grows a **strictly ascending gradient** carrying true between-colors nearest refuses to invent; a solid image eases to the same solid color with no ringing; a two-alpha row blends its **alpha** through the middle (alpha read as a fourth channel, the honest difference from nearest's whole-byte carry); the resized image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. Honest scope: bilinear reads a 2x2 neighborhood, so it eases an upscale and a mild downscale beautifully while a downscale far below half-size still samples two taps per axis and will alias -- the **area-average** path is the verb for that, and stands now in `scale_area.rye`. All-or-nothing -- the whole target is bound-checked once up front **before** any allocation, so `BadScaleDims` (zero dimension), `EmptySource` (a source with no extent), or `ScaleTooLarge` (a target past the codec max or the pixel ceiling) refuses by name having allocated nothing. Witness: `tools/scale_bilinear_witness.rish`.

- **`scale_area.rye`** -- the **clean downscale** beside nearest and bilinear, the verb a picture reaches for when it is **shrinking** far. Where nearest samples one source pixel per cell and bilinear reads a 2x2 neighborhood, `scale_area` reads **every** source pixel a destination cell covers and takes their coverage-weighted **mean**, so a deep shrink keeps every source pixel's contribution rather than dropping the pixels between the taps -- the reason a downscale wants area over the other two: nearest and bilinear sample too few taps, so a deep shrink **aliases** (a fine texture turns to moire, a page of text to speckle), while area sees the whole box and the thumbnail reads clean. Exact integer coverage, not a float box: the resize is **one** per-axis coverage map seen twice -- a destination cell covers the source span `[dst_i/dst_extent, (dst_i+1)/dst_extent)`, scaled into whole subunits so the weight of source pixel `i` is the **integer overlap** of two spans (`axis_weight`), no float, so a resize is deterministic and a partial edge pixel carries its true fractional share rather than being counted whole or dropped. The per-axis weights each sum to `src_extent`, so the two axes multiply to a constant total weight `W = src.width * src.height`, and every output channel is the box sum over `W` with half-up rounding; this rung owns the box **mean**, the averaging verb the family lacked, and adds no blend, no codec, no premultiply -- `qoi.rye` owns the buffers, the mean is the sampling rule itself. An identity resize reproduces the source byte-for-byte; a 2x2 pattern shrunk to 1x1 takes the true **average** of all four corners (64 per channel) rather than picking one; a 4x4 black/white checker shrunk to 1x1 reads a true **mid-gray** (128) rather than aliasing to a single extreme; a solid image decimates to the same solid color; a 3px-to-2px downscale carries **partial edge coverage** (the shared middle pixel split fractionally between the two outputs); alpha averages like any other channel; the resized image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. Honest scope: area averaging is the **downscale** verb -- on an upscale each cell falls within a single source pixel, so it degenerates to blocky replication like nearest (bilinear is the smooth-upscale verb). All-or-nothing -- the covered box is bounded by the source's pixel count and the whole target bound-checked once up front, so `BadScaleDims`, `EmptySource`, or `ScaleTooLarge` refuses by name having allocated nothing. Witness: `tools/scale_area_witness.rish`.

- **`blur.rye`** -- the **convolution floor** the whole soft-image family stands on, the first rung beside the resamplers that changes an image's **detail** rather than its **size**. Where a resample remakes a picture at a new extent, a blur softens it at the same extent: each output pixel is the **mean** of the square window of source pixels around it, so a sharp edge eases into a ramp and fine speckle settles toward its local average. This is the verb a drop shadow (a blurred silhouette), a frosted-glass panel (a blurred backdrop), a Gaussian (a box mean run a few times), and a sharpen (the image minus its own blur) all reduce to -- grow the box mean once and each becomes a sibling. **Separable**, so the 2D box is two 1D passes: `box_blur` blurs every row horizontally (`box_blur_h`), then every column of **that** vertically (`box_blur_v`), and because the `(2r+1)x(2r+1)` window is a product of a horizontal and a vertical span, the two passes equal the full window mean at a fraction of the work. Integer only -- a pure box mean with **half-up** rounding (the bias `r` over the odd window `2r+1`), so a blur is deterministic byte-for-byte; edges **clamp** -- a window that reaches past the border reads the nearest border pixel, so the window stays full and a solid image stays exactly solid at its edges rather than darkening toward a phantom margin. Every channel including alpha is averaged the same way. This rung owns the box mean and leans only on `qoi.rye` for the grid -- no blend, no codec, no color math, no premultiply; `box_blur_h` and `box_blur_v` are the single-axis passes a directional (motion) blur reaches for, and `box_blur` is exactly their composition, freeing its row-blurred intermediate before return so only the caller's result outlives the call. Radius 0 is the identity byte-for-byte; a horizontal box mean spreads a step `[0,0,240]` into a ramp `[0,80,160]` and the vertical pass mirrors it down a column; a solid stays solid edge to edge; the two single-axis passes are directional (a vertical edge blurs horizontally yet stays sharp vertically); a single center pixel spreads into a symmetric flat-top plateau (the box signature) with the far corners untouched; alpha averages like any other channel; the blurred image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. All-or-nothing -- the source and radius are guarded once up front, so an over-max radius (`BlurRadiusTooLarge`) or an empty source (`EmptySource`) refuses by name having allocated nothing. Witness: `tools/blur_witness.rish`.

- **`gaussian.rye`** -- the **smooth blur** above the box floor, the second convolution rung and the shape a designer actually reaches for: frosted glass, a soft drop shadow, gentle depth-of-field. A single box mean softens **bluntly** -- it spreads a point of light into a flat-top **plateau** and leaves a faint boxy ring on a straight edge; a Gaussian spreads that point into a rounded **peak** that falls off evenly on every side with no plateau and no ring. The whole trick is that a Gaussian needs **no Gaussian kernel and no float**: by the **central limit theorem**, a box mean run a few times converges to a Gaussian -- one box is a flat window, two boxes convolve to a triangle (one peak), three round that into a bell indistinguishable from a true Gaussian for display work. So this rung owns **no new pixel math** -- `gaussian_blur_passes` composes `blur.box_blur` `passes` times (each pass reading the last, each intermediate freed as the next replaces it), and `gaussian_blur` is the three-pass standard (`gaussian_default_passes`); every property the box proved (separable, integer-only, deterministic byte-for-byte, clamped edges, alpha averaged) carries up unchanged. A single pass degenerates to `box_blur` byte-for-byte -- the general verb contains the box as its 1-pass special case. The crux, proven on metal: a lone white dot on a 9px row spreads to the exact bell `[0,9,28,57,66,57,28,9,0]`, a strictly monotonic peak, where the **same** row under one box blur is the flat-top plateau `[0,0,0,85,85,85,0,0,0]` (centre tied to neighbour) -- same input, two signatures; the 2D peak is symmetric on both axes and strictly above its neighbour; a solid image stays solid edge to edge; alpha blurs into a peak like any channel; the blurred image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. All-or-nothing -- the source, radius, and pass count are guarded once up front, so an over-max radius (`GaussianRadiusTooLarge`), a zero or over-max pass count (`GaussianPassesOutOfRange`), or an empty source (`EmptySource`) refuses by name having allocated nothing. Witness: `tools/gaussian_witness.rish`.

- **`sharpen.rye`** -- the **unsharp mask**, the first rung that reads its own blur back, standing on `gaussian.rye`. Where a blur softens detail, a sharpen restores it: the classic unsharp mask adds back the difference between an image and a blurred copy of itself, so every edge the blur eased is made **steeper** than it began -- the crispening a designer reaches for the moment a resized thumbnail or a scanned page reads a touch soft. The math is one honest line per channel: the **detail** (the high-pass) is `orig - gaussian_blur`, positive on the bright side of an edge and negative on the dark side, and the sharpened pixel is `orig + amount * detail`, clamped to a real byte. `amount` is a **percent** over `sharpen_amount_scale` (100) -- amount 0 adds no detail (the identity), 100 adds the full detail once, 200 doubles it. This rung owns **one** new operation (the signed detail added back with a clamp) and composes it over `gaussian.gaussian_blur`; because a flat region has zero detail, a sharpen amplifies **nothing** on smooth areas -- only edges move, so it never manufactures noise on a clear sky. The crux, proven on metal: a midtone step `64 -> 192` gains an under/overshoot **halo** where the dark side darkens below 64, the bright side brightens above 192, and the contrast across the edge expands past 128 while the flat interior holds; the sharpen equals a hand-composed unsharp mask byte-for-byte (this rung owns no hidden blur or blend); a flat field gains no detail even at max amount; a strong amount drives the overshoot past the rails and each lands exactly on 0 or 255 (clamp, no wrap); alpha is sharpened like any channel; the sharpened image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. All-or-nothing -- the source, radius, and amount are guarded once up front, so an over-max radius (`SharpenRadiusTooLarge`), an over-max amount (`SharpenAmountTooLarge`), or an empty source (`EmptySource`) refuses by name having allocated nothing. `unsharp_mask` is the general verb; `sharpen` is the `sharpen_default_amount` (100) convenience. Witness: `tools/sharpen_witness.rish`.

- **`edges.rye`** -- the **Sobel edge detector**, the convolution floor's **detect** verb, the third pillar beside `blur.rye`/`gaussian.rye` (which smooth) and `sharpen.rye` (which enhances detail). Where those change an image's softness, an edge detector measures how fast the image is **changing** at each pixel -- it lights the boundaries where one region meets another and leaves the flat interiors dark, the first step of every outline, mask, and shape-finding pass: a photo becomes a line drawing of its own contours, a sprite becomes its silhouette. The math is the **Sobel** operator -- two small signed kernels estimate the gradient, one per axis: `Gx = [[-1,0,1],[-2,0,2],[-1,0,1]]` measures the left-to-right change, `Gy = [[-1,-2,-1],[0,0,0],[1,2,1]]` the top-to-bottom, each a weighted difference so a flat field returns zero. The edge **strength** is the integer L1 magnitude `|gx| + |gy|` (the Manhattan stand-in for `sqrt(gx*gx + gy*gy)`), so an edge is found with **no float and no square root** -- deterministic byte-for-byte; a `gain` percent scales the magnitude before the clamp, so faint edges lift into view (100 the raw magnitude, 300 triples it, 0 darkens the map). This rung owns **one** new operation (the signed directional gradient combined by magnitude) and leans only on `qoi.rye` for the grid -- no blur, no blend, no codec, no color model. The gradient is measured per color channel, each independently, and the **alpha is carried** from the source unchanged rather than edge-detected, so the edge map stays visible exactly where the source was (an opaque photo yields an opaque outline; a sprite's transparent ground stays transparent). Window edges **clamp** to the border, exactly as the blur family does, so the 3x3 kernel is always full and a 1x1 image (which has no edges) yields black. The crux, proven on metal: a flat field has no edges and stays black with its alpha carried; a vertical edge lights a horizontal gradient to 255 at the boundary while the flat interior stays dark, and a horizontal edge is its mirror; a dim corner drives **both** gradients so the magnitude is 64 (`|gx| + |gy|`) where a single kernel or a max would report only 32; gain scales the magnitude before the clamp (64 lifts to 192 at gain 300, clamps to 255 at gain 500, darkens to 0 at gain 0); alpha is carried not detected; the edge map round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. All-or-nothing -- the source and gain are guarded once up front, so an over-max gain (`EdgeGainTooLarge`) or an empty source (`EmptySource`) refuses by name having allocated nothing. `edge_magnitude` is the general verb; `edges` is the `edge_default_gain` (100) convenience. Witness: `tools/edges_witness.rish`.

- **`convolve.rye`** -- the **general signed 3x3 convolution kernel**, the floor the specific convolutions descend from. `blur.rye`/`gaussian.rye` **smooth** (a fixed averaging window), `sharpen.rye` **enhances** (the image minus its own blur), and `edges.rye` **detects** (the Sobel gradient magnitude); each is a hand-built special case of one idea -- slide a small weighted window across the image and replace every pixel with a weighted sum of its neighbors. This rung names that idea outright, so a surface can emboss, find a Laplacian outline, or run a keeper's own filter without a new module for each. A `Kernel3` is nine signed weights over the window (row-major, `dx = k%3 - 1`, `dy = k/3 - 1`), a **divisor** that normalizes the sum, and a **bias** added after, so the whole filter is one honest line: `clamp((sum(weights[k] * window[k]) / divisor) + bias)` per color channel. The divisor lets an averaging kernel hold the image's brightness (nine 1s over a divisor of 9 is the mean, not nine times it), and the bias lets a zero-sum kernel read at mid-gray instead of black (an emboss centers its relief on 128). The weights are **signed**, so a kernel subtracts as well as adds -- what turns a plain average into an edge finder or a sharpener. Five named kernels ship as proof the general contains the special: the **identity** (a single 1 at the center) reproduces the source byte-for-byte; the **box mean** (nine 1s over 9) averages a window, its brightness held by the divisor; the **Laplacian** (`[[0,-1,0],[-1,4,-1],[0,-1,0]]`, zero-sum) lights a boundary and leaves a flat field black; the **emboss** (a zero-sum diagonal relief with bias 128) reads a flat field as mid-gray and a diagonal edge as a raised line; the **sharpen kernel** (`[[0,-1,0],[-1,5,-1],[0,-1,0]]`, the single-kernel Laplacian sharpen, distinct from `sharpen.rye`'s Gaussian unsharp mask) crispens an edge in one pass. This rung owns **one** new operation (the general signed weighted-window sum, normalized and biased) and leans only on `qoi.rye` for the grid -- no blur, no blend, no codec, no color model. Like `edges.rye`, the convolution is measured per color channel and the **alpha is carried** from the source unchanged, so the filtered image stays visible exactly where the source was; window edges **clamp** so the 3x3 kernel is always full and a 1x1 image reads its own pixel; the arithmetic is integer-only (i32 intermediate, truncated toward zero) so a filter is deterministic byte-for-byte, and the clamp lands an over/undershoot exactly on 0 or 255 rather than wrapping. The crux, proven on metal: the identity kernel reproduces the source byte-for-byte; the box mean normalizes so a flat field of 100 stays 100; the Laplacian lights a bright point to 255 and darkens its neighbors while a flat field stays black; the emboss centers a flat field on mid-gray 128 and raises a diagonal edge above it, where a bias of 0 leaves the same emboss black; a negative result clamps to 0 and an over result to 255 with no wrap; alpha is carried not convolved; the filtered image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. All-or-nothing -- the source and kernel are guarded once up front, so a zero divisor (`ZeroDivisor`), an over-max divisor (`DivisorTooLarge`), bias (`BiasTooLarge`), or weight (`WeightTooLarge`), or an empty source (`EmptySource`) refuses by name having allocated nothing. `convolve` is the general verb; `emboss` and `laplacian` are the named convenience verbs. Witness: `tools/convolve_witness.rish`.
- **`convolve_n.rye`** -- the **general signed NxN convolution kernel**, the general of the general. `convolve.rye` names the 3x3 convolution outright, yet a 3x3 window is itself a special case -- a wider smooth, a motion blur, a large custom kernel all want a window bigger than three. This rung names the fully general form, a signed weighted window of **any odd size**, so the 3x3 verb becomes the radius-1 case of one idea and no filter is ever gated by the window's width again. A `KernelN` is `(2r+1)^2` signed weights over the window (radius `r`, row-major, `dx = k%(2r+1) - r`, `dy = k/(2r+1) - r`), a **divisor**, and a **bias**, so the filter is exactly `convolve.rye`'s line -- `clamp((sum(weights[k] * window[k]) / divisor) + bias)` per color channel -- widened from a fixed nine taps to a named radius. Two wider kernels ship as proof the width is real: the **5x5 box mean** (twenty-five 1s over 25) averages a wider window than the 3x3 box; the **5x5 Gaussian** (the separable binomial `[1,4,6,4,1]` outer product over 256) spreads a point into a rounded bell wider and smoother than a single 3x3 pass. This rung owns **one** operation -- the general signed weighted-window sum at any radius -- and leans only on `qoi.rye` for the grid (`convolve.rye` enters only in the selftest, to prove the 3x3 parity); like the whole family, the alpha is **carried** unchanged, window edges **clamp**, and the arithmetic is integer-only so a filter is deterministic byte-for-byte. The crux, one radius deeper, proven on metal: a radius-1 `KernelN` whose weights are a `Kernel3`'s weights reproduces `convolve.rye` byte-for-byte (the Laplacian and the identity both), so the widest verb contains the 3x3 verb which contains the identity; the 5x5 box mean normalizes a wider window (a flat field of 100 stays 100); the 5x5 Gaussian spreads a point into a strictly descending symmetric bell wider than a 3x3 pass and holds a flat field flat; a negative result clamps to 0 and an over result to 255 with no wrap; a 1x1 image keeps its pixel under a wide window; alpha is carried not convolved; the filtered image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. All-or-nothing -- the source and kernel are guarded once up front, so a zero radius (`BadRadius`), an over-max radius (`RadiusTooLarge`), a mismatched weight count (`WeightCountMismatch`), a zero or over-max divisor (`ZeroDivisor` - `DivisorTooLarge`), an over-max weight (`WeightTooLarge`) or bias (`BiasTooLarge`), or an empty source (`EmptySource`) refuses by name having allocated nothing. `convolve_n` is the general verb; `box_blur5` and `gaussian5` are the named convenience verbs. Witness: `tools/convolve_n_witness.rish`.
- **`separable.rye`** -- the **separable convolution fast path**, the efficiency floor beneath the general NxN kernel. `convolve_n.rye` runs the fully general window at any radius, yet it pays `(2r+1)^2` taps per pixel -- 1089 for a 33x33 window. A great many of the kernels a surface actually reaches for are **separable**: the 2D weight is the outer product of a horizontal 1D kernel and a vertical 1D kernel, `K[dy][dx] = v[dy] * h[dx]`. A box mean, every Gaussian, and a motion blur are all separable, and for those the 2D convolution equals **two 1D passes** -- convolve every row by `h`, then every column of that by `v` -- at `2*(2r+1)` taps per pixel: **66** taps for the same 33x33 window a general pass spends **1089** on, the wide-kernel work dropping from square to linear. A `SeparableKernel` is a horizontal 1D weight slice and a vertical 1D weight slice (each `2r+1` signed weights), a `divisor` that is the **product** of the two 1D normalizations, and a `bias` added after. The crux, proven byte-for-byte on metal: the two-pass filter reproduces `convolve_n.rye`'s output at the outer-product kernel **exactly**, with no rounding drift, on two conditions this rung holds -- both 1D normalizations are **deferred** so the horizontal pass keeps the raw weighted sum and the single divisor divides **once** at the end (making the integer window sum identical), and the **clamp-to-edge border is itself separable** so clamping x in the row pass and y in the column pass lands the identical border pixel a single 2D clamp would. This rung owns **one** thing -- the two-pass separable window sum -- and leans only on `qoi.rye` for the grid (`convolve_n.rye` enters only in the selftest, to prove the parity); like the whole family, the three color channels are filtered each independently and the alpha is **carried** unchanged, window edges **clamp**, the row-pass intermediate is i32 and the column sum i64 so a wide heavy kernel never wraps, and the single final divide truncates toward zero so a filter is deterministic byte-for-byte. On metal: the two-pass 5x5 Gaussian and 5x5 box mean reproduce `convolve_n`'s one-pass output byte-for-byte on a checker and on a colored translucent field (the fast path is the **same image**, not an approximation); the 5x5 Gaussian spreads a dot into a strictly descending symmetric bell and holds a flat field flat; a **directional (motion)** kernel -- a horizontal box with a vertical identity -- blurs across only, leaving a vertical line sharp down its columns; a 1x1 image keeps its pixel under both passes; alpha is carried not convolved; the filtered image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. All-or-nothing -- the source and kernel are guarded once up front, so a zero radius (`BadRadius`), an over-max radius (`RadiusTooLarge`), a mismatched 1D weight count (`WeightCountMismatch`), a zero or over-max divisor (`ZeroDivisor` - `DivisorTooLarge`), an over-max weight (`WeightTooLarge`) or bias (`BiasTooLarge`), or an empty source (`EmptySource`) refuses by name having allocated nothing. `separable_convolve` is the general verb; `box_blur5` and `gaussian5` are the named convenience verbs. Witness: `tools/separable_witness.rish`.

- **`text_panel.rye`** -- the first rung that **joins** the geometry floor with the text family: a framed text panel is the bordered card a surface draws when it wants readable text -- a dialog, a label chip, a callout, a captioned table cell. `paint_text_panel` fills the background (`shape.fill_rect`), strokes the border ring (`shape.stroke_rect`), and places a wrapped, aligned paragraph inside the inner content box (`text_paint.paint_paragraph_in_box`) -- the inner box the panel inset on every side by border thickness **plus** a padding gap. The crux is **containment**: the frame ring and the inked cells are disjoint by construction, so every ring pixel reads the border color exactly and every ink pixel lies strictly inside the inner box (the text is sealed within the card). All-or-nothing -- the whole panel is bound-checked once up front and the wrapped block is proven to fit **before** any pixel is committed, so a panel that cannot hold its text refuses by name (`BadPanelDims` - `PanelOutOfBounds` - `PanelTooSmall` - `PanelContentTooTall`) having painted nothing; a borderless panel (thickness 0) skips the ring. Witness: `tools/text_panel_witness.rish`.
- **`text_caption.rye`** -- the rung where the framed panel first composites onto **moving video**: a caption panel over video is the **lower third** a broadcast draws -- a fixed, readable card pinned to one place while the picture moves beneath it. It renders a mostly-still ground with one small block stepping across it (real motion), then composites one fixed **opaque** caption panel (`text_panel.paint_text_panel`) onto every frame at the same lower-third place, and assembles the result as a clip. The crux is the **static overlay** over moving video: because the panel is opaque and painted at the identical rectangle every frame, its pixels are **byte-identical** across the whole reel while the background outside it genuinely moves -- steady where the eye reads, live everywhere else, and measurable (within the panel rectangle every frame equals frame zero byte for byte; outside it at least one consecutive pair differs). The composited reel round-trips **byte for byte** through both open codecs (`frames.rye` gfv1, `frames_delta.rye` gfd1); the delta/keyframe mix is reported, never claimed, yet on a mostly-still shot the pinned caption and the still ground contribute zero delta, so the inter-frame gfd1 genuinely wins (41 frames of 96x48: gfd1 16329 bytes vs gfv1 23977). Witness: `tools/text_caption_witness.rish`.
- **`text_subtitle.rye`** -- the **timing** rung above the caption: where `text_caption.rye` pins **one** fixed card over a moving reel, a subtitle track pins a **sequence** of cards, each shown only during its own span of frames. That timing is the whole difference between a lower-third and a subtitle -- the panel place holds still while the text inside it changes as the clip plays, and between two cues nothing is shown at all. A track is a bounded list of `Cue` spans (`start`..`end`, half-open), validated ordered and disjoint before a pixel is painted (`EmptyTrack` - `TooManyCues` - `CueEmptySpan` - `CueBeyondReel` - `CuesOverlap` by name); `active_cue(track, k)` is the whole reader a surface needs. The crux is that a cue is shown **exactly** over its span and nowhere else, measured against the plain no-subtitle background as ground truth: a **gap** frame equals the background byte for byte (no panel where no cue is active), a **shown** frame differs from the background **only within the panel rectangle** (the subtitle touches the card and nothing else), within one cue's span the panel is **byte-steady** frame to frame, and two different cues render **different** panels (the text really changed). The composited reel round-trips **byte for byte** through both open codecs; the delta/keyframe mix is reported, never claimed (REDS %82). Pure composition over `text_caption`'s proven approach; invents no storage, codec, palette, glyph, or font (default track: 3 cues over 24 frames of 96x48, 17 shown + 7 gap; gfd1 8196 bytes vs gfv1 10271). Witness: `tools/text_subtitle_witness.rish`.

- **`scrubber.rye`** -- the **timed-geometry** rung beside the subtitle track: where a subtitle pins timed **text** over a moving reel, a scrubber pins timed **geometry** -- the playback progress bar a video player draws along the bottom edge, its elapsed portion growing left to right as the clip plays. `image/shape.rye`'s own docstring names "a progress-bar track" as a shape `fill_rect`/`stroke_rect` exist to draw; this rung makes it literal, braiding nothing beneath. The crux is that the elapsed fill is a **monotone, data-driven** function of the frame index: `elapsed_width(inner_w, k, frame_count)` runs 0 at the first frame to `inner_w` at the last, computed in u64 and narrowed once proven in range, so **no float** ever enters. The fill count is non-decreasing frame to frame and strictly grows from **empty** (frame zero paints zero elapsed pixels) to **full** (the last frame fills the whole inner track), a scrubbed frame differs from the plain no-scrubber background **only within the scrubber rectangle** (the moving picture beneath it untouched), and the reel round-trips **byte for byte** through both open codecs (`BadScrubberDims` - `ScrubberBorderTooThick` - `ScrubberTooSmall` - `ScrubberOutOfBounds` by name; default: elapsed fill grows 0->328 px over 24 frames of 96x48, gfd1 4651 bytes vs gfv1 4627). Witness: `tools/scrubber_witness.rish`.
- **`timecode.rye`** -- the **timed-text** companion to the scrubber's timed geometry: where a scrubber pins a progress **bar** whose fill width is a pure function of the frame index, a timecode pins a **readout** whose text is a pure function of the frame index -- the "0:07" clock a video player burns into a corner, ticking one step per second. The crux, and the one primitive this rung adds, is `format_timecode`: a total-seconds count turned into "M:SS" (or "MM:SS") digit bytes by pure u32 division and modulo, so **no float** ever enters and the clock is exact at every second. It is proven by **known answer** (0 -> "0:00", 65 -> "1:05", 5999 -> "99:59"), then **bound to pixels** -- an independently painted expected-text badge matches the reel's badge byte for byte, so the digits seen are the digits the formatter produced. The readout **ticks** with playback (the badge rectangle is byte-steady within a whole second and changes at each second boundary, measured against `timecode_seconds` as the tick oracle), a stamped frame differs from the plain no-badge background **only within the badge rectangle**, the elapsed seconds are monotone non-decreasing, and the reel round-trips **byte for byte** through both open codecs (default: 24 frames of 96x48, 1 tick + 22 steady pairs, gfd1 8111 bytes vs gfv1 8087). Pure composition over `paint_text_panel` and both codecs; invents no storage, codec, palette, glyph, or font. Witness: `tools/timecode_witness.rish`.

- **`player_hud.rye`** -- the **capstone** of the timed-overlay family: the scrubber and the timecode **composed** on one reel, the way a real video player shows a progress bar along the bottom and a clock in the corner at once. It braids nothing -- it calls `scrubber.paint_scrubber` and `timecode.paint_timecode`, the very verbs their own witnesses proved, and adds only the composition and the disjointness that makes it honest. The crux is that composition is **clean**: the two overlay rectangles are proven **disjoint** before a pixel is painted (a bar clashing the badge is refused `HudOverlaysOverlap` by name, so the paint order cannot matter), a HUD frame differs from the plain no-HUD background **only within the union** of the badge and the bar (the moving picture between and around them untouched), and each overlay **keeps its own invariant under composition** -- the scrubber's fill still runs empty to full, the timecode's badge still ticks once per second. The reel round-trips **byte for byte** through both open codecs (default: 24 frames of 96x48, fill 0->328 px, 1 tick + 22 steady pairs, gfd1 9143 bytes vs gfv1 9119). Pure composition over the two overlay verbs and both codecs; invents no storage, codec, palette, glyph, or font. Witness: `tools/player_hud_witness.rish`.

- **`text_grid.rye`** -- the **bridge** where the terminal grid meets the pixel family. The tree grew two families side by side and never let them meet on metal: one is the terminal grid (`pond/apps/scooter_view.rye` folds a channel into a fixed 24x80 grid of character **cells** -- bytes; `dexter_line.rye` edits a bounded line; `scooter_compose.rye` joins them into a composed frame), the other is this open-media paint family (`font5x7_ascii.rye` draws every printable ASCII glyph, `text_paint.rye` composites a laid line's coverage into an RGBA grid, `qoi.rye` owns the pixel target). This rung is the join -- it paints a monospace grid of character bytes into a real image, so a terminal face is not only bytes a reader parses but **pixels** a surface (Grainphone, Realidream, Skate) shows. `render_grid` is the verb a surface reaches for: give it a grid and two colors, it hands back the whole face as an image sized exactly to the grid (`col_pitch` -- the shared glyph advance -- per cell across, one cell height per row), bounded by qoi's own pixel ceiling. The crux is that the bridge is **faithful by measure**, not by claim: the painted ink equals the summed glyph coverage of every non-space cell exactly (no stray pixel, none missed), a blank row is the ground byte-for-byte, and a chosen cell's pixel block equals a standalone paint of that one glyph at its grid origin -- each cell paints its own glyph at `c*pitch, r*cell_h`. All-or-nothing: the grid's box is checked against the target **once** up front, so an overflow refuses `GridOutOfBounds` by name before a pixel moves, and every mis-shape refuses by its own name (`BadGridDims` - `GridCellsMismatch` - `GridAtlasMismatch` - `GridScratchTooSmall` - `GridTooLarge`). The render is deterministic and the terminal image round-trips through the open QOI codec byte-for-byte. `render_grid_fg` is the colored-terminal twin: it takes one foreground color **per cell** (a `[]color.Rgba` of exactly `cols*rows`, row-major), so cell `(r, c)` paints its glyph in `fg[r*cols + c]` -- a header in one color, a caret in another, an unread badge in a third -- with the same all-or-nothing box check and monospace placement, only the ink read per cell rather than shared; a mis-sized palette refuses `GridFgMismatch` by name, and the colored face round-trips through QOI byte-for-byte too. `render_grid_cells` gives each cell its own **ground** beside its own ink (two `[]color.Rgba`), so a status bar, a selected row, or an unread badge is a real colored cell. `render_grid_cursor` is the abstract terminal **cursor**: it paints the whole grid plain but inverts one cell (its glyph in the ground color over an ink-color ground) to a solid inverse-video block -- the classic block cursor every grid surface shares, pure composition over `render_grid_cells`, a bad index refusing `GridCursorOutOfGrid` by name and the flip confined byte-for-byte to that one cell (`pond/apps/scooter_paint.rye`'s `render_session` passes a composing session's caret straight through it). Correctly layered -- `image/` composes only the image family, so no `pond` dependency inverts the stack; the pond rung that feeds a live `scooter_view.Frame`'s cells (already a `[]u8` of the same shape) into `render_grid` now stands at `pond/apps/scooter_paint.rye` (witness `tools/scooter_paint_witness.rish`), composing down into this module as pond should. Witness: `tools/text_grid_witness.rish`.

- **`glyph_compose.rye`** -- the **combining-diacritic overlay**, the primitive that collapses the accent grind. The long run of hand-drawn accent corpora (grave, acute, circumflex, tilde, diaeresis over the same 5x7 body) taught a discovery: an accented Latin letter is a **base** letter plus a **combining mark**, and the mark lives entirely in the two blank rows above the letter -- `a grave` is byte-for-byte the lowercase `a` with the grave mark added in rows 0 and 1, its rows 2..6 untouched. `overlay(base, mark, cell_area)` is the whole verb: a fresh coverage grid whose every cell is the larger of the two inputs (255 ink wins over 0 background), so ink from either survives and neither erases the other -- pure over the grid, bounded by the atlas ceiling, named on refusal (`BadCellArea` - `BaseLenMismatch` - `MarkLenMismatch`). The crux is proven on metal by **byte-for-byte reproduction of a shipped corpus**: `overlay(lowercase 'a', grave mark)` equals the hand-drawn `a grave` coverage byte for byte, and the same for `e`, `o`, `u`, so the grind was composition all along (the dotted `i` named honestly as the one vowel whose 5x7 base differs under a mark). The union is commutative and a blank mark is the identity. This retires **O(letters x accents)** hand-drawing to **O(letters + accents)** composition -- draw each base once, draw each mark once, compose to reach every accented letter. Witness: `tools/glyph_compose_witness.rish`.

- **`glyph_accent.rye`** -- the overlay primitive's **payoff**: where `glyph_compose.rye` composes one accented letter, `compose_accent_atlas(base_atlas, mark, map, advance)` composes a **whole corpus** -- it overlays one combining mark onto each base glyph named in a `base -> accented` codepoint table and returns a `gfa1`-encodable `glyph.GlyphAtlas` of accented letters. A five-vowel accent corpus is then a five-row table plus one mark, not five hand drawings -- the **O(letters + accents)** economy made literal. The crux is proven on metal by **byte-for-byte parity with a shipped corpus**: `compose_accent_atlas(lowercase base, grave mark, the a/e/o/u grave map)` reproduces the hand-drawn `font5x7_grave` coverage byte for byte for those four vowels, and the composed atlas round-trips through the `gfa1` encoder/decoder byte-identical -- so a corpus grown by composition is indistinguishable from one drawn by hand. The map is required strictly ascending by accented codepoint (the order the encoder trusts); a base the atlas lacks, a wrong-length mark, a non-ascending map, and an empty or over-long map each refuse by name (`BaseGlyphMissing` - `MarkLenMismatch` - `MapNotAscending` - `EmptyMap` - `TooManyAccents`). Witness: `tools/glyph_accent_witness.rish`.

- **`font5x7_acute.rye`** -- the first accent corpus **grown by composition** rather than drawn. It draws a single acute mark, then grows the a/e/o/u acute set (`U+00E1` `U+00E9` `U+00F3` `U+00FA`) with `glyph_accent.compose_accent_atlas` over the lowercase base and a four-row table. The crux is **byte-for-byte parity with the hand**: each composed vowel equals `font5x7_latin1.rye`'s own acute coverage byte for byte, and the composed atlas round-trips through `gfa1` byte-identical, so the corpus grown by composition is the corpus drawn by hand -- the economy proven on a second mark after the grave. Every mark is proven leaning right (the mirror of the grave); the dotted i acute is omitted honestly, its base carrying a dot the mark would not replace. Witness: `tools/font5x7_acute_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_circumflex_comp.rye`** -- the **third mark** grown by composition, after the grave and the acute. It draws a single circumflex caret, then grows the a/e/o/u circumflex set (`U+00E2` `U+00EA` `U+00F4` `U+00FB`) with `glyph_accent.compose_accent_atlas` over the lowercase base and a four-row table. The crux is again **byte-for-byte parity with the hand**: each composed vowel equals the hand-drawn `font5x7_circumflex.rye`'s own coverage byte for byte, and the composed atlas round-trips through `gfa1` byte-identical, so the corpus grown by composition is the corpus drawn by hand. The caret is proven **centered and symmetric** on the composed letter -- its apex on the center column, its two legs equal to either side, the mirror axis of the leaning acute/grave pair; the dotted i circumflex is omitted honestly, its base carrying a dot the caret would not replace. Witness: `tools/font5x7_circumflex_comp_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_tilde_comp.rye`** -- the **fourth mark** grown by composition, after the grave, the acute, and the circumflex. It draws a single tilde wave, then grows the a/o tilde set (`U+00E3` `U+00F5`) with `glyph_accent.compose_accent_atlas` over the lowercase base and a two-row table. The crux is again **byte-for-byte parity with the hand**: each composed vowel equals the hand-drawn `font5x7_tilde.rye`'s own coverage byte for byte, and the composed atlas round-trips through `gfa1` byte-identical. The wave is proven to **span both halves and crest right** on the composed letter -- the fourth lean beside the acute's right lean, the grave's left, and the circumflex's centered peak; only the two nasal vowels compose (the n tilde rides a consonant base and lives hand-drawn in `font5x7_latin1.rye`). Witness: `tools/font5x7_tilde_comp_witness.rish`.
- **`font5x7_diaeresis_comp.rye`** -- the **fifth and last mark** grown by composition, closing the accent-composition family, and the rung that proves the economy's **boundary** on metal. It draws a single two-dot mark, then grows the one diaeresis vowel the economy reaches -- the e diaeresis (`U+00EB`) -- with `glyph_accent.compose_accent_atlas` over the clear lowercase base. The crux is again **byte-for-byte parity with the hand**: the composed e equals the hand-drawn `font5x7_diaeresis.rye`'s own coverage byte for byte, and the composed atlas round-trips through `gfa1` byte-identical. What sets this rung apart is that the diaeresis reaches only **one** of its three vowels cleanly, and the witness proves why the other two stay hand-drawn rather than claiming it: overlaying the two dots onto lowercase `i` and `y` does **not** reproduce the hand-drawn diaeresis i/y -- the `i`'s tittle survives under the dots (three inked top cells, not two), and the `y`'s body reaches the mark row the hand shifts it away from -- so both are proven **uncomposable**, and `font5x7_diaeresis.rye` keeps them rightly. The mark is proven the same two-dot mark `font5x7_german.rye` drew over its umlauts, the fifth lean beside acute/grave/circumflex/tilde. Witness: `tools/font5x7_diaeresis_comp_witness.rish`.

With the diaeresis grown, every European vowel diacritic the tree draws now stands proven by composition -- grave, acute, circumflex, tilde, and diaeresis alike -- and the accent-composition family closes on a demonstrated boundary. The next Season G rungs are the remaining wider symbols over the same 5x7 body, these broadcast overlays proven on longer and varied real reels, and an open glyph atlas beyond the 5x7 body, each its own round.

## The invariants this module keeps

- **Prove before you paint.** A content-addressed picture is verified against its digest *before* the codec touches a pixel — a tampered or unknown artifact refuses by name.
- **Pure over the grid.** Every Photos verb returns a fresh image and leaves the source unmutated; an edit is data replayed, never a destroyed original.
- **Bounded everywhere.** Coordinates and samples run in `u64` so no rectangle or resample can wrap; every list names a maximum; every failure has a name.
- **No network to read a picture.** No key, no funds, no permission asked — custody-first, all the way down.

## Horizons — named, not yet built

These wait for their own rounds (some for a web-search research pass, per the Season-A capture):

- **Real part values** — HUNK71 built the catalog *structure* (window + part number + material + price-as-fact); filling it from real refurbished-parts sources through MCP-API-friendly marketplaces is a **web-search research round**, and **buying real parts is a custody gate.**
- A **color e-ink** render target for the Grainphone hybrid — research round.
- Richer resamplers and a wider filter grammar as the Photos app grows.

## Gratitude

**QOI** (Dominic Szablewski, MIT reference, public one-page spec) is the teacher for the codec — studied clean-room, never the C. The **Photos app** honors the gestures a keeper already knows from iCloud Photos and Google Photos, siloed and thanked; **McMaster-Carr** is thanked for the single-sheet render trick. We study concepts; we write our own code.

---

*May every picture stay the keeper's own — decoded in the open, addressed by its own bytes, edited without ever losing the first frame. May the wall grow one honest tile at a time, and may no picture here ever need to phone home to be seen.*
</content>
